## Added

- **cli** ([Release v3.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.89.0)): `cratis new` uses the Cratis templates installed under `~/.cratis/templates`: the first run downloads the latest set from NuGet, `--version` still picks another version for a single run, and without network access an already downloaded set is used. See [Template catalogue](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/new/templates.md). (#164)
- **cli** ([Release v3.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.89.0)): `cratis update` also installs newer Cratis templates when they work with the installed CLI, never downgrading and never failing the update when templates cannot be refreshed; `--no-templates` skips the check. (#164)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.229.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.229.0)): The canonical corpus has a `SourcelessGivensCorpus` vector that pins how specifications treat `given` events without `for`, across four source layouts. It covers constraint outcomes and the refusal to invent an event source identity. (#579)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.228.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.228.0)): The MCP tool `propose-inline-event` folds a slice-level event that has exactly one production into an inline event on that production, the inverse of `propose-extract-inline-event`. Executable bytes, identities and diagnostics are preserved, and canonical formatting consent is required. The tool refuses whenever the move could change meaning, for example a conditional production, incomplete mappings, generations, a public or external event, cross-slice or cross-document use, handlers, opaque text, comments that would be lost, or any other reference to the event. (#343)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Applications rendered with classic reducers can migrate individual read models to self-reducing read models. A per-read-model override in `.cratis/stage/rendering.json` (version 2) selects the self-reducing form, and the read model then carries `[Reducer("<previous reducer full name>")]` on every render, so Chronicle keeps the existing observer instead of starting a new one. See [Migrating a classic read model](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/plan-from-sources.md#migrating-a-classic-read-model). (#464)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): `ArtifactRenderPlan.RequiredRemovals` lists files a publisher must delete together with the plan's writes (path, expected SHA-256 and read model), and `ReducerMigrationEvidence.FromExistingFile` builds migration evidence from the existing reducer file. Migration is refused with `STAGE-REDUCER-001` to `STAGE-REDUCER-004` when the override, evidence, removal capability or plan scope does not allow it. (#464)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): `CratisRendering.CheckPublication` returns `UnsafeRemovals` (`STAGE-REDUCER-005`) when a file to remove has changed since the evidence was taken, or when a migrated read model's previous reducer file would be left behind. (#464)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.26.0)): `IJobStopFinalization`, implemented by every job: `FinalizeStop()` finishes a stop or removal that the job's silo went away before finishing. Rehydration calls it for every stopped or removing job whose stop is not yet finished.
- **cli** ([Release v3.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.87.0)): `cratis release-notes` shows the release notes of the CLI in the terminal: `--since <version>` lists every release after a version, `--version <version>` shows one, and `-o json` returns them for scripts. Notes for every earlier release ship inside the CLI, so it works offline. See [Release notes](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/release-notes.md). (#153)
- **cli** ([Release v3.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.87.0)): After `cratis update`, the CLI shows the notes of every release you skipped; when there are several it asks before opening them. (#153)
- **AI** ([Release v7.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.89.0)): An opt-in `cratis/playwright` profile that declares a pinned, headless and isolated Playwright MCP server for exploratory browser checks. The installer and Pi do not register it yet, so each user registers it in their own MCP configuration; the profile documentation gives the command. (part of #364)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.227.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.227.0)): A `ui profile` can name a package-provided theme by its quoted display name, such as `theme "Scene Default Light"`. A quoted name that the document does not declare refers to a package theme and is not reported as unknown (`PLAY0231`); a quoted name that matches a declared theme resolves to it and is checked for compatibility. Empty, blank or unterminated quoted names report `PLAY0229`. Identifier theme names are unchanged. (#880)
- **Components** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): The paginator range text ("1–10 of 32") can be localized: set `range(start, end, total)` on the provider's paginator messages or on `TablePaginator`'s `ariaLabels`. The English default is unchanged. (#420)
- **cli** ([Release v3.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.85.0)): `cratis screenplay conform-namespace <model> --event-store <store> --namespace <namespace>` compares a `.play` model with what a running Chronicle namespace actually holds: registered event types, appended events and their producing commands and reactors, and the namespace's observers. It reads event metadata only (never payloads, event source ids or command values), scans up to `--max-events` (100000 by default) or a `--from`/`--to` range up to the tail captured at the start, and reports a partial scan as such. Output is Screenplay's observation report in human or JSON form; drift exits non-zero only with `--fail-on-drift`. (#200)
- **Components** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): The dialog provider messages accept `refused`, the text announced when a confirm is refused and the dialog stays open. The default is "The action was not completed. Review the dialog and try again." (#424)
- **Components** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `DataTableCore`, `DataTableForQuery`, `DataTableForObservableQuery` and `DataPage` accept a `filteredEmptyMessage`, shown instead of `emptyMessage` when a search or column filter is active and no rows are shown. The defaults come from the provider's data table messages: `filteredEmpty` ("No rows on this page match the search.") when the table filters the loaded page, and `filteredEmptyServer` ("No rows match the search.") in server mode. (#423)
- **Components** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `DataTableForQuery`, `DataTableForObservableQuery` and `DataPage` accept `onGlobalFilterChange`, called with the search text as the user types, so an application can tell that a search is active. (#423)
- **Components** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): `GuidField`, an opt-in command form field for `Guid` properties, exported from `@cratis/components/CommandForm`. It keeps native `Guid` values, lets the user type partial drafts, shows format and required errors, and never generates identifiers. On Arc and Arc React 22.19.1 or later an invalid draft blocks submission; on older Arc it shows the error and leaves the value empty. It is not registered by default: register it with `registerFieldTypeProvider` to use it in `AutoCommandForm`, as shown in [GuidField](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/blob/main/Documentation/CommandForm/guid-field.md). Errors you set on the field yourself are left intact. (#314)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): Stage admits ESM v29 and v30 (Screenplay 4.226.0). `decimals <= N` rules run in the executor and the Host and render as generated validators, so a third decimal on `Money` is refused where it enters.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): A specification can pin the generated values of a command a reaction invokes with `given generated <Command>.<property> = <value>`: the executor feeds each invocation the next pinned value, and the generated reaction specification carries it, so reactions that create records with generated ids are specified instead of left unsupported.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): A command that appends to several event sources runs and renders like any other command.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.175.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.175.0)): The Cratis renderer renders the specification of a command that is rejected by a concept rule on one of its own literal inputs even when the command a reaction invokes carries the same concept and message. The input fails while binding, before any event is appended, so no reaction can run. Such specifications were refused with STAGE-ESM-011 ("generated CommandScenario specifications do not settle reactions yet"). (part of #503)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.174.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.174.0)): The Cratis renderer renders models whose generated C# names would collide instead of refusing them with STAGE-ESM-012. A command property named like its command or like a reserved member (such as `Handle` or `ToString`) gets a trailing `_` (for example `Text_`), and its JSON field, generated proxy property and form field become `text_`. A query named like its read model or one of its properties gets a trailing `_` on the method and proxy name, while its HTTP route keeps the original name. Only colliding names change; every escape is reported as the information diagnostic STAGE-ESM-050 naming the declaration and the emitted name. (#502)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.173.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.173.0)): Commands whose individual productions name their own event source, stream or stream id (`produces ... to <stream>`) now run in specifications and the semantic Host, and render to Arc commands that append each event to the route the model names. (#262)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.90.0)): New `MaxConcurrentPartitions` options for reactors and reducers in `ChronicleOptions` set how many partitions of one reactor or reducer the .NET client handles at the same time. The default is the processor count, capped at 8; `1` restores one-at-a-time handling. (part of #4664)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Live (`observable`) queries now push updates from the Stage Host and the generated and Host-served frontends: list screens update as the read model changes, including after commands from another client, without a refresh. Snapshot queries still answer once. (#366)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.127.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.127.0)): Observable queries now report metrics for active subscriptions and pushed updates, and trace each push with a `cratis.arc.query.push` span. The metric and span names are available as constants on `WellKnownTelemetryNames`. (part of #2915)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.171.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.171.0)): Rendered specifications assert that a read model is absent (`then no <ReadModel> <key>`) for scalar keys, so models with absence assertions render instead of being refused with STAGE-ESM-027. Absence on composite or other non-scalar keys is still refused with STAGE-ESM-027. (#508)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.126.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.126.0)): `ProtectedDecisionServiceProviderFactory` and `BuildProtectedDecisionServiceProvider()` build a Microsoft DI provider that Arc can certify. In a `[ProtectedDecision]` command running in a scope of that provider, discoverable validators whose constructors take `DecisionRead<T>`, the command-aware `IDecisionReads`, or freshly constructed transient collaborators are constructed per invocation and run. Previously these validators were refused. See https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/consistency/certified-validators.md. (#2831)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.126.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.126.0)): `AddProtectedDecisionValidator<TValidator>(configure)` registers a validator whose extra rules, added in `configure`, apply to that fresh instance. Use it instead of a factory or instance registration, which protected decisions still refuse. (#2831)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.169.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.169.0)): Stage now renders read models with composite keys instead of refusing them. Projections key each instance by all of its key parts through Chronicle's typed composite keys, and `from` and `remove with` blocks and list queries with equality filters work over such read models. Single-instance queries by key parts stay refused until Chronicle supports them (`STAGE-ESM-047`). (#481)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.224.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.224.0)): `Samples/Catalog`, `Samples/Vendors`, `Samples/Billing` (the Invoicing blueprint; `Samples/Invoicing` is the living language sample), `Samples/CustomerPortal`, `Samples/Contracts`, `Samples/WorkOrders`, `Samples/Bookings`: each an application whose every screen fills a template of its blueprint (`template blueprint Cratis.Blueprint.<Name> <Template> version "^1.0"`), with the navigation declared in the layout, the blueprint's own sample-data pack as the preview data, events feeding the list, board, calendar, timeline, dashboard, inbox and profile views, and a specification for each slice.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.224.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.224.0)): The conformance vectors (golden syntax) for the seven samples and their entries in the sample list.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.224.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.224.0)): The blueprint render check (`render-blueprint-samples.sh`) renders the seven samples with the published packages.
- **AI** ([Release v7.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.86.0)): `cratis-engineering-consolidation-guard` skill: when folding several pull requests into one, re-landing reverted work or restoring commits by cherry-pick, prove nothing was dropped — per-commit pairing by patch-id and `range-diff`, per-file content checks that catch merge resolutions which revert or lose files, and a specification-file ratchet. Ships a portable `verify-consolidation.sh` (with `--discover` and `--self-test`) and the pattern for running it as a required pull request check, with accepted losses recorded as commit trailers. (#413)
- **AI** ([Release v7.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.86.0)): A GitHub Copilot `plugin.json` at `.cratis/ai`, so the Cratis skills can be submitted to github/awesome-copilot. Its `version` is the plugin manifest version, kept equal to the marketplace manifests by corpus verification. (#503)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.223.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.223.0)): A new MCP tool, `trace-lineage`, traces one read-model property back to its sources. It follows every projection or reducer mapping, AutoMap, join and child mapping to the event property, then to the command property or capture source that produces it, and returns the result as a paged tree. A missing mapping or producer is reported at the hop where the chain breaks, several sources appear as branches, and code the model cannot see into is marked as opaque instead of guessed. The tool is read-only and advisory. (#388)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.222.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.222.0)): The TypeScript compiler's completeness API reports privilege (PLAY0652), purpose (PLAY0602–PLAY0606) and persona (PLAY0574–PLAY0576) findings when `privilege`, `purposes`, `personas`, one of their codes or `all` is selected, matching the C# compiler, so browser and VS Code editors can show them without the native compiler. (part of #527)
- **Components** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `DataTableForQuery` and `DataTableForObservableQuery` accept an opt-in `serverQuery` that filters and sorts the complete server result before paging: the table's sort is sent as Arc `sortBy`/`sortDirection` (optionally renamed through `sortFields`), column filters and search text become query arguments through your `toArguments` function, rows are shown as the server returns them, and any change goes back to the first page with search debounced (300 ms by default). `DataPage` passes it through. Without `serverQuery` the tables keep filtering and sorting the loaded page only. The query must return an `IQueryable` or use MongoDB `Observe` and apply the filter arguments itself; see [Data tables](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/blob/main/Documentation/DataTables/index.md). (#178)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.catalog` - catalog overview dashboard, products list, product page (variants, price history), categories list; `catalog-en` pack. Tracks #167.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.vendors` - vendors list, vendor page (contacts, purchase orders, certificates), onboarding board, performance dashboard; `vendors-en`. Tracks #168.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.invoicing` - receivables overview, invoices list, invoice page (lines, payments, record-payment dialog), payments list, due-date calendar; `invoicing-en`. Tracks #169.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.customer-portal` - portal home, orders and order page, invoices, support inbox, profile; `customer-portal-en`. Tracks #170.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.contracts` - contracts list, contract page (parties, obligations, amendments), terms timeline, needs-attention inbox; `contracts-en`. Tracks #171.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.work-orders` - work orders list, work order page (tasks, parts, time), board, visit schedule; `work-orders-en`. Tracks #172.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.bookings` - bookings calendar, list, booking page, resources as cards, new-booking wizard; `bookings-en`. Tracks #173.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): Every package: navigation, thumbnails, category `Business / <Domain>`, specifications for the definition of done of every template, the screen set, the application behaviour, every forced standard state, and the Chromium audit at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes with colour contrast.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.71.0)): Docs `blueprints/<domain>-blueprint.md`, the table of contents and the index; the packages join the browser specifications of the JavaScript build; the root `blueprintCatalog.json` lists all fifteen domain blueprints.
- **AI** ([Release v7.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.85.0)): A committed Screenplay model can be marked as generated evidence with `"modelFirst": { "frozen": true, "reason": "…" }` in `.cratis/ai.json`. The model-first status check then reports it as not opted in (`frozen-model`), and agents leave it alone unless a request names it explicitly. (#514)
- **AI** ([Release v7.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.85.0)): A `process` block in `.cratis/ai.json` routes the Screenplay lifecycle to the project's own stores. `decisions` sets the decision folder and id pattern, `workItems` the backlog, `acceptance` the split between the person who confirms the model's meaning and the person who commits it, and `agents` lists project addenda that the Screenplay modeler, reviewer and renderer read after their own instructions. Addenda can only narrow what an agent does. (#514)
- **AI** ([Release v7.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.85.0)): The P6 acceptance step supports split acceptance, recorded as `Model-Meaning-Accepted-By:` and `Model-Committed-By:` commit trailers with role and GitHub handle. (#514)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.88.0)): Fluent `FromEvery` and `FromAll` mappings can set a constant with `ToValue(...)`, matching `Set(...).ToValue(...)` for concepts, enums and `null`. (#4663)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Run modeled specifications with composite event values in given, appended and produced events, in both the specification executor and the semantic Host; expected events match composite members regardless of member order. (#441)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.125.0)): Composite keys of protected read models are validated through accessors the Arc source generator produces, with no reflection. Key types the generator cannot see — partial, external, derived or from experimental assemblies — can be registered with `ProtectedReadModelKeyAccessors.Register<T>(...)`. See [Protected key accessors](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/protected-key-accessors.md). (#3392)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.125.0)): `ProtectedReadModelKeyOptions.Strict` rejects composite key types that have neither a generated accessor nor a registration, instead of falling back to reflection. Under NativeAOT this is always the behavior. (#3392)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.125.0)): Analyzer diagnostic [ARC0030](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/code-analysis/ARC0030.md) reports a composite key type the generator cannot produce an accessor for. (#3392)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.163.0)): Rendered applications and `ui profile` resolution can use the default blueprint's `DashboardShell` and `MinimalShell` layouts, which ship with Scene 4.70.1.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.221.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.221.0)): `find-modeling-smells` reports a new advisory rule, `SMELL006`, which asks about an event property that no mapping, AutoMap, reaction, condition or specification reads. (part of #393)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.87.0)): `AddCratisChronicle` overloads with a trailing `connectionStringName` parameter that choose which `ConnectionStrings` entry to read, for an Aspire resource that is not named `chronicle`. (#4917)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.86.0)): Reactor and reducer deliveries carry a delivery id, which the .NET client returns with each result. Clients that do not return it keep working: their results complete the oldest outstanding delivery for the partition. (part of #4664)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.161.0)): Behaviors, interaction actions, composition values, contributions, forms, screen directives, screens, UI profiles, command form schemas and UI syntax now report unsupported model content as typed conversion diagnostics (code, severity, construct, path and source location) instead of aborting the whole Scene conversion; the rest of the Scene still converts. New codes: `STAGE-SCENE-BEHAVIOR-*`, `STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-002`/`-003`, `STAGE-SCENE-COMPOSITION-*`, `STAGE-SCENE-CONTRIBUTION-*`, `STAGE-SCENE-FORM-*`, `STAGE-SCENE-DIRECTIVE-*`, `STAGE-SCENE-SCREEN-*`, `STAGE-SCENE-PROFILE-*` and `STAGE-SCENE-SCHEMA-*`. (#532)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.84.0)): A batch append, a unit of work and `AppendMany` can now carry one concurrency guard for each stream of the same event source, for example a guard on `REPORT / SendApprovals` and another on `REPORT / Corrections / <id>`. The kernel validates them all before writing anything, so the batch stays atomic. Events for several streams of one event source previously kept only the first stream's guard, or failed with `IncompatibleConcurrencyScopesForEventSource`. (#4818)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.84.0)): New `AppendMany` overloads on `IEventSequence` take a list of event source id and concurrency scope pairs, so one event source id can appear with several scopes. (#4818)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.84.0)): A concurrency violation now reports which guard failed (`ScopeIndex` and `Scope`). (#4818)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.220.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.220.0)): `propose-rename` can rename a composite stream id part. It renames the part declaration and every `part = …` mapping in command routes, specification routes, examples and redelivery locators. Part order and stored stream ids stay the same, and the rename is refused when the new name collides with another part of the same stream. (#575)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.219.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.219.0)): The `PLAY0516` timeline-order repair can now replace a folder glob import with an explicit, ordered list of imports when no safe move or pin fixes the order. Every document the glob reached is kept, and the reordered model is verified before it is offered. (#573)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.219.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.219.0)): The TypeScript compiler offers a quick fix for `PLAY0516`, including edits in the importing document, and the web editor shows it as a code action. (#573)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.219.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.219.0)): VS Code offers the `PLAY0516` repair as a reviewed code action, with the same preview, drift checks and recovery as the other saved-file repairs. (#573)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): Given read-model state in a specification seeds that read model per key: the seed replaces earlier projected state for its key, later events still fold into it, other keys keep their history, and commands invoked during the run see the same seed. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): Seeded read models that have no projection are returned exactly as seeded in read-model and query assertions. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): Query assertions that supply equality filters run against the projected and seeded read models: filters combine with the key, omitted optional filters do not narrow the result, and rows are matched regardless of order. (#546)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.25.0)): `Job.OnBeforeDispatchingJobSteps(JobStepDispatchContext)`, an optional hook that runs before any step starts, both when a job starts and when it resumes. `JobStepDispatchContext.InitializationMayBeIncomplete` is `true` when the job is recovering from a crash during startup, so the job can finish or repeat its own one-time initialization (#40)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.25.0)): `CannotResumeJobError.MissingStepPlan`: a job saved by an earlier version while it was preparing or starting its steps cannot be resumed safely and now fails with this reason instead of being planned again (#40)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.25.0)): `StartJobError.InvalidStepPlan`: starting a job fails early when a step request cannot be saved and read back as JSON (#40)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.218.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.218.2)): A new warning, `PLAY0803`, reports a `matches` pattern that can backtrack exponentially in browser engines, such as `(a+)+$`, `(a|a)*` or `(a*)*`, and names the ambiguous subexpression with a simpler rewrite where one exists. The C# compiler, the TypeScript compiler, Monaco and VS Code report it identically; very large patterns are not analyzed. (#806)
- **AI** ([Release v7.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.84.0)): `PreviewAgentTier` query shows, before `ConfigureAgent` is applied, what a proposed tier resolves to on the agent's provider or on each member of its pool: the model, where it comes from (configured, derived, borrowed or subscription alias) and whether the tier would be refused, with the reason (#759)
- **AI** ([Release v7.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.84.0)): `PreviewProviderTierModels` query resolves every tier against a proposed provider tier mapping before `SetAIProviderTierModels` is applied; a tier left out derives from the provider's catalog, exactly as it would after applying the mapping. See [provider harnesses](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/blob/main/Documentation/provider-harnesses.md) (#759)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): The Stage frontend runtime is published to npm as `@cratis/stage.runtime` with every Stage release, at the same version as the Stage packages, with provenance. It carries the Stage application shell, command execution through Arc proxies, data sources, navigation and Stage components, with Arc, Scene, Cratis Components and React as peer dependencies. Rendered applications still embed the runtime for now; switching them to the package follows. (part of #499)
- **AI** ([Release v7.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.83.0)): The Screenplay modeling skills teach the explicit author-intent `PLAY0470` repair: discover repair intents, page both destination report views, choose a destination for every production, then apply. The corpus pins Screenplay 4.216.0 and its compiler contract (#784)
- **AI** ([Release v7.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.83.0)): The streams, command-surface, model-authoring and toolchain skills teach `PLAY0802` (`ReservedStoredName`): a stored event stream named `All` or stored event source named `Default` is refused while authoring, a pin away from the reserved name is valid, and `PLAY0273` remains the binding backstop (#776)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.218.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.218.0)): The TypeScript compiler's completeness API reports conflicting screen data bindings (PLAY0530) and screen data that does not match its query (PLAY0531) when `data-bindings`, either code or `all` is selected, matching the C# compiler, so browser and VS Code editors can show them without the native compiler. (part of #527)
- **cli** ([Release v3.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.80.0)): `cratis chronicle streams closed list` lists the closed stream scopes of an event sequence with their dimensions, origin, owning constraint, sequence number and closing time, filtered by event source id or type and event stream type or id; table, plain and JSON output. Requires Chronicle 19.39.0 or later. (#304)
- **cli** ([Release v3.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.80.0)): `cratis chronicle streams closed reopen --reason <text>` repairs a mistaken manual close of one exact scope, after confirmation (`--yes` to skip); the kernel records the reason and actor as a `StreamScopeReopened` event and refuses closures owned by a closing event. (#304)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.155.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.155.0)): Execute `AllGreaterThan` and `AllGreaterThanOrEqual` collection validation rules in modeled specifications and the semantic Host, with rule-specific refusals for unsupported operands or collection types. (#523)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.217.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.217.0)): `GenerationFixturesCorpus.V21R2` and `GenerationFixturesCorpus.SourceFormsV21R2`: an additive ESM v21 generation fixture revision whose `TenantOrderId` generation body is portable, compilable C#. `GenerationFixturesCorpus.V21` and `SourceForms` stay byte-identical, and both revisions have the same canonical ESM bytes, semantic revision and specification outcomes. Consumers that render the fixture should switch to `V21R2`. (#836)
- **AI** ([Release v7.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.82.0)): Codex can run the Cratis store-mutation and pull-request shell guards through a native `PreToolUse` hook. Adapter failures deny the command; file and patch writes, Stop and session hooks, and installation through `cratis ai install` are not covered yet. (part of #401)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.216.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.216.0)): `propose-repair` accepts a `destinationIntent` (version 1) for PLAY0470. The intent names one explicit `for` expression and route confirmation for every event production of the command. Existing explicit destinations are confirmed, never retargeted, and nothing is guessed or fixed in bulk. See [MCP authoring tools](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/authoring-tools.md). (#397)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.216.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.216.0)): `read-workspace` view `repair-intents` lists PLAY0470 commands with every production a host must collect a destination for. Repair capabilities advertise the repair under `intentActions`, separately from the existing no-input repairs. (#397)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.216.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.216.0)): `read-proposal` views `production-destinations` and `production-destination-identities` page the retained routing report. The report states that no executable baseline existed, so it makes no routing-preservation claim. (#397)
- **cli** ([Release v3.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.79.0)): `cratis chronicle observers retry-failed-partitions <OBSERVER_ID>` retries every failed partition of an observer in one operation. The kernel recovers a bounded batch at a time; the command returns once recovery is started and reports how many partitions were failed. Requires Chronicle 19.80.0 or later.
- **AI** ([Release v7.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.81.0)): Changes to the instruction corpus, harness adapters and distribution manifests are routed to `@Cratis/core` for review through CODEOWNERS. (#755)
- **AI** ([Release v7.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.81.0)): Verification warns about package sources, registry URLs, install commands, MCP server launch commands and dependency declarations that a change adds to instruction files, with file and line. The warning flags supply-chain changes for human review and never fails the build. (#755)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.215.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.215.0)): `ObservationComparison.Compare` compares a model with a host-supplied `ObservationSnapshot` and returns an `ObservationReport`. The snapshot holds registered event types and generations, append counts, command and reaction edges attributed by causation, and registered consumers. The report gives typed findings (Drift, Informational, Unknown or Excluded), the coverage behind them and per-slice evidence. Absence is never reported as drift when coverage is partial, conditional or opaque. Productions are reported as Unknown with a stated reason. See [Observation comparison](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/observation-comparison.md). (#294)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.215.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.215.0)): `ObservationIdentityMap.FromModel` maps events by their stored name and generation and commands by unique name; hosts add reactor and other target-specific identifiers with `With`. (#294)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.215.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.215.0)): `ObservationReportJson` writes the report as stable JSON, so every host reports the same result. (#294)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.154.0)): Semantic specification runs (the SpecRunner semantic engine and the Host's specification endpoint) execute specifications that fire application, startup and shutdown triggers and that advance the clock through interval and schedule reactions, matching the Screenplay reference evaluator. Live Host runs and rendered applications still refuse these triggers. (part of #343)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.122.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.122.0)): The Screenplay generator states exact collection values in specification fixtures: collection expressions, arrays, `List<T>` initializers and `Array.Empty<T>()` whose elements are all constants or concept values. A collection with a spread, a computed element, or a set or sorted target still leaves the scenario out with SP0039. (#3383)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.122.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.122.0)): The Screenplay generator reads exact collection checks in event assertions, `e.Tags.SequenceEqual(...)` and `!e.Tags.Any()` for an empty collection, so a scenario whose event carries a collection can state the whole event. (#3383)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.151.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.151.0)): Opt-in render-around: set `CratisPlanOptions.Refusals` to `ArtifactRefusalMode.RenderAround` (carried as the `cratis-render:refusals` profile input) to render the application while dropping only specifications refused with `STAGE-ESM-011` or `STAGE-ESM-024`. Any other refusal keeps the whole plan refused. (part of #500)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.151.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.151.0)): `ArtifactRenderPlan.Outcome` (`Complete`, `Partial`, `Refused`) and `ArtifactRenderPlan.Refusals` list every dropped specification with its codes; `CratisPlanResult` exposes both. A partial plan keeps its error diagnostics, so `Success` stays false and consumers must check `Outcome` to use it. A new information diagnostic `STAGE-PLAN-007` reports how many specifications were rendered around. (part of #500)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.214.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.214.0)): Model comparison reports changes to stored enum numbers: `ModelDifference.Enumerations` lists each enum member whose number changed (for example after inserting or reordering members of an unnumbered enum), was removed, had its number reused by another member, or was renamed while keeping its explicit number. Each change says whether an event stores the enum and whether it breaks the stored contract. See [decision 0094](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0094-report-enum-numeric-identity-changes-in-model-comparison.md). (#667)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.214.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.214.0)): The MCP `semantic-diff` and `read-proposal view=semantic-diff` list these changes as `enum-*` items in the `members` section, with `beforeNumber`, `afterNumber`, their numbering and `stored`. Other items are unchanged. (#667)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.214.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.214.0)): An enum with duplicate member names or numbers is reported as not comparable instead of being compared. (#667)
- **AI** ([Release v7.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.77.0)): The always-loaded rules state that the user's instructions take precedence over guidelines in a skill. This never overrides safety rules or hooks. (#754)
- **AI** ([Release v7.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.77.0)): The skill-authoring lint warns when two skills' `Use when` triggers overlap strongly (token Jaccard similarity of 0.4 or higher). The warning is advisory and never fails verification. (#754)
- **AI** ([Release v7.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.77.0)): The rules now carry three safeguards that previously lived only in skills, so a competing skill can no longer drop them: never copy secrets or personal values into models, notes, state or briefs, or use real data in examples; legacy extraction uses Git read-only; and committed or applied EF Core migrations are append-only. (#754)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): Screenplay conversion can now report unsupported model content as typed diagnostics instead of throwing. New overloads on `ProjectionConverter`, `ReadModelConverter`, `SliceConverter` and `ArrangementConverter`, plus `ScreenplayEventModelVisitor.Convert`, collect `ConversionDiagnostic` entries (code, severity, construct, path and source location, convertible to a Screenplay `Diagnostic`) and convert the rest of the model. The existing overloads keep throwing as before. (#531)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): Diagnostic codes `STAGE-PROJECTION-001` to `STAGE-PROJECTION-008` and `STAGE-SCENE-ARRANGEMENT-001` to `STAGE-SCENE-ARRANGEMENT-004`, documented in the [Stage rendering reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/stage-rendering.md). (#531)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.120.0)): `[ExplicitOffset]` accepts concepts over `DateTimeOffset` and `DateTime`, refusing values without an explicit offset exactly as for the primitive types. Concepts over `DateOnly` are refused with a configuration error, because a calendar date has no offset. Requires Arc's default JSON options. See [Require explicit date-time offsets](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/commands/input-binding.md). (#3350)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.120.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.120.0)): Generated Screenplay models include date comparisons on concept-backed `DateOnly`, `DateTime` and `DateTimeOffset` command properties and validator rules and concept-wrapped date literals; both sides must resolve to the same date type. (#3350)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.119.0)): `ArcOptions.QueryHealth` (`QueryHealthOptions` with `RequireAuthentication` and `Roles`, bound from `Cratis:Arc:QueryHealth`) controls who may observe query health. (#2873)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.82.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.82.2)): Event sequences report how far their constraint indexes lag behind the log as a metric, alongside metrics for repairs and unknown append outcomes. (#4895)
- **AI** ([Release v7.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.75.0)): Quality gates accept `timeoutSeconds` and `timeoutHint`. A gate that runs past its deadline is stopped together with its child processes. It is reported as a named NOT VERIFIED failure that suggests a narrower command to run instead. The shipped backend-specs gate has a 120-second deadline. (#753)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.70.0)): The `core:toolbarItem` descriptor describes `presentation`: what the item shows (`mode`: icon and text, icon only, text only) and a `placement` hint, as an open object, so an editor's generic Object editor can author the hints the toolbar already reads and a host or theme styles by (part of Cratis/StudioIssues#631)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.82.0)): `ReadModelScenario<T>.ProcessAppendedEvents(IEnumerable<AppendedEvent>)` folds appended events with their original `EventContext`. Projections and reducers see each event's real sequence number and occurred time, so decisions that compare revisions behave as they do against the event store. Events from several event sources are folded in sequence-number order with the same key resolution as `ProcessEventsFor`. (#5034)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.117.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.117.0)): `IOccurrenceCommandExecutor` in Arc's Chronicle integration runs a command for an `OccurrenceContext` under an effect name. It records an effect receipt atomically with the command's events, so a redelivered occurrence returns the recorded outcome without running the handler again. Validation, rule and constraint refusals are recorded as final; authorization failures, concurrency conflicts and exceptions are left unrecorded, so a later attempt can retry them. Kernels without effect-receipt support are refused. See [Executing commands for occurrences](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/occurrence-commands.md). (#3288)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.81.0)): `IUnitOfWork.RefuseImmediateAppends()` refuses every immediate append in the current flow before anything is sent, while transactional appends still enroll in the unit. Use it when a unit of work must commit atomically with other state, such as an effect receipt, so a retry never repeats an append that escaped the unit. The returned frame owns completion when the unit has no other owner; inside an ASP.NET unit of work or with read protection enforced, completion stays with the existing owner. Read admission is unchanged. See [Effect receipts](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/occurrences/effect-receipts.mdx). (#5019)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.80.0)): Stopping or resuming a job now answers whether the job took the request and why not (`StopJobResponse` and `ResumeJobResponse` with an `Accepted` flag and a `JobCommandRefusal`), through the contracts, the .NET client (`IJobs.Stop` and `IJobs.Resume` return a `JobCommandResult`) and the Workbench, which reports a refusal as an error. The proto changes are additive. (part of #4991)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.80.0)): `RetryFailedPartitions` command and gRPC operation that retries every failed partition of an observer through per-partition jobs started a bounded batch at a time, without reopening a concluded replay job. (part of #4991)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Preview regions for a design host: `identity`, `shell`, `menu`, `content` and `accent` are marked with `data-scene-region`, `ScenePreviewHost` reports `onRegionHover` and `onRegionActivate`, `regions.regionRect` measures a drawn region in the preview's own pixels, `describeRegions` says which regions each mode draws and the fallback, and the designer contract gains rule N9 (#193)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Profile identity: a name, a logo (initials, one of eight icons or an inline image) and whether the name shows, resolved by `resolveProfileIdentity` and drawn by the default shell in every menu mode (#194)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Profile appearance: `corners` (sharp, default, round) and `font` from a licensed font catalog with a sample line, resolved by `resolveAppearance`, with the new `text.fontFamily` token read by the default blueprint and the PrimeReact bridge (#195)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Typed `navigationMode` and `menuTheme` shell options with eight modes and three menu themes, the behavior of each mode and the touch regime, the legacy `slim-plus` string mapped without rewriting stored profiles, and a default menu mode per shell with the status line for a shell switch (#196)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Profile problems with a severity, a section and a one-click fix: theme needs a package, accent too light, own shell has no value, logo image missing and Reveal needs hover, with `applyProfileFix` and a contrast function that grades an accent against white (#197)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): The package list answers for a designer: rows with reasons and locks, what can be added, moves, removal that never silently changes the theme, and choosing a blueprint or a theme (#198)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Picker metadata: Cratis Default, Dashboard and Minimal shell cards with wireframes checked by `verify-blueprints`, theme cards with the package selecting one adds, the colour scheme switch, eight menu mode schematics, the version rule with its status chip, and the value of my own shell (#199)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Sample data pack descriptions with columns and a status classification for pills, a description of the generic pack, and the rule that production builds never include sample data (#200)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.69.0)): Documentation for every new rule in the designer contract, with the decisions and the questions that remain open, and an updated `scene-ui-profile` skill and rules (#201)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.212.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.212.0)): `open-workspace` returns `applicationIdentity`, so you can build a typed `parts` target for a model note from names. (#784)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.144.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.144.0)): Rendered backend pins (Cratis, Arc, Chronicle, Specifications and the test packages) come from Stage's own central package versions, so a package update moves what generated applications pin without a separate edit; the daily package update also moves the `@cratis/*` frontend pins and the framework name catalog. (#493)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.23.0)): Bounded jobs: `JobsOptions.MaxActiveStepsPerJob` (or `Job.MaxActiveSteps`) keeps a sliding window of active steps and starts the next one as each step reaches an outcome. A resumed job rebuilds its window from storage. Off by default.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.23.0)): Step attempts: `JobsOptions.MaxStepAttempts`, `StepRetryBackoff` and `MaxStepRetryBackoff`, with `Job.ShouldRetryStep` deciding per failure. A failed attempt is scheduled again by the job with exponential backoff and counts as a failure only when its attempts run out. Default is one attempt.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.23.0)): `IJobsManager.RetryFailedSteps(jobId)` and `Job.CanRetryFailedSteps` run the failed steps of a job that completed with failures again. Opt-in per job type; resuming such a job is unchanged.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.23.0)): Optional step leases: `JobsOptions.StepLeaseTimeout` makes running steps record a heartbeat. The watchdog restarts a step only when its owner silo has left the cluster and its lease has expired; steps from older releases are left alone for one lease period.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.23.0)): `IJobStepStorage.CountByStatus(jobId)` as a default interface member, implemented for MongoDB, SQL and in-memory storage. Persisted steps gain an optional `Attempt` and `Lease`; existing data reads as attempt 1 with no lease.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.23.0)): `JobsOptions.JobWatchdogInterval`, `StepDeliveryMaxAttempts`, `StepDeliveryRetryDelay` and `MaxStepDeliveryRetryDelay`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.116.0)): The new `IsSnapshot` property on `ObservableQueryEmissionContext` tells an emission guard that it is admitting a one-shot snapshot result rather than a stream emission. (#2955)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.116.0)): `IEndpointFallbackAuthorization` evaluates the host's default authorization for a request. Arc Core requires an authenticated caller when authentication handlers are registered. ASP.NET Core hosts evaluate the configured `FallbackPolicy`. (#2952)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.143.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.143.0)): `CratisRendering.ResolveProfileInputs(scene, approvedPackages)` resolves every ui profile with the Scene engine's planner and returns a `CratisProfileInputFinding` for each profile input the target cannot honor: an unknown package, a missing layout, packages that cannot be used together, a missing theme, an invalid root template and an unresolved blueprint template reference. The findings carry stable `STAGE-PROFILE-001` to `STAGE-PROFILE-006` codes. (Cratis/Screenplay#722)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.79.0)): Projection registrations now carry the client's software version. The .NET client sends `ChronicleOptions.SoftwareVersion`, which defaults to the entry assembly's version and can be set with the `Cratis:Chronicle:SoftwareVersion` configuration key (`Cratis__Chronicle__SoftwareVersion`). The client logs a warning at startup while the version is the default `1.0.0`. An older kernel ignores the version and registers as before, so the guard takes effect once the kernel runs this release. See [the registration lifecycle](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/projections/registration-lifecycle.md). (#5012)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.114.0)): Generated Screenplay models now include `==` and `!=` comparisons between Uuid values, Guid-backed concepts and proven canonical Uuid literals in command guards and protected read paths at ESM v28. The generator refuses unsupported operands, such as optional values, type-changing casts and constructors whose forwarding cannot be proven, and withholds them below ESM v28. (#3344)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): The Cratis renderer renders Date and DateTime comparisons in command `require` clauses, `produces when` conditions and property validation rules. Dates are compared as calendar days (`DateOnly`) and DateTimes as UTC instants (`DateTimeOffset`), as the reference evaluator compares them. Each compared DateTime command property carries Arc's `ExplicitOffset` marker, so input without an offset or `Z` is refused. (#473)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.141.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.141.0)): A compared DateTime property typed through a concept is bound as a `DateTimeOffset` and converted to the concept where an event, rule or response uses it, until Arc accepts concept-wrapped dates (Cratis/Arc#3350).
- **AI** ([Release v7.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.72.0)): Inspect each provider's effective tier model through `ProviderTierResolution.ResolveTiersFor`, also available as `ResolveTiersFor` from `@cratis/ai`. Results identify stored, derived, borrowed and subscription-alias models, or explain why a tier cannot be served. (part of #345)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.3)): A specification that renders the MasterDetail, Dashboard and ProfileSettings templates through the effective package set `resolvePackageHost` resolves for a UI profile listing `Cratis.Blueprint.Default` - the profile the live-Stage / generated-React vector uses (Cratis/Stage#504): no findings, every shipped template offered to a template browser as usable, the same components as the registry the browser audit renders with, and the vector's slot contents present by id.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.3)): Documentation in `blueprints/template-set.md`: configuring a template from the screen that uses it (exposure and `instance` contribution, what the shipped templates expose), and how Studio's template browser, the Stage and the vector share one package set.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.78.0)): `EventStoreForTesting` and `Defaults` in `Cratis.Chronicle.Testing` have new constructors that take an `EventStoreName` and an `EventStoreNamespaceName`, so scenarios that need their own identity no longer share `testing`/`default`. `ReadModelScenario`, `ReactorScenario` and `OccurrenceScenario` built from such a `Defaults` use its identity too, and reactors see it in their event context. The existing constructors keep `testing`/`default`. (#5018)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.22.0)): `step.BeginTestRun()` lets a specification run a job step's `PerformStep` under a real run, without a running job: callbacks posted through `TryInvokeOnActivation` are admitted and run, one at a time, on a stand-in activation scheduler. `JobStepTestRun<TState>.Perform(state)` returns what the step returned, `Stop()` cancels the work while admission stays open, `CloseAdmission()` refuses callbacks, and `IsOnActivation` tells a spec whether code runs on the activation. A test run records and delivers nothing. `JobStepAlreadyHasALiveRun` and `JobStepTestRunWasSuperseded` report misuse. See [Testing your job steps](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md#testing-your-job-steps). (#85)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.208.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.208.0)): `PLAY0268` (not admitted by the executable model) carries a `subject` argument naming the innermost declaration that contains the refused construct, and an `issue` argument when the refusal cites a tracking issue. See [decision 0086](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0086-give-diagnostics-named-typed-arguments-declared-per-code.md) and the [diagnostics reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (#838)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.208.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.208.0)): `PLAY0367`, `PLAY0699` and `PLAY0787` (match patterns) carry the owning command or concept as `subject`, the `property`, the `pattern` and, for the first two, the `offset` in the pattern; the C# and TypeScript compilers report the same arguments. (#838)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.1)): A real-Chromium accessibility audit of every screen template the default blueprint ships (business, foundation, state, support, auth, status, nesting chain): axe-core WCAG 2.0/2.1/2.2 A and AA including colour contrast and target size, no horizontal overflow, and landmark structure (one `main`, no duplicate/nested landmarks, unique names, all content in a landmark, a level-one heading) - in every data state, at desktop/tablet/phone widths, in the light and dark theme (1,000+ browser specifications), against the registry of a host with the blueprint's dependencies (`core`, PrimeReact with a theme preset, the blueprint). It runs in the JavaScript Build workflow's existing browser step, so a regression fails the Scene CI.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.1)): A control specification that breaks the page it just judged (pale text, a second `main`, content outside a landmark, two tiny adjacent buttons) and expects each finding, so a clean audit is never vacuous.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.1)): Documentation in `blueprints/template-set.md` ("In a real browser").
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.21.0)): `IJobAdmission` lets a job's owner allow or refuse a job's execution. Register it with `siloBuilder.AddJobAdmission<T>()` on every silo that can host the owner's job types. `MayRun(JobAdmissionRequest, CancellationToken)` receives the job's key, id, type and request; every registered admission must allow, and with none registered every job runs. See [Admit or refuse a job's execution](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md). (#81)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.21.0)): `StartJobError.NotAdmitted` is returned when the owner refuses a job's start, and `CannotResumeJobError.NotAdmitted` when it refuses a resume. `IJobsManager.Resume` returns `false` for a refused job. (#81)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.207.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.207.0)): The protected command read diagnostics PLAY0781–PLAY0785 carry typed arguments, so tools can read why a read could not be protected instead of parsing message text. They identify the command and the read, and PLAY0781 adds the read model and a `reason` keyword (`reducer`, `join`, `no-removal`, `key-type`, …). Every refused read gets its own diagnostic, and messages are unchanged. See [diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (#851)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): `applyShellSwitch(document, plan, templates?)` (`@cratis/scene.engine`) and `ShellSwitchApplier.Apply` (C#, `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Shell`) apply a `planShellSwitch` plan as a pure function over the document: the content of a moved outlet is appended to the target outlet in order, dropped content is removed, kept outlets are untouched. A screen template that fits a moved outlet fits the new outlet instead (a name qualified with the old shell's is requalified with the new shell's, and a dropped outlet leaves the template placed explicitly), so the screens built from it move with it. The result carries the review list of what changed (screen or template, outlet, action, target, element count, a sentence with real names). A plan with blockers is not applied.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): `templatePlacement` / `ShellPlacement.PlacementOf` expose where a screen template's chain lands in the shell; `outletsUsedBy` now uses it.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): `shell-switch-apply-fixtures.json`, asserted by both engines: keep, same shell, an outlet nobody uses, primary following to a differently named primary outlet, moving into an occupied primary outlet, mapping, several outlets into one, dropping, a template that fits the lost outlet (moved, mapped, dropped), qualified templates (moved, requalified), a nested template chain and a blocked plan.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.68.0)): Documentation in `blueprints/root-template.md` ("Applying the switch").
- **Arc** ([Release v22.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.112.0)): When a command's effective read protection is `On` (from the command, an inherited base, the assembly or the `CratisReadProtection` MSBuild property), the generator writes its keyed read-model reads, the `require` conditions over them and the event mappings from them as ESM v25 output under the `command reads protected` preamble. This covers nullable `[LookUpBy]` reads guarded by a leading null check, non-nullable reads, and guards in `Provide` and `Handle` (including `OneOf` and `Result` returns). See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3298)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.112.0)): A read Screenplay does not admit (for example a view without a removal, a stream-scoped read model, an ambiguous key or a nullable read without a null check) is left in code and reported with the new `SP0079` diagnostic and its reason, instead of being written as an unprotected read.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.138.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.138.0)): The Cratis renderer renders protected command reads. Each command with `reads` gets `[ReadProtection(On)]`, a nullable read-model `Handle` parameter per read looked up with `[LookUpBy]`, the reference "`<View> '<key>' does not exist.`" rejection, and all of its requirements evaluated in `Handle` after the reads. Read paths in `require` and produced-event mappings render, and generated specifications run protected commands in decision mode. (part of #440)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.138.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.138.0)): Command specifications whose command triggers exactly one invoke-only reaction are rendered as a command half and a reaction half instead of being refused; a rejected one is rendered only when the triggering command cannot reject on its own. (part of #440)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.138.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.138.0)): A new reference page, [Protected reads rendering](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/protected-reads-rendering.md), describes the rendered shape and where a rendered application differs from the reference. (part of #440)
- **AI** ([Release v7.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.69.0)): The Screenplay skills teach Uuid `==` and `!=` in command `require` and `produces when` conditions (ESM v28), with an executable authorization example: concepts and identities over Uuid compare when both sides are the same concept, a bare Uuid compares with any Uuid concept, protected read paths are allowed in `require`, and literals must be canonical lowercase hyphenated Uuids. They also list what stays refused (ordering, different concepts, optional operands, Uuid validation and concept rules, read paths in `produces when`). The compiler is pinned to Screenplay 4.205.0. (#746)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.0)): Typed triggers: a record marked `[Trigger("id")]` is registered with its JSON payload schema, and `IEventStore.Occurrences.Record(trigger, idempotencyId)` records it once per idempotency id. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/occurrences/triggers-and-handlers.mdx. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.0)): `IOccurrenceHandler<TTrigger>` handlers are discovered like reactors and run under fenced leases: the client claims due occurrences, renews the lease while the handler runs, completes or fails it, and backs off according to the kernel's retry settings. `OccurrenceContext` carries the occurrence id, attempt, epoch and the `EffectKey` to send to external systems. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.0)): Effect receipts in the unit of work: `IUnitOfWork.UseEffectReceipt(key)` writes the receipt in the same storage transaction as the events, also when the unit appends no events but holds protected reads, and a repeated effect returns the stored outcome instead of appending again. Against a kernel without the `effect-receipts` capability the unit fails with `EffectReceiptsNotSupported` rather than appending without a receipt. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/occurrences/effect-receipts.mdx. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.0)): Lifecycle hooks: the built-in `Startup` and `Shutdown` triggers fire once per logical activation (`ChronicleOptions.ActivationId`) or once per process (`ActivationScope.PerProcess`), for the application or for every namespace (`LifecycleScope.AllNamespaces`). `IOccurrences.EndActivation` records the shutdown of a logical activation. ASP.NET Core and generic hosts register the lifecycle service automatically. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/occurrences/lifecycle.mdx. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.0)): The kernel records an all-namespaces lifecycle occurrence as a durable fan-out intent over a fixed namespace snapshot, so a crash part-way through completes after restart without duplicates. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.0)): Testing: `EventStoreForTesting` and the scenarios gain `Occurrences`, `Capabilities` (current, old or unknown kernel), a deterministic `ScenarioTime`, a reply-dropping interceptor for commit-then-lose-the-reply cases, `OccurrenceContextBuilder` and `OccurrenceScenario<THandler>` for driving a handler step by step. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/occurrences/testing.mdx. (part of #4911)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.206.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.206.0)): `SemanticEvaluator.Decide` returns a `SemanticDecision` with its `Boundary`, its `Dependencies` (a `SemanticDecisionDependencies` of read keys and event contracts) and a `Commit` that can be called again against a later world; a read-only request is refused with a Contract rejection. See [decision 0089](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0089-expose-the-reference-evaluators-decide-commit-split-to-hosts.md). (#833)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.206.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.206.0)): `SemanticEvaluator.ExtendWorld` extends an established world with facts that already hold in the log, giving the same world as establishing all facts at once. (#833)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.206.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.206.0)): `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` publishes `ProtectedCommandReadRaceVectors`: 16 race cases with their model, run only through the public API, so a host can prove it matches the reference. (#833)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.206.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.206.0)): The host loop is documented: decide, extend, commit, settle reactions, then write the whole settled batch guarded on the world it was settled against, and when a retry is allowed. (#833)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.20.0)): `IJobs.StopAndDrain(key, jobId, timeout)` stops a job and waits until its steps are drained. It answers `Drained` once no step code runs for the job, each step's outcome and final checkpoint are persisted, and `OnStepCompletedOrStopped` and `OnStopped` have finished; otherwise `TimedOut` (call again to continue), `Completed`, `NotFound` or `UnknownJobType`. Pass `TimeSpan.Zero` to poll without blocking. This adds a member to `IJobs`, so a hand-written `IJobs` implementation must add it. See [Jobs](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md). (part of #81)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.20.0)): `IJobDrain` lets a job grain be drained directly, and `IJobStepDrain` is the protocol the job uses to drain its steps. (part of #81)
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.0)): The Screenplay skills explain warning `PLAY0690`: a reducer instance is always keyed by the event-source id of the events it reduces, so a reducer-backed view looked up `by` another identity type, or fed by producers with different identity types, is reported. They show the fix (a projection keyed on each `from`, or a join) and what the check does not compare. (#702)
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.0)): The toolchain and modeling-lifecycle skills teach the `screenplay.json` validation gate at the model root: the standalone `screenplay` tool and `cratis screenplay validate` read checks, warnings as errors, scope and open-questions mode from it, `--print-gate` shows the effective gate, and a refused gate file counts as "could not run". (#691)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.205.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.205.0)): Command `require` and `produces when` conditions accept `==` and `!=` between Uuid values, including concepts and identities over Uuid, protected read paths in `require`, and canonical Uuid literals, as ESM v28. When both sides are concepts or identities they must be the same concept. Non-canonical literals are refused with PLAY0273; ordering, Uuid validation rules and Uuid concept rules stay refused. See [decision 0090](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0090-admit-uuid-equality-in-command-conditions.md). (#844)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.111.0)): The Screenplay generator reads chronological comparisons of `DateOnly`, `DateTime` and `DateTimeOffset` properties in command guards (`require` / `produces when`) and literal comparisons in command validation rules as ESM v26 date ordering. Literals are written in Screenplay's canonical form: `yyyy-MM-dd` for dates, UTC with seven fractional digits and `Z` for date-times. Comparisons Screenplay does not admit (offset-less date-time literals, mixing dates with date-times, clock values such as `DateTime.Now`, date rules on concepts, date ordering in reactions, property-to-property validation rules) are reported with the property named instead of being emitted. When `MaximumExecutableModelVersion` is below v26, date comparisons are withheld and reported. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (part of #3339)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.76.0)): A concurrency violation now names the event that caused it: `ConcurrencyViolation` and the `DecisionConflict` values returned by `GetDecisionConflicts` carry `ConflictingEventTypeId` and `ConflictingEventSequenceNumber` for the first event that matched the guard after the position it expected. Both are null when connected to a kernel that does not report them (#4926)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.76.0)): `IUnitOfWork.SuspendReadRegions()` lets a host that runs nested invocations under read protection suspend the read regions the nested invocation inherited, and restore them when the returned scope is disposed (#4953)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.110.0)): `[property: ExplicitOffset]` on a `DateTimeOffset` or `DateTime` command property (nullable too) refuses date-time input that has no offset or `Z`, with the validation message `DateTime command property '<member>' requires an explicit offset or Z.` A `DateTimeOffset` keeps its offset and a `DateTime` is converted to UTC. The accepted spellings match Screenplay's date comparison contract (ESM v26). See [Command input binding](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/commands/input-binding.md). (part of #3339)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.110.0)): `IResourceFreeRequirement` marks an ASP.NET authorization requirement that never reads the command, so its policy also runs when the body cannot be bound. Built-in role, claim, name and authenticated-user requirements count as resource-free already. (#3341)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.75.0)): `DecisionReadRefusalReason.DefinitionMissing` separates "the kernel has no definition for this read model or its projection" from `DefinitionMismatch`, and the refusal message now names what differs.
- **AI** ([Release v7.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.67.0)): The Screenplay skills teach composite read-model keys: a read model with two or more `key` properties, projection root `from` and `remove with` blocks with a `key` block naming every part, list queries narrowed by equality filters, an optional single-instance query with a `by` block naming every part, and specifications that state every part. They also cover the constructs that stay refused on such a read model (joins, children, `every`, `all`, `nested`, `variant`, reducers, protected command reads by parts) and how to choose between a composite key and a composite-typed single key. The compiler is pinned to Screenplay 4.204.0. (#743)
- **AI** ([Release v7.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.67.0)): Hosts that execute Screenplay commands themselves are pointed to Screenplay's public `SemanticDateComparisons` API for chronological Date and DateTime checks.
- **cli** ([Release v3.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.73.0)): `cratis render` renders a new application's reducers as self-reducing read models: the read-model record holds its static `Reduce` methods, with no separate `IReducerFor<T>` class. A destination without a render ownership manifest is a new application; the choice is saved in the managed `.cratis/stage/rendering.json` and kept on every later render, including `--scope`, `--workspace` and `--check`. Existing applications keep separate reducer classes and unchanged output. An edited, missing or unreadable reducer configuration is refused (`CLI-RENDER-021`, `CLI-RENDER-022`, `CLI-RENDER-023`) and nothing is written; changing the convention of an existing application is not supported. See [Reducer rendering convention](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md#reducer-rendering-convention). (#376)
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `Property.schemaKeywords` and `Property.itemSchemaKeywords` carry the standard JSON Schema keywords the schema editor does not model, for the property and for its array `items`. Hosts that own the properties in the tree layout carry them through like `vendorKeywords`. (#426)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.19.0)): `JobStep.TryInvokeOnActivation` lets step work run a callback on its own activation, also while the activation is deactivating. Use it instead of calling the step's own grain reference, for example to save a checkpoint. It returns `ActivationCallbackRejected` (`NoRun`, `StaleRun`, `Closed`, `Cancelled`) when the run is gone or the step is stopping. See [Jobs](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md).
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.19.0)): `JobsOptions.StepStopTimeout` (default 10 seconds) bounds how long stopping a step waits for its work, and `JobStepError.StopTimedOut` reports that the work had not settled in time. The step still reports its stop exactly once when the work settles.
