- **Stage** ([Release v4.135.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.135.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay models compiled to executable model version 26. The specification runner and the host compare `Date` values as calendar dates and `DateTime` values as instants at tick precision in `require`, `produces when` and validation rules, so `require to >= from` works across time zones, matching Screenplay. (part of #473)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.135.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.135.0)): The specification runner and the host reject a `DateTime` command value without an offset or `Z` when it takes part in such a comparison, after authorization and before any rule runs. (part of #473)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.204.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.204.0)): Read models with two or more `key` properties bind and execute: projections key root `from` blocks with a `key` block naming every part, list queries narrow them with equality filters, and an optional single-instance query looks one up with a `by` block naming every part. Models that use them select ESM v27; every other model keeps its version and bytes. See [decision 0088](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0088-admit-composite-read-model-keys-into-the-executable-model.md). (#599)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.204.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.204.0)): `remove with` accepts a `key` block with one `part = expr` line per key part, so a composite-keyed instance can be removed and later recreated. (#599)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.204.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.204.0)): Specifications state every key part in `given readmodel`, `then readmodel` and `then no readmodel`, and query arguments supply every `by` part; a missing part is reported as PLAY0661 and an unknown or ill-typed part as PLAY0662. (#599)
- **cli** ([Release v3.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.71.0)): `CLI-RENDER-020`: a `template blueprint <Package> <Template>` reference to a template the approved blueprint's manifest does not list - no such template, a version outside the pin, or a blueprint that is not approved - stops `cratis render`, and `cratis run` before Docker starts, naming the blueprint and the template. The resolution is Scene's own template resolver, run by Stage's render planner over the approved package set.
- **cli** ([Release v3.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.71.0)): A specification that checks a screen naming a template the blueprint does not ship while another one it does ship is accepted.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.203.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.203.0)): Hosts that execute commands themselves can now apply the reference evaluator's exact Date and DateTime comparison semantics through public .NET APIs instead of copying them. `SemanticDateComparisons` provides `Participants`, `TryParseInstant`, `Compare`, `Check` and `CheckInputs`, and `SemanticConditionEvaluator.Evaluate` evaluates `require` and `produces when` conditions, including protected read operands. See [Check command date comparisons](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/date-comparison-checks.md). (#842)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.132.0)): A specification that only projects facts - events given as history, optionally one more appended with `when append`, then `then readmodel` - renders as a generated read-model scenario (`STAGE-ESM-011` no longer refuses the 37 `Listing...` specifications of the samples). An append an event reaction or constraint governs is still refused, and so is an instance no given event reaches, because the generated scenario could not prove it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.132.0)): A root projection block keyed by a constant (`key "global"`) renders as `UsingConstantKey`, with its `summaryId = "global"` mapping, so a one-row summary projection (`SalesFigures`, `SupportFigures`, `InventoryFigures`) renders.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.132.0)): `RenderFindingKind.TemplateReferenceUnresolved`: a `template blueprint <Package> <Template>` reference the approved blueprint's manifest does not list is reported with the blueprint and template named, through Scene's own resolver (Cratis/Scene#182). It is no longer possible to render a screen that silently has no template.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.132.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.132.0)): `when_planning_the_domain_blueprint_samples` plans each sample (kept in `Verification/live-blueprints/models`, the Screenplay samples) as authored; `when_executing_projected_fact_specifications` builds and runs the generated application of a model using all of the above, and `when_refusing_unprovable_projected_facts` pins what stays refused.
- **AI** ([Release v7.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.66.0)): The Screenplay skills teach the `ui profile` shell: `root template` (an own layout or application-scope screen template, or `blueprint <B> <Name>` with a `version` pin), shell `option` lines, `appearance` (`color scheme`, `accent`) and `new screens use template`. `cratis-screenplay-ui-composition` carries compiled worked examples, the PLAY0788 to PLAY0799 error and fix table and a legacy `layout` negative example (PLAY0791, with its quick fix). The toolchain diagnostics and diagnostic-arguments references list the new codes, and `cratis-screenplay-model-authoring` teaches the new `ui-profiles` MCP read tool (44 tools) and the typed nodes that edit the shell. (#733)
- **Components** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `SchemaEditor` tree layout: a "Generated" toggle that marks a property with `x-generated: true`. Hosts offer it with `allowGeneratedProperty` (a flag or a per-property predicate), or with `onSetGeneratedProperty` in controlled mode. Its labels can be overridden through `labels`, and it is styled through the `generated` part. (#425)
- **Components** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): A generated property shows a read-only marker when the toggle is not offered, for example in `readOnly` mode. Schemas that already contain `x-generated: true` show this marker without any host change. (#425)
- **Components** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `Property.isGenerated`, `Property.vendorKeywords`, `Property.itemVendorKeywords` and the `GENERATED_KEYWORD` constant. (#425)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): `storage.connectionResolution` resolves the connection for every event store, namespace and read model database, on MongoDB and SQL alike, using Arc's connection resolution contract: `Template` and `Http` resolvers from configuration, or `Custom` when hosting the kernel in-process. A resolved connection string or database is used as given, without deriving a name or recording a `DatabaseNames` binding. The cluster database stays `connectionDetails`. (#4948)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): The `Http` resolver calls a language-neutral REST endpoint, the same for every storage type and specified at https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/connection-resolution-contract.md. Chronicle sends `kind`, `eventStore` and `namespace` as its scope. (part of #4948)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): Schema changes connect for the `Schema` purpose and data access for `Runtime`, so migrations can run as a different database user than the one reading and writing data. This covers creating databases and schemas, migrations, table migrations and the replay table swap. (#4949)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): `storage.createIfMissing`: set it to `false` and a missing database or schema fails with `DatabaseDoesNotExist` or `SchemaDoesNotExist` instead of being created. (#4947)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): `storage.schema` keeps Chronicle's SQL tables in a schema of your choice. On PostgreSQL it is put first on the search path. On SQL Server the database user's default schema must match, or migration fails with `DefaultSchemaMismatch`. (#4946)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): MongoDB storage resolves the server, database name and identity of the event store, namespace and read model databases. Access tokens are used as MONGODB-OIDC credentials. (#4950)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): Documentation for storage connection resolution, including a database-per-tenant setup with Entra managed identities: https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/hosting/configuration/storage-connection-resolution.md (#4951) (#4945)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.202.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.202.0)): `BrowserHarness/render-blueprint-samples.sh` renders all eight domain blueprint samples with the installed `cratis` and the approved package set of each blueprint's published npm package (`render-blueprint-samples.cjs` writes the set from the manifest the package publishes), and fails when one does not render.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.202.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.202.0)): `.cratis/ai/rules/project/samples.md` documents it.
- **AI** ([Release v7.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.64.0)): The Screenplay skills teach chronological `Date` and `DateTime` comparisons (ESM v26): `==`, `!=`, `>`, `>=`, `<` and `<=` between same-primitive values in `require`, `produces when` and command or property validation rules. They cover `DateTime` compared as a UTC instant, the Validation rejection for a `DateTime` input without an offset, PLAY0273 for offset-less or unparsable literals, and the forms that stay PLAY0268 (`today`, `$context` operands, mixed `Date`/`DateTime`, concept rules and reaction `where`). Stage guidance says Stage refuses ESM v26 with STAGE-ESM-016. (#735)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.66.0)): Four cases in the shared `template-applicability-fixtures.json` (a manifest-only blueprint resolves a screen template, a dialog template and a layout it lists, and diagnoses one it does not), asserted by both engines.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.129.0)): The specification runner and the host now run protected command reads (`command reads protected`, Screenplay 4.193.0): a command whose required read does not exist is rejected with "<view> '<key>' does not exist." before anything else runs, and `require` conditions and produced-event values can use the read's properties, matching Screenplay's own evaluation. Rendering protected reads is still refused with STAGE-ESM-044. (part of #440)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): `CrmActivityCreateForm` and `CrmActivityEditForm` screen templates (the foundation `FormScreen` for an activity, create with Save and new, edit showing the record), with their thumbnails and the `crm.activities.new` / `crm.activities.edit` ready-to-use screens. The blueprint now has twenty screen templates and five dialog templates.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.65.0)): `ActivitySections` and `ActivityRecord` shapes in both sample-data packs (`crm-nordic-smb`, `crm-enterprise-en`), so each new form is answered by the data of either pack.
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Atomic reaction batches (`reaction batches atomic`, ESM v22): when to declare them, what one batch covers, the rollback and budget rules, and that new models start with them. (#717)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Described command handlers (ESM v23): the payload-only result contract, PLAY0695–PLAY0698, and how specifications and Stage treat a pending handler. (#722)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): First-class text rules `contains`, `starts with` and `ends with` on properties (ESM v24), the PLAY0787 repair for legacy text-rule patterns, and the `PortableMatchPattern.Check` / `checkPortableMatchPattern` APIs. (#724)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Protected command reads (`command reads protected`, ESM v25): which reads qualify, `read.property` paths in `require` and produced events, absence and concurrent-change rejection, and PLAY0780–PLAY0786. (#720)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Reading the typed `arguments` of compiler diagnostics instead of parsing message text, with the codes that carry them and how to tolerate older responses. (#728)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Mapping `$eventSourceId` or `$eventContext.eventSourceId` into an invoked command from a reaction. (#734)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Captures over public events in specifications (PLAY0700, PLAY0701) and the PLAY0694 warning for a directionless Translate slice that reacts to its own capture. (#712)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Added-only first observations in record captures. (#716)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): MCP generation details: `generation.arguments`, exact support counts and entropy, `derivedPattern`, and per-rule proof status with witnesses and repairs. (#698)
- **AI** ([Release v7.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.63.0)): Generated values: `generate pattern` with optional checksums, `generate random` ranges and `generate rule`, rule proofs (PLAY0688, PLAY0689), `validate checksum`, the target-neutral generation context, and the `GenerationStrategyCatalog`, `SemanticConceptCheck.Check` and `SemanticValidationRules.Check` APIs for tool authors. Fundamentals 7.25.0 `GenerateValue.Pattern`, `Integer` and `HasValidChecksum` in the concept skill. (part of #576)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.128.0)): A new application can choose to have its reducers rendered as self-reducing read models, the default that Chronicle's documentation recommends. In that form, the read model record holds its queries and its static `Reduce` methods together, with no separate `IReducerFor<T>` class.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.128.0)): **Opting in:** `CreateNewApplicationProfile` selects the self-reducing form and persists the choice with the application. On later renders, `ReducerConventionInput.Read` loads it back and `WithReducerConvention` passes it to Stage.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.128.0)): **Existing applications:** anything that doesn't select the self-reducing form keeps today's separate reducer classes with unchanged output. Moving an existing application to self-reducing read models is not available yet.
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Directory member pages render the full profile of Core and Partner members from their `/cast.json`, and a profile from public GitHub data for everyone else, each with the terminal, command palette, themes and a printable `/<login>/cv/` (#10)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): `/cast.json` version 2 carries the complete, render-ready profile, with a published JSON Schema (`cast-json.schema.json`) and a format guide so sites not built with Cast can produce it (#10)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): An optional `profile` field in the registry points the directory at a member's `cast.json` when it does not live at `<site>/cast.json` (#10)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): `planShellSwitch` (TypeScript) / `ShellSwitchPlanner.Plan` (C#): the pre-flight of switching the application's shell. Default mapping by outlet name, the current primary outlet and `content` following to the new shell's primary outlet; every outlet of the current shell (and any a screen uses that it lacks) gets a `move` (`keep`, `primary`, `unused`, `unresolved`, and the user's `moveToPrimary` / `mapTo` / `drop` passed back as remaps). An outlet that needs a decision is a blocker with `profileSlotUnplaced` and a message naming the screens and outlets; the candidate's own problems (`rootTemplateUnknown`, `rootTemplateVersionNotSatisfied`, `rootTemplateNotApplicable`, `rootTemplateMissingContentSlot`, `rootTemplateCycle`) block too; the blueprint to add and warnings are listed.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): `findProfileBlockers` (TypeScript) / `ProfileBlockers.Find` (C#): the rules Studio kept in `findProfileBlockers` (missing packages, version conflicts, dependency cycles, unusable shell, incompatible theme, unavailable default screen template, layouts, screen templates, dialog templates and slots the screens still use), moved into the engine with messages that name what they are about. Studio keeps only the presentation (REN2).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): `describeUiProfile` / `UiProfileNodeKind`: component descriptors, in the existing format, for the nodes of a UI profile (Application, Shell with `root.options.*`, Theme with colour scheme and accent, Packages, Sample data), with the choices the application supplies; a theme the packages do not support stays on offer with the reason.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): `placeableScreenTemplates` / `ShellPlacement` (shared by the resolver and the planner), Engine-owned codes `ShellOutletAction` and `ProfileBlockerCode`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): Shared corpora `shell-switch-fixtures.json` (27 cases) and `profile-blockers-fixtures.json` (20 cases), each asserted byte-for-byte by C# (`for_ShellSwitchPlanner`, `for_ProfileBlockers`) and TypeScript (`for_planShellSwitch`, `for_findProfileBlockers`), including a regression vector that no message carries an id or an unsubstituted placeholder (the `{subject}` defect).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): Tests: `for_describeUiProfile` (18).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.64.0)): Docs: "Switching the shell", "What stops a profile from being applied" and "Describing the profile for a designer" in `blueprints/root-template.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.199.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.199.0)): Chronological ordering of `Date` and `DateTime` with `==`, `!=`, `>`, `>=`, `<` and `<=` in `require` and `produces when` conditions (a command property against a command property or a literal) and in command and property validation rules (against a literal). Both sides must have the same primitive. `DateTime` values are compared as UTC instants at tick precision, so one instant written with different offsets is equal. Models that use it select ESM v26; every other model keeps its version and bytes. See [decision 0087](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0087-admit-chronological-date-and-datetime-ordering-in-command-conditions.md). (#803)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.199.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.199.0)): A `DateTime` command input that takes part in a date comparison must carry an offset or `Z`; without one the command is rejected with a Validation outcome before any rule runs. (#803)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.199.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.199.0)): A `DateTime` literal without an offset, or a date literal that does not parse, is reported as PLAY0273. Date ordering with `$context` operands, `today`, protected read paths, mixed `Date` and `DateTime`, on concept rules or in reaction `where` stays refused with PLAY0268. (#803)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): `Cratis.Arc.Connections`: a connection resolution contract (`IConnectionResolver`, `ConnectionRequest`, `ResolvedConnection`, `ConnectionIdentity`, `ConnectionPurpose`). Every part of a resolved connection is optional, so a resolver can answer for the connection string, the database, the schema, the identity, or any combination. (#3314)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): Built-in `Default`, `Template`, `Http` and `Custom` resolvers, with a `Connection` slot and an `Identity` slot that use the same shape and are applied in order. Resolutions are cached in memory per request, honor `expiresAt`, and are dropped when a login fails. (#3314)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): `ArcOptions.ConnectionResolution`, configurable in code or under `Cratis:Arc:ConnectionResolution`, resolves connections for MongoDB and Entity Framework Core alike. An integration's own `ConnectionResolution` replaces it for that integration. (#3314)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): `ConnectionPurposeScope.Begin(ConnectionPurpose.Schema)` so migrations can connect with a different identity than data access. (#3314)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): `MongoDBOptions.ConnectionResolution`, configurable in code or under `Cratis:MongoDB:ConnectionResolution`. A resolved user name and password becomes a SCRAM credential, and a resolved access token becomes a MONGODB-OIDC credential that refreshes through the resolver. (#3315)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): `EntityFrameworkCoreOptions.ConnectionResolution`. Arc-registered contexts resolve the connection for the current tenant and purpose every time it opens, so pooled contexts stay correct across tenants. Access tokens become `SqlConnection.AccessToken` on SQL Server and the password on PostgreSQL. (#3316)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): A language-neutral REST specification, with an OpenAPI description, of the endpoint the `Http` resolver calls: https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/connection-resolution-contract.md (part of #3317)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): Documentation for connection resolution, with how-to pages for MongoDB and Entity Framework Core: https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/tenancy/connection-resolution.md (#3317) (#3313)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.108.0)): A documentation snippet of a protected command for the Cratis documentation site (#3324)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.127.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.127.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay models compiled to executable model version 25 (Screenplay 4.193.0) wherever an equivalent earlier model was accepted. (part of #440)
- **cli** ([Release v3.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.69.0)): A spec checks a `ui profile` naming `Cratis.Blueprint.Crm`: refused with `CLI-RENDER-007` until approved, accepted once approved with the manifest the package publishes (with its `blueprint` and `sampleData` members).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): `migrateLegacyProfile(legacy, library)` (TypeScript, `@cratis/scene.engine`) and `ProfileMigrator.Migrate(legacy, library)` (C#, `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Profiles`): one pure function that turns a legacy profile - the blueprint (`shell`) and `layout` name plus the Studio-only `navigationMode`, `colorScheme`, `accent` and `defaultScreenTemplate` - into the new shape: `root` (blueprint reference pinned to `^major.minor`, or own reference), `root.options.navigationMode`, `appearance`, `defaults.newScreenTemplate`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): Idempotent: a migrated profile comes back unchanged (`changed: false`) with or without the legacy fields; a value already in the new shape wins. Once a root exists the legacy `layout` is dropped (readers accept both shapes, writers emit the new one).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): Six engine-owned diagnostics for what cannot be mapped, with real names: `legacyLayoutUnknown`, `legacyShellNotInstalled`, `legacyShellHasNoLayout`, `legacyColorSchemeUnknown`, `legacyNavigationModeWithoutRoot`, `legacyDefaultScreenTemplateUnknown`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): Shared corpus `profile-migration-fixtures.json` (29 permutations incl. idempotence) asserted by `for_ProfileMigrator` (C#) and `for_migrateLegacyProfile` (TS) with identical profiles (canonical JSON) and identical diagnostics.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.63.0)): Docs: "Migrating a legacy profile" and the compatibility rules in `blueprints/root-template.md`.
- **Cast** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): `@cratis/cast`, an Astro integration that turns one `cast.yaml` into a complete profile site with a career replay, live public GitHub data, writing, talks, projects, a printable `/cv`, a terminal, a command palette and light and dark themes (#1)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): `@cratis/create-cast`, run with `npm create @cratis/cast`, scaffolds a site prefilled from your public GitHub profile, with an hourly GitHub Pages deploy workflow (#1)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Cast badges: Core and Partner come only from the reviewed registry in this repository, Contributor is earned from merged public pull requests in the Cratis organization, and every badge links to its verification page in the directory (#1)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Every Cast site publishes `/cast.json`, a JSON Resume `/resume.json` and `/llms.txt`, so people, directories and AI agents read the same profile (#1)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): The Cast directory for https://cast.cratis.io, with Core, Partner and Contributor sections, a verification page and embeddable badge for every member, and a guide to joining (#1)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.62.0)): `for_effectiveShell/when_composing_with_a_root_template` (blueprint shell, per-screen layout ignored, FullPage opt-out, unknown root falls back with a diagnostic, root screen template with its own primary outlet, legacy profile).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): `TemplateRole.Root` and `TemplateMetadata.role` / `.primaryOutlet` (C# + TS + shape contract). A screen template with the root role is an application shell like a layout: offered only at the `Application` scope, never fits a slot, never a dialog template. `ApplicableTemplates(Application)` returns layouts and root screen templates; the template-applicability corpus has 6 new vectors and a root screen template in the library.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): `resolveEffectiveShell` (TS, `@cratis/scene.engine`) and `ShellResolver.Resolve` (C#, `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Shell`) returning `EffectiveShell` (reference, template, kind, source, package + version, pin evaluation, outlets, primary outlet, options, diagnostics). Order: `profile.root` -> legacy `profile.layout` -> first installed blueprint default -> built-in Bare (single `content` outlet).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): Diagnostics `rootTemplateUnknown`, `rootTemplateVersionNotSatisfied`, `rootTemplateNotApplicable`, `rootTemplateMissingContentSlot`, `rootTemplateCycle`, `rootTemplateBlueprintNotInstalled`, `profileThemeIncompatible`, `profilePackageMissing`, `profileSlotUnplaced`, each with a fixture vector and a message with real names.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): Shared corpus `effective-shell-fixtures.json` (own, blueprint, pinned, drifted, unknown, legacy, none; 31 cases) asserted byte-for-byte by C# (`for_ShellResolver`) and TS (`for_resolveEffectiveShell`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): `blueprintCatalog.json` entries describe a shell's `outlets`, `primaryOutlet` and `role`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.61.0)): Docs: `blueprints/root-template.md` (resolution order, root role, diagnostics table, catalog).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.73.0)): `IEventStore.RemoveNamespace` in the .NET client and a `RemoveNamespace` gRPC command remove a namespace: its observers are unsubscribed and removed (which unregisters the reminders that used to bring the namespace back at the next start), the MongoDB event store namespace database is dropped, the namespace is forgotten and a `NamespaceRemoved` event is appended to the system event sequence. The default namespace cannot be removed, removing an unknown namespace does nothing and an interrupted removal can be repeated (#4942).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.73.0)): `IEventStoreStorage.DropNamespace` lets a storage provider drop the data of a removed namespace; the SQL and in-memory providers keep their data for now.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): `Session:CookieDomain` shares the session cookie with every host under a parent domain, for example `.example.com`. Sign-out deletes it for the whole domain, and the stale host-only copy that an earlier deployment left in a browser is deleted on sign-in, on sign-out, when the cookie arrives twice and when it cannot be read. (#194)
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): `Session:CookieName` renames the session cookie, so two AuthProxy deployments under one domain do not read each other's sessions. The default stays `.Cratis.AuthProxy.Auth.v2`.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): `SignIn:CallbackHost` runs every OAuth or OIDC handshake on one host. A sign-in or sign-out that starts elsewhere is sent there and returns to the original address, so the provider needs one callback and one post-logout address. It requires `Session:CookieDomain`.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): A sign-in may return to an absolute address only when `SignIn:CallbackHost` is set, and only for an origin of a declared service host, a configured frontend, the lobby or `Logout:AllowedRedirectOrigins` that lies inside the cookie domain, on the scheme the request arrived on. Anything else is reduced to a path on the host it started on. Sign-out uses the same list.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): Aspire: `WithHosts`, `WithPathPrefix`, `WithSessionCookieDomain`, `WithSessionCookieName` and `WithSignInCallbackHost`.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): A startup warning when `Session:CookieDomain` is set while `AllowedHosts` accepts every host, and when the cookie name uses the `__Secure-` prefix.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): A how-to, [One proxy, several hosts](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/blob/main/Documentation/configuration/one-proxy-several-hosts.md), with the migration from several instances.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.196.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.196.0)): `Samples/Orders`: an application whose four screens fill the `OrdersList`, `OrderPage`, `OrdersFulfilmentBoard` and `OrdersDeliveryCalendar` templates and whose quick-create fills `OrdersQuickCreateOrder`, with the navigation declared in the layout, five specifications and a README.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.196.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.196.0)): The `orders-sample` conformance vector (golden syntax) and its entry in the sample list.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.orders`: the orders list, the order page (key facts, lines, shipments, activity), the fulfilment board, the delivery calendar (the foundation `calendarGrid`) and the new-order quick-create dialog, with navigation, thumbnails, the `orders-en` sample-data pack and the `Business / Orders` category.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): Specifications for the definition of done of every template, the screen set, the application behaviour, every forced standard state, and the Chromium audit at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes with colour contrast.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.59.0)): Docs `blueprints/orders-blueprint.md`, the table of contents and the index; the Orders package joins the browser specifications of the JavaScript build.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.72.0)): Analyzer CHR0065 warns when a read model has a method that is plainly meant to fold an event into it but will never be discovered as a self-reducer. This catches a fold whose name is not `Reduce` (for example `Reducer` or `reduce`) and a fold that is not `static`. Previously such a read model never folded and nothing said why. The diagnostic names the reason and shows the expected `Reduce` signature, and also recognizes historical event generations and event types that inherit `[EventType]`. See [CHR0065](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/code-analysis/CHR0065.mdx). (#4962)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): The Stage ships `PrimeReact` and `Cratis.Blueprint.Default` in the catalog a `ui profile` is resolved against, next to `core`, `scene.web` and `Cratis.Components`. A domain blueprint (`@cratis/scene.blueprint.crm`, `.ticketing`, `.approvals`, `.inventory`, `.hr`, `.project-tracker`, `.administration`) now only needs approving itself. The declarations are generated from the real package manifests (`shippedPackages.spec.ts` fails when `Rendering.Cratis/Shipped/ShippedPackages.json` is stale), and `core:*` component names resolve.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): A live Stage and a generated React application load the screen and dialog templates of the approved blueprints, so `template blueprint <Package> <Template>` renders the blueprint's template. A live Stage maps `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`, `@cratis/scene.engine` and `@cratis/scene.model` to its own instances, as it does for React.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): `Verification/live-blueprints` runs the Stage image against all seven published blueprints at 4.53.1 (approved set made from the published manifest as written) and asserts in a browser that every screen the blueprint's templates build mounts with content, with no load diagnostics and no sample data served. It is a new step of the `live-custom-packages` CI job.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.195.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.195.0)): `Calendar` feature under `TimeOff` in `Samples/Hr`: a `TeamCalendarEvents` read model projected from `TimeOffRequestRegistered`, a screen on the `HrTeamCalendar` template, a specification, and a *Team calendar* entry in the sidebar.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.195.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.195.0)): `Timeline` feature in `Samples/Projects`: a `ProjectTimelineItems` read model projected from `ProjectRegistered`, a screen on the `ProjectTimeline` template, a specification, and a *Timeline* entry in the sidebar.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.195.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.195.0)): The golden syntax of both samples regenerated for the conformance vectors `hr-sample` and `projects-sample`; the sample READMEs describe the new features.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): `RootTemplate(Template: TemplateReference, Options)`, `ProfileAppearance(ColorScheme?, Accent?)`, `ProfileDefaults(NewScreenTemplate?)` and the `ColorScheme` enum (`Light`, `Dark`, `System`), and the optional members `UiProfile.Root`, `.Appearance`, `.Defaults` - in C# (`Cratis.Scene.Model`) and mirrored in TypeScript (`@cratis/scene.model`). The root template reuses the existing `TemplateReference` (own or blueprint, id, blueprint, version pin).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): `UiProfileJson` (C# `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Profiles`) and `parseUiProfile`/`serializeUiProfile` (TypeScript engine): lenient reading, one canonical writing, byte-identical in both languages.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): Shared fixture corpus `ui-profile-fixtures.json` (own, blueprint + pin, options, legacy layout only, nothing new, appearance, defaults, everything, lenient enum case, non-ASCII, unknown members) run by both toolchains; `scene-model-shape.json` extended for the new types and enum.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.57.0)): Documentation: new `blueprints/root-template.md`, `layouts.md` links to it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.194.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.194.0)): `Diagnostic.Arguments` carries the subjects of a diagnostic as named values: text, keyword, index or declaration path. Each code declares its arguments, with their kinds and meanings, in the diagnostic catalog and the published contract. The names, kinds and meanings are permanent. Sixteen codes carry arguments in this release, among them PLAY0524 (an incomplete specification step), which names the specification, the step role and position, the missing property and its type, and the example or case. MCP and the `screenplay` tool include the arguments in their JSON output. See [Diagnostics](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (#804)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.124.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay models compiled to executable model version 24 (Screenplay 4.188.0), including the text validation rules `contains`, `not contains`, `starts with` and `ends with`. Generated validators, the specification runner and the host check them exactly as Screenplay does: case-sensitive, and an absent value passes. (#448)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): `ProjectTimeline` screen template and the `projects.timeline` screen in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.project-tracker`: every project as a bar in a lane for its owner with progress, two milestones and dependencies, built on the foundation `timelineGrid`; the scale is kept in the address and on a phone the schedule is the table.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): Timeline items and columns, and the day the schedule counts from, in the `project-tracker-en` sample-data pack.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.56.0)): A `Timeline` entry in the navigation, a thumbnail, and the docs in `blueprints/project-tracker-blueprint.md` (the not-in-delivery note is removed).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): `HrTeamCalendar` screen template and the `hr.timeoff.calendar` screen in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.hr`: who is away and when on a month, a week or as an agenda, built on the foundation `calendarGrid`; a filter by person or kind of leave; the people away listed beside it; one column on a phone.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): Calendar events, columns and filter fields in the `hr-nordic` sample-data pack, made from its time-off requests (a declined request is left out).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.55.0)): A `Team calendar` entry beneath Time off in the navigation, a thumbnail, and the docs in `blueprints/hr-blueprint.md` (the not-in-delivery note is removed).
- **AI** ([Release v7.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.61.0)): The `cratis/engineering` profile now tells agents to claim an issue when they start work on it: for a numbered issue in a known repository with nobody assigned, the agent assigns the current `gh` user, and it never replaces or adds to an existing assignee.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Components in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`: `calendarGrid` (month, week, agenda; keyboard; the busy day listed in full; day and view in the address), `timelineGrid` (Gantt-style lanes with progress, milestones, dependencies and conflicts told in words; a table alternative) and `inboxList` (grouped by day, unread told in words, filter and category in the address, keyboard, mark read or unread).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Foundation screen templates: `CalendarScreen`, `TimelineScreen`, `InboxScreen`, `SettingsScreen`, `ProfileScreen`, `ReportViewer` and `ImportWizard`, each with a thumbnail and preview data from the foundation sample-data pack.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): The template types `Calendar`, `Timeline`, `Inbox`, `Settings`, `Profile`, `Report` and `Import` in the type vocabulary of both the TypeScript and the C# engine, with the shared verification fixture.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Specifications: behaviour of each component, definition of done and preview of each template, and the Chromium audit of every new component at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.54.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md` and `blueprints/foundation-templates.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Application-root `command reads protected` preamble opts a model into protected command reads, bound as ESM v25. Declare it once in the root document; imported commands inherit it. See [Commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/commands.md) and [decision 0085](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0085-admit-protected-command-reads-through-an-explicit-preamble.md). (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Under the preamble, `read.property` paths qualify single-level read-model values in `require` conditions and in produced-event mappings. (#802)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): The reference evaluator rejects a command with `Validation` when a required read-model instance is absent, and with `Conflict` (`DecisionStateChanged`) when a matching fact is appended between decision and commit. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0780` to `PLAY0785` report a misplaced or duplicate preamble, a read that cannot be protected (with its closed reason), a read without a key, reads combined with `concurrency`, and unknown or ambiguous read paths. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Informational `PLAY0786` on eligible legacy models, with a typed workspace and MCP repair that previews inserting the preamble for individual review before apply. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.193.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.193.0)): Syntax, highlighting, completion and diagnostics for the preamble in the TypeScript compiler, the Monaco editor and the VS Code extension. (#129)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.192.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.192.0)): A runnable application shell vector. `ScreenCompositionCorpus.V1` publishes `screen-shell-navigation-runnable`: the shell constructs (a topbar and sidebar that inherit, extend and override per module, item targets and slots, a toolbar button that starts a State Change slice, a sample data pack the ui profile selects) with the queries, projections and screens the generated backend needs. The structure-only `screen-shell-navigation-positive` case is not a backend model - `cratis render` answers `PLAY0268` and `PLAY0269` for it - so the CLI leg reads this case; both stay in the corpus.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.192.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.192.0)): `BrowserHarness/screen-shell-cli-render.sh`, also a `shell-cli` step of `run-public-acceptance.sh`: it renders the case, from the published corpus package when `SCREENPLAY_CORPUS_PACKAGE_VERSION` is set, with the installed public `cratis` (3.67.0 or later, which bundles Stage 4.119.1) and requires the module navigation targets and the toolbar slice in `scene.json` and no sample data anywhere in the generated application.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.18.0)): `IJobs`, a service for starting, stopping and deleting jobs without routing generic job types through the shared jobs manager: `Start<TJob, TRequest>(key, [jobId,] request)` starts the job from the calling silo, so it is placed only on silos that know its type, and the job stays recoverable. See [Jobs](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md). (#76)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.18.0)): `IJobs.Stop` returns a `StopJobResult` and `IJobs.Delete` a `DeleteJobResult`: stopped or deleted, not found, `UnknownJobType` when the stored job belongs to a release this silo does not have, or refused with the job's own error. A job is never reported as stopped or deleted unless it was. (#76)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.18.0)): `IJobStorage.GetJobType(jobId)` reads a stored job's type without reading its request, in MongoDB, SQL and in-memory storage. Custom stores keep compiling through a default implementation. (#76)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): `IJobsManagerKeys`: implement it to list the job scopes and namespaces your host runs jobs in (for example event store × tenant namespaces), so recovery can find every interrupted job. Without one, recovery covers only the scopes this silo has used and logs a startup warning. See [Jobs](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/jobs.md). (#63)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): `AddCratisOrleansJobsRecovery()` on `ISiloBuilder` registers job recovery for silos not set up through `AddCratisOrleans` (such as `TestCluster` silos). (#63)
- **AI** ([Release v7.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.60.0)): A MongoDB rule (`mongodb.md`): query with `Builders<T>`, `Find` and `Aggregate` pipelines instead of `AsQueryable()`, which fails at request time with `Expression not supported` for anything the driver cannot translate. It is registered with the backend developer and code reviewer agents and the rule index.
- **AI** ([Release v7.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.60.0)): Guidance in `cratis-arc-query-paging` for paging MongoDB without LINQ: return `collection.Observe(<FilterDefinition>)` and let Arc apply the client's sort, page and size. Sorting resolves the BSON element name, so concept-typed members sort by their stored value, and ties break on `_id`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.60.0)): An eval and a trigger for a paged MongoDB query that fails with `Expression not supported`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.122.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.122.0)): Stage now accepts Screenplay models compiled to executable model version 23 (Screenplay 4.182.0) wherever an equivalent earlier model was accepted. (part of #316)
- **AI** ([Release v7.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.59.0)): A shell step in `Source/Verification/screens-smoke/screens-smoke.sh`: it installs the CLI pinned as `shellCli` (3.67.0, the first release that bundles Stage 4.119.1), renders `screens-smoke/shell/application.play` - a sidebar and topbar that inherit, extend and override per module, item targets and slots, a toolbar button that starts a State Change slice and a sample data pack - and requires the module navigation targets and the toolbar slice in `scene.json` and no sample data anywhere in the generated application.
- **AI** ([Release v7.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.59.0)): A `Shell CLI` row in the screens release vector of `Documentation/compatibility.md`; the screens authoring spec compares it with the `shellCli` pin the way it already does for the Smoke CLI.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): `GenerationStrategyCatalog` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Semantics` lists every generation strategy and what it accepts:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): the ESM kind, primitives and the version that introduced it;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): whether it is admitted or reserved, and whether it needs an implementation;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its fixed entropy;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): each option's placement, kind, limits, choices, default and conditions.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): `SemanticConceptCheck.Check` checks one concept, given as syntax or from an executable model, and returns:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its findings, each pointing at the generation, an option or a rule rather than a source location;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its rule proofs;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): its generation support and entropy;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.191.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.191.0)): the ESM version it requires.
- **cli** ([Release v3.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.68.0)): A spec evaluates every `plan.*` assertion the canonical corpus pins for its application shell vector (`screen-shell-navigation-positive`, Stage `Desktop` plan) - topbar and sidebar regions at application level and in three modules (inherit, extend, override), hierarchical items with target and slot, toolbar buttons that invoke State Change slices, blueprint template references, slice kinds, and no served sample data - against the Scene the CLI hands to its renderer for that document. A path the corpus adds is refused until the CLI understands it. (part of #367)
- **AI** ([Release v7.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.58.0)): A `cratis-screenplay-ui-composition` reference, `references/application-shell.md`, built on the canonical `screen-shell-navigation-positive` vector: how a module inherits, extends or overrides a topbar or sidebar region, item targets, slots and sub-items, why a module's own items may only target that module (`PLAY0750`) and what that means when a region switches from inherit to override, toolbar `button` lines that start a State Change slice (`PLAY0762` for anything else), blueprint references with a version range and the profile listing the blueprint, categories, and the sample data pack chosen once in the `ui profile`. Every example compiles with `--warnaserror`, and four refusal blocks assert `PLAY0750`, `PLAY0747`, `PLAY0762`, `PLAY0721` and `PLAY0722` through `expect` fences.
- **AI** ([Release v7.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.58.0)): A section in the skill with the same rules in short form and a checklist row; `cratis-screenplay-model-authoring` gains the MCP edit loop for the shell (read the typed nodes, propose, stale revision, apply, reopen) in `references/screen-authoring-workflow.md`, and the model-authoring and read-surface routing tables name the new constructs.
- **AI** ([Release v7.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.58.0)): Two evaluation tasks (module navigation modes with slice buttons; blueprint template with sample data), three trigger queries folded into the existing ones, and verification assertions for the reference.

- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `ApprovalInbox`: the requests waiting for the signed-in person as cards (the foundation list's Cards variant) with **Approve, Reject and Request changes as buttons on each card**; a decision opens `DecisionDialog`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `MyRequests` (draft, submitted, approved, rejected as chips, and a New request button), `AllRequests` and `RequestTypes` (the request types an organisation offers, for administrators).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `NewRequestWizard`: request type, details, attachments (a file uploader), then review and submit, on the foundation `WizardScreen`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `RequestPage`: summary, the **approval chain** (`approvalChainStepper`), the request's fields, attachments and the comments as the page's activity, and the decision buttons.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): `RequestTypeEditForm`, and dialogs `DecisionDialog` (a confirmation with the decision and a comment) and `RequestTypeQuickCreate`.

- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Navigation: a topbar naming the `approvals` module and the module's sidebar (Inbox, My requests, All requests, Templates), the application structure and `approvalsApplication()`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Sample-data packs `approvals-general` (en-GB, GBP) and `approvals-nordic` (nb-NO, NOK) answering the same shapes, with five request types as presets (Leave, Expense, Purchase, Travel, Invoice approval).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.0)): Specifications: definition of done per template and package, screen set consistency, applicability by level, every screen previewed in both packs with no accessibility violation, standard states forced on seven screens, the behaviors of the application (decide from the inbox, start and step through the wizard, open a request and its chain, edit a request type), and a Chromium audit of every screen and dialog at desktop, tablet and phone widths in light and dark themes (56 specs, in CI).

Every screen fills a template of the blueprint (`template blueprint Cratis.Blueprint.Approvals ApprovalInbox version "^1.0"`); the decision dialog and the wizard give their `actions` slot the command they issue; the layout declares the topbar and sidebar; the `ui profile` activates the blueprint's English pack with `active blueprint approvals-general`. Seven rejection vectors pin the diagnostics of the shell rules (`PLAY0710`, `PLAY0714`, `PLAY0721`, `PLAY0722`, `PLAY0744`, `PLAY0747`-`PLAY0749`, `PLAY0761`, `PLAY0762`) five MCP edit expectations that name the transcripts editing each construct, and a `Desktop` Stage plan pinning what the published Stage image `cratis/stage:4.119.1` serves for the document (navigation, toolbar buttons, blueprint references; no sample data).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): Revision-checked MCP transcripts for navigation regions, toolbar buttons and blueprint template references: read the typed nodes, propose, refuse a stale revision, apply, reopen. Switching a module's region mode, copying the application's items (retargeted to the module, `PLAY0750` otherwise), adding and removing sub-items, pointing a button at a State Change slice (`PLAY0762` for anything else) and changing a template version range are all ordinary `propose-ast` edits. See [Author the application shell](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/authoring-tools.md#author-the-application-shell).

- **Scene** ([Release v4.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.46.0)): Specifications for the definition of done per template and package, the screen set agreeing with the manifest, structure and navigation, applicability by level, every screen previewed with no accessibility violation, every standard state forced, the behaviors of the application, and a Chromium audit of every screen and dialog at desktop, tablet and phone widths in the light and dark themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.46.0)): Docs: `blueprints/inventory-blueprint.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.186.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.186.0)): `Samples/Crm/crm.play`: a sales team's CRM - leads, contacts, companies, deals with a pipeline and a grouped list, activities and a dashboard - in one file of 18 slices, each with a specification. Every screen fills a template of the blueprint (`template blueprint Crm CrmLeadList version "^1.0"`), the layout declares the topbar and sidebar with Deals holding the Pipeline and the List, and the `ui profile` activates the blueprint's Nordic pack with `active blueprint crm-nordic-smb`. It compiles with no error and no warning, also under `--check all`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.186.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.186.0)): The conformance manifest lists the sample (`crm-sample`) with its golden syntax, so the TypeScript and C# compilers are held to the same tree for it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.186.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.186.0)): Docs: `Samples/Crm/README.md`, the samples table and the samples rule.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): `dataList` `imageField`: each card shows the row's picture (initials when it has none), decorative because the title names the row.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): `dataList` column `kind: progress`: a 0-100 value drawn as a progress bar named by its column with the figure in words.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): `dataList` `toneField`: tints a row by its tone; pairs with a status column so colour is never the only cue.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): `orgTree` `urlKey`: the chosen node is kept in the address (a refresh restores it).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md` (Lists of records).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): `@cratis/scene.blueprint.crm`, a blueprint package (domain `crm`, category `Business / CRM`) that depends on the foundation set only through its public templates and components: eighteen screen templates, five dialog templates and eighteen ready-to-use screens.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): `CrmDashboard`: open pipeline value, won this month and conversion rate; leads by source and deals by stage as a funnel; deals closing soon; my tasks.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): `CrmLeadList`, `CrmContactList`, `CrmCompanyList`, `CrmActivityList`: name, company, owner, status and last contact; filters by owner, status, source and created date; row actions edit, assign, convert (leads) and delete; choosing a contact or company opens its page.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): `CrmDealPipeline` (a column for each stage with the stage's value in its header, a card for each deal) and `CrmDealList` (the same deals grouped by stage with subtotals).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): `CrmContactPage`, `CrmCompanyPage`, `CrmDealPage`: key facts, tabs, the record, related lists, activity and next steps.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): `Crm<Entity>CreateForm` and `Crm<Entity>EditForm` for leads, contacts, companies and deals, and `CrmQuickCreate<Entity>` dialogs for those and activities.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Each template carries its `scopes`, so a template picker offers it only where it fits and hides the blueprint where nothing applies.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Navigation of the application: a topbar naming the `crm` module and the module's sidebar (Dashboard, Leads, Contacts, Companies, Deals with Pipeline and List, Activities), the application structure, and `crmApplication()` that assembles the document.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Sample-data packs `crm-enterprise-en` (en-GB, GBP) and `crm-nordic-smb` (nb-NO, NOK) answering the same shapes; the pack's one-row `CrmSettings` shape gives every figure and date its locale and currency.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Specifications for the definition of done per template and package, the screen set agreeing with the manifest, structure and navigation, applicability by level, every screen previewed in both packs with no accessibility violation, every standard state forced on the main screens, the behaviors of the application (open a quick-create dialog, choose a row, edit, move a deal between stages), and a Chromium audit of every screen and dialog at desktop, tablet and phone widths in the light and dark themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Docs: `blueprints/crm-blueprint.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): `kanbanBoard` can total a numeric card field in each column header: name the field in `totalField` (a deal's `amount`) and `format` (`currency` with a `currency` code, `percent`, `compact` or a plain number); the totals are formatted in the element's `locale` and follow a card as it is moved, by dragging or by its Move-to menu.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md` (Boards).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.185.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.185.0)): `SemanticValidationRules.Check` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Semantics` checks a value against one rule, against every rule of a concept, or against a type, walking composites and collections. Each `SemanticRuleCheck` says whether the rule was satisfied, failed or could not be evaluated, and carries the rule, its message and, for `matches`, the pattern check. It is the same implementation the reference evaluator uses: absent values pass every rule except not-empty, text compares ordinally, numbers compare as exact decimals, and Studio text-rule patterns evaluate as their first-class rule. See [Check values against validation rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/value-rule-checks.md). (#811)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.184.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.184.0)): Concept and command rules `contains "x"`, `not contains "x"`, `starts with "x"` and `ends with "x"` on text values. They compare UTF-16 code units ordinally, an absent value passes, and they are written to the executable model in a new ESM version; models that do not use them keep their bytes and version. See [Commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/commands.md). (#810)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.184.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.184.0)): Public pattern admission checks `PortableMatchPattern.Check(pattern)` in .NET and `checkPortableMatchPattern(pattern)` in `@cratis/screenplay-compiler`. They report whether a `matches` pattern is admitted, deprecated, refused or invalid, with the diagnostic code, the construct and its offset, a suggestion, and the first-class replacement for a Studio pattern. (#810)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.119.0)): Concepts with an ESM v21 `generate pattern` (optionally with a checksum) or `generate random a..b` strategy now render: generated applications create values with Cratis.Fundamentals `GenerateValue.Pattern` and `GenerateValue.Integer`, dated patterns take their date from the generation context at run time, and Stage's executor and Host check pinned specification values against the pattern or range with the same semantics as Screenplay. (part of #414)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.107.0)): Screenplay generation recovers pattern and signed integer random strategies, checksum validation, and self-contained C# generation rules using `Now`, `Tenant`, and `GenerateValue` context overloads. (#3116)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.107.0)): Generated-value pins are checked against recovered generation contracts, while executable model version caps retain unsupported validation in source rather than silently dropping it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.118.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.118.0)): Concepts with an ESM v21 `validate checksum` rule (Luhn, Mod11 or Mod97) now render: the generated concept validator checks the checksum with Cratis.Fundamentals, and Stage's executor and Host evaluate the rule with the same semantics as Screenplay. Pinned specification values that fail the checksum are refused when the model is admitted. (part of #414)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): A command can declare a pending `handler implementation` intent with ordered `hint`s beside unconditional `produces`. The body owns only the event payload values. Which events, their order, destinations, tags, generated values, the response, authorization and validation stay declarative, and event-property mappings become non-normative hints, so required event properties may be left unmapped. See [Commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/commands.md). (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): The executable semantic model version 23 carries a described command's handler requirement (`handler { requirementId, resultVersion }`), and the requirements sidecar reports it as a Pending CommandHandler requirement with its hints and provisional/ambiguous flags. Models without a described command keep their previous version and bytes, and consumers that do not admit version 23 refuse the model rather than dropping the described logic. (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): Diagnostics PLAY0695 (a command is described but not implemented), PLAY0696 (a described command has no productions or only a response), PLAY0697 (conditional productions under a pending intent) and PLAY0698 (a pending intent next to another handler, in both compilers). (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): Reference execution reports a specification that reaches a described command's body as Unsupported (`ImplementationBody`), naming the command and its requirement, directly and through reactions. Authorization and validation outcomes are evaluated as before. (#782)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.182.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.182.0)): The MCP `handler-intents` and `implementation-requirements` views report pending requirements with their hints and flags, and whether each described command is admitted. (#782)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Read protection modes `Off`, `Measure` and `On`, set with `[assembly: ReadProtection(...)]`, `[ReadProtection(...)]` on a command or the MSBuild property `<CratisReadProtection>`. `ReadProtection:MaximumMode` lowers the mode at runtime and logs a warning at startup (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Under `On`, read-model parameters of `Handle()` and of a `CommandValidator<T>` constructor are read fresh from the event log and guard the command's append. If one changed before the commit, nothing from the command is written and the caller gets a 400 saying which read model changed and to submit again (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): `[Unprotected("why")]` marks a read-model parameter, type or method body as context that does not guard the write (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): `[LookUpBy(nameof(Command.Property))]` names the command property that is the key of a read-model parameter when it is not the event source id (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Under `On`, reads that cannot be protected are refused with a message that says why and what to do instead, including raw collection reads, filtered or constructor-dependent reducers, immediate appends and shared validator instances (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): `Measure` leaves behaviour unchanged and reports which reads `On` would guard, refuse or find changed, through startup warnings, periodic log summaries and telemetry (#3268)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Read models declared with `[EventStream<TSource>(stream)]` can be plain parameters of a command or validator: Arc reads them from the stream the command's route points to, so a monthly limit is guarded by that month only (#3306)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): Analyzers ARCCHR0024, ARCCHR0028 and ARCCHR0033 to ARCCHR0038 report reads that cannot be protected, aggregates and reads on the same source, unprotected method bodies, early aggregate commits, unproven exact scopes, read-model streams that are not on the command route and stream guards that widen (#3268) (#3306)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.106.0)): A guide to keeping command decisions correct: choosing a mechanism, getting started, adopting with Measure, protected and advisory reads, validators, refusals, how guards are derived, and scenarios from a single entity to per-stream limits and processes (#3268)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): `ArtifactImplementationGap`, `ArtifactImplementationStub`, `ArtifactImplementationContext` and `ArtifactImplementationBodyLocator` describe a gap, its requirement id, role, hints, and each typed stub with its file, signature, body location and acceptance specifications. (part of #316)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): `ArtifactRenderPlan.ImplementationGaps`, forwarded through `CratisPlanResult`. A plan with gaps includes them in its digest; a plan without gaps keeps its existing digest. (part of #316)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.0)): `ImplementationGapMode` (`Refuse` or `Stub`) on `CratisPlanOptions` and `CratisRenderingOptions`. It defaults to `Refuse` and has no effect until a target supports stubs. (part of #316)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Dialog templates in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default`, category `Foundation / Dialog`: `QuickCreateDialog` (six fields at most, Cancel and Create), `LookupDialog` (search and a list, single or multiple selection), `SideSheet` (a drawer from the inline end hosting a form or record) and `FilterDialog` (the filter builder; fills the page on a phone). `ConfirmDialog` now lists the consequences, takes a `danger` tone and names its verb on the confirming button; `FormDialog` and `DetailDialog` gain the same frame.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Auth and state screens fitting the full-page layout, categories `Foundation / Auth` and `Foundation / State`: `LoginScreen` (logo, form, identity-provider slot, live-region error area, help link), `NotFoundScreen`, `ForbiddenScreen` (binds no data, so nothing leaks) and `ErrorScreen` (retry and the support reference id).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Foundation components: `dialogFrame` (heading, message, consequences, close button; gives the host's dialog element its accessible name, focus on open, a Tab trap, Escape, focus restored on close, placement `center`/`side`/`full`, a phone filling the page, RTL mirroring), `actionButton` (permission rule enabled/disabled/hidden, behaviors, danger emphasis) and `notice` (a live-region message); a `password` field type and a `saveAndNew` switch on the form.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Cancel and Close carry a `CloseDialog` action as data, so the same document closes the dialog in Scene, Stage and the preview.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `GalleryDialogPreview`, stories for each dialog state, sample-data pack shapes for the dialogs and screens, thumbnails for every template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): `verifyTemplateDefinitionOfDone` now also takes a dialog template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-templates.md` (dialogs, auth and state screens, aliases), `foundation-components.md`, `template-set.md`, `pattern-coverage.md`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): Stage admits ESM v21 models. Concepts that use `generate pattern`, `generate random` or `generate rule` are refused with `STAGE-ESM-028`, naming the strategy, until Stage renders them. (part of #414)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): `validate checksum` rules are refused with the new code `STAGE-ESM-042` until Stage renders them. (part of #414)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Eight foundation screen templates in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` under `foundation/templates`, category `Foundation / <type>`: `WorkspaceScreen` (`moduleNav`, `moduleContent`), `DashboardScreen`, `ListScreen` (header, toolbar, filters, list, bulkBar, footer), `BoardScreen`, `MasterDetailScreen` (fits the qualified slot `WorkspaceScreen.moduleContent`, offers the `master-detail.detail` outlet), `RecordPage` (tabs, related, activity, aside), `FormScreen` and `WizardScreen`. Each fits the `AppShell` `content` slot (or its container's qualified slot), so any of them is placed in the shell without per-template shell code; each carries a compact arrangement (list to cards, master/detail to a stack, section rail to tabs), the five standard states through the foundation components, permission rules on action controls, address-reflected filters/sort/page/selection/step, and a keyboard alternative on the board.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Preview data is the sample-data pack `sampleData/foundation-en.json` (templates only declare query bindings), a thumbnail for each in `thumbnails/`, and the pack and thumbnails ship in the package.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): `verifyTemplateDefinitionOfDone` in `@cratis/scene.engine`: the Part B 6 per-template checklist (metadata, type, category, one-sentence description, thumbnail in the package, every slot arranged and none undeclared, placed by the slot resolver, no inline sample rows, every bound query answered by the pack), with specs and used for each foundation template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Gallery screens for the eight templates (breadcrumbs, nesting through the workspace for the qualified slot), the preview host takes a sample-data pack and forced state.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-templates.md`, `template-set.md` and `pattern-coverage.md` (Board, Record page and Wizard no longer deferred).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `dataList`: the records of a collection as a table, as cards, or grouped with subtotals, with search, sort, paging, selection (one or many), row actions and a filter (the filter builder's value). Search, sort, page and selection live in the address under a `urlKey` prefix, so a shared link or a refresh restores the list. Sorting cycles ascending, descending, none, and the header says so in words and in `aria-sort`. A table becomes cards on a phone, with nothing hidden.
