Real table elements: a screen reader announces row and column. Cells are text, number, currency, date (all locale-formatted) or status (tone + icon + text).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `tabSet` (the ARIA tabs pattern, the active tab in the address, the content of tab `overview` is the slot `overview`) and `subNavigation` (a module's sections as tabs or a left rail, `aria-current`, a deep link restores the section).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `bulkBar`: appears only while records are selected, says how many politely, offers bulk actions with permission rules, clears the selection.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `globalSearch` (a `search` landmark; text is the output and can live in the address; Escape clears) and `notificationsButton` (the unread count said in words, the badge hidden from assistive technology).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `kanbanBoard`: columns mapped to a status with cards that move by dragging **and** by each card's own "Move to" menu (the keyboard and screen-reader path), every move announced, per-column limits that say "Over the limit" in words, collapsible columns, read-only without the move permission; a card whose column is gone is shown in the first column rather than lost.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `wizardProgress`: linear steps, Back/Next/Finish and Save draft; Next is blocked while the step is not valid; the last step is the review; completed steps are revisitable but a step cannot be opened before those ahead are done; the current step (address) and completed steps (session) survive a refresh and a refresh cannot land past a step that was not done. Storage access is guarded, so a sandboxed frame loses only the memory (the Chromium audit caught this).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `formScreenBody`: titled sections in a 1-3 column grid with eleven field types (text, number, currency, date, select, multi-select, lookup, toggle, file, rich text, address), inline validation on blur and a summary on submit that takes focus, server errors mapped onto fields (an error for an unknown field becomes a general one, never lost), required marked by asterisk **and** word, four modes (create, edit, read-only, settings with a save per section), a sticky action bar, and an unsaved-changes guard (`beforeunload` + Discard). It collapses to one column on a phone.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Shared: `useUrlState` (state that lives in the address without
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): `cratis render` admits Screenplay ESM v20: validation rules on generated concepts render with both the concept's `New()` factory and its validator. Specifications using such a concept must pin its generated value; a pin that fails the rules is refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, and warning or information rules on generated concepts with `STAGE-ESM-038`. ESM v21 is still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`. (#372)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): When Stage's default `eventmodel` engine refuses a model only for capabilities the semantic engine runs, `cratis run` without `--engine` restarts once on `semantic` and says so. An explicit `--engine` never switches; refusal suggestions give the exact `cratis run <path> --engine semantic` command with your other options. (part of #324)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): `cratis run` refusals are grouped by kind into **Fix your model**, **Not supported by this engine yet** and **Ignored by this engine**, with counts and the first three locations; `--verbose` lists every issue, and JSON output keeps every issue plus the groups. (part of #324)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): `cratis run` shows the compiler notes Stage reports as ignored: a one-line count when the session is ready (listed with `--verbose`), grouped under **Ignored by this engine** on a refusal, and as a raw `notes` array in JSON output.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Manifest** (`ScenePackage.blueprint`, C# `ScenePackage.Blueprint`): `BlueprintMetadata` - `domain`, `description`, `thumbnail`, `screenSet` (named `BlueprintScreen`s with template, parent, order and icon, i.e. the navigation) and `defaultSampleData`. Sample-data packs stay the existing `sampleData` references. `TemplateMetadata.thumbnail` for each template. There is deliberately no second version on the blueprint: the package `version` is the blueprint's version. The shared `scene-model-shape.json` keeps C# and TypeScript in step.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **`verifyBlueprintPackage`** (C# `BlueprintVerification.Verify`), the definition of done: manifest kind and version; domain slug, description (at most 200 characters), thumbnail path and presence in the package; a non-empty screen set with unique names, titles, existing templates, parents in the set, no ancestor cycle and every screen provided; sample data present, references valid and in the package, a declared default when there are several; every template with display name, description, type from the vocabulary, a `Business / <domain>` category and a thumbnail, on top of the provenance `describeTemplateCatalog` already checks; every screen template placed by the slot resolver (unplaced, ambiguous and self-nested are reported); a dependency on the foundation set. A package with no `blueprint` block (a foundation blueprint) is held to the template, placement and sample-data rules only, or to all of them with `requireBlueprintMetadata`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Version pin semantics**: `evaluateBlueprintPin` (C# `BlueprintVersionPin.Evaluate`) decides Current / UpdateAvailable / UpdateBlockedByPin / NotSatisfied / Invalid for a reference's pinned range against the installed and latest versions; `defaultBlueprintPin` is the caret range of the installed version. `compareVersions` / `PackageVersionRange.CompareVersions` are new in both engines.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Gallery data**: `describeBlueprintCatalog` (C# `BlueprintCatalog.Describe`) and the `BlueprintCatalogFile` shape (`blueprintCatalog.json`): per domain blueprint its description, thumbnail, version, license, dependencies, screen set in navigation order, templates with type, category and thumbnail, and sample-data packs with the default marked. Every domain blueprint ships its entry; the repository root `blueprintCatalog.json` collects them.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **`blueprint-verification-fixtures.json`**, a shared corpus (27 verification cases, 5 catalog cases, 17 pin cases, 8 version comparisons) asserted by `Engine.Specs/for_Blueprints` (C#) and `engine/for_blueprints` (TypeScript), plus a specification that fails when a rule has no case.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): **Scaffold**: `yarn new-
- **Scene** ([Release v4.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.38.0)): `SceneNavigationModel.moduleOf(screen)`: the host says which module a screen belongs to. Absent, the first segment of the qualified id decides, as before.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): `approvalChainStepper`: the steps a request goes through, who decides each and where it stands. The chain applies its own rules, so a request page needs none: a rejection stops the chain and the steps after it read "Not reached"; with no step marked current the first undecided step is the current one; only one step is current. The overall outcome is stated, every step says its state in words as well as by icon and color, the current step carries `aria-current="step"`, and the list reads vertically on a phone.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): `orgTree`: an organization as an expandable tree built from flat rows (`id`, `label`, `title`, `parentId`) as a query returns them - someone whose manager is missing becomes a root and a reporting cycle is cut instead of looping. It follows the ARIA tree pattern with one tab stop and the full keyboard set, says how many reports each person has in words, and publishes the selected id as `value`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): `permissionMatrix`: roles across, permissions down (optionally grouped), a checkbox where they meet, named "Grant *permission* to *role*"; every header is a real header cell. Grants of a role or permission that does not exist are ignored; changes are published as `value`. Without the edit permission each cell says "Allowed" or "Not allowed" in words and by icon. Wide matrices scroll in a keyboard-focusable region with the permission column kept in view.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories (every state, light and dark, a rejected chain, a read-only matrix), docs, and specifications for the chain rules, the tree building and the grant handling.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): CI: the JavaScript build now installs Chromium and runs `yarn workspace @cratis/scene.blueprint.default test:browser`, which audits every foundation component in every state at desktop, tablet and phone widths in the light and dark themes with colour contrast and page overflow (Part B 6), so a regression fails the build.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `checklistSection`: sections of tasks with owner and due date, a labelled progress bar per section ("2 of 5 complete" is read, not only seen), "Overdue" said in words for an open task past its due day, ticking publishes the done task ids as `value`; read-only without the edit permission.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `activityFeed`: what happened to a record, grouped under a heading for each calendar day in a chosen time zone (newest first, or `order: ascending`), each entry naming its kind in words as well as by icon; times and days formatted for the locale. The application supplies the content; the component only lays it out.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `avatarStack`: overlapping avatars with initials or a picture, a "+N" chip announced as "N more", the stack labelled "N people".
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `fileUploader`: a drop zone and a labelled button over a real file input (keyboard and screen-reader operable), checked against `accept`, `maxSizeMb` and `multiple` before anything is sent, with the reason for each refusal announced. The selection is published as `value`. Sending goes through the host's `FileUploaderHandlerProvider` - the same `(files, serverUrl) => Promise<void>` signature the PrimeReact `fileUpload` uses, so one host function serves both - with uploading, done and failed (with retry) states in words.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories (every state, light and dark), docs, specifications for each model (reading, grouping, overdue, selection checking) and each control, and the Chromium audit on desktop, tablet and phone in both themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): `ApplicationHost.scope(navigation, children)`: what a host provides around everything that renders - the screen on the page and the dialog over it - inside the navigation providers.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): `ApplicationHost.renderDialogTemplate(template, content, navigation)`: the host draws a dialog template the document declares, in place of the plain one; called inside `scope`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): What a host draws through `renderScreen` and `renderDialogTemplate` runs as part of the application, so a hook it uses runs under the providers `scope` put in place.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `chartWrapper`: line, bar, stacked bar, donut and funnel charts drawn as scalable vector graphics from the theme tokens (no chart library, so it renders in Stage, Scene and Studio alike). The drawing is hidden from assistive technology and followed by a table of the same numbers ("Chart data"); series are told apart by marker shape, dash and hatch as well as color; the chart draws at the width it is shown at, so labels keep their size on a phone. The scale, donut and funnel arithmetic are pure functions with their own specifications.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `filterBuilder`: field/operator/value conditions joined by AND or OR in groups nested three deep, operators by field type. The filter is published as the `value` output (`{ operator, conditions, groups }`); a bound-in `filter` is read back with conditions on unknown fields dropped and operators repaired. Read-only without the edit permission.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `columnChooser`: show/hide and reorder columns with buttons (no drag-and-drop needed), locked columns, Escape closes and returns focus. Publishes the ordered visible ids as `value`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `savedViewsMenu`: the ARIA menu pattern (arrows, Home/End, Enter, Escape, focus returns to the button), the chosen view as `value`, and "Save current view" / "Manage views" on the `action` output, each with its own enabled/disabled/hidden permission rule.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Descriptors, categories, stories (every state, light and dark, every chart kind), docs in `blueprints/foundation-components.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Chromium audit extended to the new components, including every disclosure open, on desktop, tablet and phone in both themes.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Foundation components in `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` under `foundation/`: `emptyState`, `statCard`, `trendChip` and `statusChip`, registered, categorised (CMP1: Feedback / Data display), described with property descriptors (data properties accept a query result, a data context or a component output - they declare a shape, never values) and iconed.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): The five standard states of Part B 1.4 as one frame, `FoundationFrame`: a skeleton while loading (announced, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected), an explanation with the primary action when empty, a message with a retry on error, what is missing and none of the data when the user has no permission, and the loaded part with the failed regions named when partial. A `state` property forces a state for previews; otherwise the state follows `loading`, `error`, `forbidden` and `failedRegions` and whether the component has data.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Cross-cutting rules of Part B 1.5 in shared helpers: every text a component says comes from a strings table merged with the element's `strings` (localization), numbers, currency and dates are formatted with `Intl` for the element's `locale`, direction-sensitive CSS uses logical properties (right to left), `density` comfortable/compact, and every action control takes an enabled/disabled/hidden permission rule (`ActionButton`; disabled stays visible and says why).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Theme tokens `status.{success,warning,danger,info}{Color,Background}` in both shipped themes, and the foundation section of the stylesheet, built only from theme tokens and one spacing scale.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `FoundationStates` and Storybook stories showing every state in the light and dark themes (and right to left).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Specifications: every component in every state audited with axe-core (WCAG 2.2 AA), no data leaked in the no-permission state, state derivation, strings, locale, right to left, permission rules, and a stylesheet scan that fails on a hard-coded color or spacing. `yarn test:browser` runs a Chromium audit at desktop, tablet and phone widths in both themes with colour contrast enabled (it found and fixed a contrast failure on the trend comparison).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Docs: `blueprints/foundation-components.md`, status tokens in `blueprints/theme-tokens.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.0)): A model can declare `reaction batches atomic`: each trigger occurrence's direct `produces`, and each selected refusal branch's `produces`, then append all or none, so a rejected production admits none of the batch's facts, matching how Chronicle appends a reactor's returned events; invoked commands stay separate, and earlier batches and commands are kept when a later one fails (#769)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.0)): Executable model version 22 carries the declaration as `reactionProductionMode: "atomic"`; models without it keep per-fact production and their version, bytes and outcomes (#769)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.0)): `PLAY0693` reports a duplicate or conflicting `reaction batches` declaration (#769)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): `ScenePreviewHost({ document, structure, navigation, target, sampleData, registry, invoker, ... })`: a module target renders the application shell with the module's effective navigation and its first feature, a feature target that feature in the shell, a State Change slice target its task dialog over a minimal host page (the feature's own page when it has one), a State View slice target its data fragment. Navigation keeps to an in-memory history, so a preview never touches the hosting page's address, and going to another module swaps the effective navigation (`effectiveNavigation`, not re-implemented). It reports `onBreadcrumbChange` (`Sales › Orders`) and calls `onExit` on Escape when no dialog is open - the strip itself is the embedding application's.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Read-only and never blank (PRV8): `diagnosePreview` judges the model with the engine's own validators and returns `PreviewProblem` entries (`code`, `severity`, `message`, `nodeId`). Errors (`targetNotFound`, `moduleWithoutFeatures`, `sliceNotPreviewable`, `screenMissing`, `layoutMissing`, `templateMissing`, `templateUnplaced`) replace the render with a problem list; warnings (`structureInvalid`, `navigationInvalid`, `toolbarInvalid`, `sampleDataInvalid`, `sliceFragmentMissing`) are reported and the preview renders. `onProblems` and `onSelectProblem` are the host's hooks.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Dry run (PRV5): a submitted dialog validates and sends nothing; a toast says "Command would be sent" with the payload in an expandable detail. A real `invoker` can be passed instead.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Sample data (PRV6): `createPreviewSampleData` asks `resolveSampleData` - bound pack, the application profile's active pack, the blueprint's default pack, generated data - for the queries the document binds and the commands its slices accept; `sessionActivePack` is the strip's session-only override of the profile's choice. The same seed gives the same rows.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): One composition for preview and Stage (PRV9, REN2): `composeApplicationScreen` (shell, chrome, the screen inside its template chain with every arrangement applied, name resolution, effective configuration), `composeOutletScreen`, `composeDialog`, `collectDestinations`, `previewProfile`, and `ApplicationHost`, which runs it - shell, nested outlets, navigation regions, routes and dialogs. A host decides only what is its own through `CompositionOptions` and `renderScreen`. The template wrapper (`Scene:template`) and the flow arrangement view Stage kept to itself now live here, so Stage can delete its copies.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): `SliceCommandField.initialValue`: a task dialog's field can start with a value - a preview fills it from the active sample data pack.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Docs: `Documentation/blueprints/previ
- **Stage** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): `GET /stage/status` returns an optional `notes` list with the compiler's information and warning diagnostics (code, message, source and severity), so clients can show what the engine ignored without treating it as a failure. (#430)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.0)): Declare a source-keyed, implicitly passive read model with `[EventStream<TSource>(stream)]`, read its stream instance through `IReadModels`, and protect decisions within the declared route. (#4932)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.0)): CHR0064 identifies invalid stream declarations and attribute misuse, with a monthly-withdrawal guide showing guarded decisions and plain queries. (#4932)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.102.0)): The Screenplay generator now recovers per-production `for` and `stream` routes for commands that return `EventForEventSourceId` wrappers, as single results, collections, tuples, or mixed with plain events, so each production keeps its own event source and stream in the generated model instead of the command's default route. Wrappers whose destination cannot be proven (for example copies made with `with`) are withheld and keep reporting SP0051 or SP0054. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3299)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): Stage converts Screenplay's `sample data` packs and a `ui profile`'s `sample data` selection to Scene's sample data model. A pack becomes a `SampleDataPack` owned by the user, with a lower-case hyphenated id derived from its name (`NordicDemo` becomes `nordic-demo`), its title, locale and rows with the authored literals; the profile's active pack, shape mappings and read model bindings are carried as the profile's `SampleDataSelection`. `active blueprint <id>` keeps the blueprint's id as written and is resolved by the build. Sample data is design and preview time only and never reaches a production build. (#406)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): The render plan reports Scene's own sample data validation as `SampleDataInvalid` findings, for example two pack names that derive the same id or an invalid locale. See the [render plan findings](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/render-plans.md#findings).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): The Billing sample declares a pack and selects it once in the desktop profile.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Stage converts a Screenplay application's `navigation topbar|sidebar` regions to Scene's navigation model: the application's regions and logo from the layout, and each module's `inherits`/`extends`/`overrides` region, with item targets, slots, icons and sub-items. A document without regions serializes exactly as before. (part of #406)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Toolbar `button`s become toolbar items that carry the slice, emphasis and icon the Scene engine reads, with the slice written as its qualified id.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Template assignments, own and blueprint with their version range, are carried as Scene template references, and the slice kinds of a `scopes slice` template travel in its metadata.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): The render plan reports Scene's own structure, navigation and toolbar validation as findings - `StructureInvalid`, `NavigationInvalid` and `ToolbarButtonInvalid` - for example a navigation target that does not exist, or a toolbar button that names a State View slice. See the [render plan findings](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/render-plans.md#findings).
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): Guidance for decision reads: `Chronicle.decide/2`, `DecisionReads.get/2`, `get_detached/2` and `admit/2`, the `DecisionConflict` retry, and which read model shapes cannot be guarded.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): Guidance for `ConcurrencyScope` builders, `at_tail/2`, `expecting_no_matching_event/1` and the `concurrency: [default: :optimistic]` client option.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): Guidance for `Chronicle.Testing` (`Case`, assertions, `wait_for_observers/1`, `Integration.setup_isolated_client/2`) and what the in-memory kernel does not model.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): Guidance for captures, standalone constraint modules, `remove_constraint/2` and `ConstraintViolation.details/1`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): Guidance for the discovery report, `:drift_policy`, id pinning, `mix chronicle.discover`, `generation_for:`, `strict_validation` and pinned generation delivery.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): Guidance for `children` collections (one-line form, `removed_with_join/2`, `clear_with/2`, unique nested ids), read model `subscribe/2` and `observe_stream/2`, `Chronicle.Projections` `query/2`, `preview/2` and `wait_for/3`, `Chronicle.Patterns`, prepared events, and OpenTelemetry and `:telemetry` events.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): An evaluation task for decision reads and a trigger query for in-memory testing.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): `GenerateValue.Pattern(pattern, checksum)` and `GenerateValue.Pattern(pattern, context, checksum)` generate a value from a pattern such as `ORD-[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{4}-#{4,6}` or `INV-{yyyy}-#{7}`. Every distinct value the pattern allows is equally likely, date segments come from `context.Now` in UTC, and an optional `Checksum` (`Luhn`, `Mod11`, `Mod97`) appends check digits; `Mod11` never produces the excluded check value 10. See [Patterns](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/blob/main/Documentation/csharp/generating-values/patterns.md). (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): `GenerateValue.Integer(minimum, maximum)` draws an unbiased number from an inclusive `long` range, including the full `long` range. (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.0)): `GenerateValue.HasValidChecksum(value, checksum)` checks that a value ends with the correct Luhn, mod 11 or ISO 7064 MOD 97-10 check digits. (part of #1176)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.3)): The Arc command skill teaches `ICommandExceptionMapper` for turning an integration's exceptions into a `CommandResult`, including declining, rollback and correlation behavior. (#710)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Top-level `sample data <Name>` block: optional `title`, optional BCP 47 `locale`, and `shape <ReadModel>` blocks of `row` blocks with `<field> = <literal>` lines (string, number, `true`, `false`, `null`). Design and preview time only: binding reports it as deferred (`PLAY0269`), it adds nothing to the executable model or ESM output and is never part of a production build.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): The `ui profile` gains an optional `sample data` block: `active <Pack>` or `active blueprint <id>`, the rare `shape <Shape> maps to <ReadModel>` and `bind <ReadModel> to <Pack>`. The active pack is chosen once, there, for the whole application; there is no per-screen, per-slice or per-component selector (decision 0081, which supersedes the spec's per-screen placement).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0702`-`PLAY0715`: invalid or duplicate pack, repeated title, locale, shape, field or selection line, a shape naming no read model (warning), a field that is not a property of the read model, a literal that does not fit the property type, an active, mapped or bound pack or read model the document does not declare.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): The canonical printer, the syntax walker and index, the folder merge, the typed JSON schema, the contract catalogue (`sample` construct, admission probe), the MCP `read-ast`/`propose-ast` typed nodes (`SampleDataPackSyntax`, `SampleDataSelectionSyntax`, `SampleDataSetSyntax`, `SampleDataRowSyntax`, `SampleDataFieldSyntax`, ...) and the canonical screen composition corpus all carry sample data.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Monaco (highlighting, hover, completion in the root, the pack, its shapes and the profile block) and the VS Code grammar and spelling dictionary know the new words; the TypeScript compiler skips the block whole.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.0)): Documentation page `sample-data.md` (grammar, `ui-profile.md`, `diagnostics.md`), the Invoicing sample and decision 0081.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Toolbar buttons that invoke State Change slices. A screen `toolbar` accepts `button <id> title "<text>" [slice <Module>.<Feature>.<Slice>] [emphasis primary|secondary|quiet] [icon <name>]` beside its `item` entries - the Screenplay form of Scene's `ToolbarButton`. The slice names the command and the dialog that opens over the page, so the button needs no command of its own. `slice` is a dotted path or any unambiguous suffix; the title is a quoted string or a `$strings.<key>` token. See [Toolbar buttons](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screens.md#toolbar-buttons).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0760`-`PLAY0765`: a malformed button line, a slice the document does not declare, a slice that is not a State Change slice (a button invokes State Change slices only), a button without a slice (warning), an ambiguous slice reference, and a duplicate id. They mirror Scene's `toolbarButtonSliceNotFound`, `toolbarButtonSliceNotStateChange` and `toolbarButtonSliceEmpty`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): The syntax tree carries `ToolbarButtonSyntax` on `ScreenToolbarSyntax.Buttons`; it is omitted from the JSON when empty, so existing documents keep their bytes. The printer round-trips every button, and the TypeScript compiler reads buttons into the same tree with a shared conformance vector (`toolbar-buttons`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Monaco and VS Code highlight, document and complete buttons; the Invoicing sample uses them.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): `reactors.StartAtTail()` option. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it, and an explicit replay still replays from the beginning. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/blob/main/Documentation/reactors.md#skipping-history-on-first-registration. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): Generated contracts for the new Chronicle `occurrences` service.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): `@StartAtTail` annotation for reactor classes. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/blob/main/Documentation/reference/annotations.md#startattail. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.53.0)): `@startAtTail()` class decorator for reactors. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/blob/main/Documentation/reactors.md#start-at-the-tail. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): `start_at_tail: true` option on `use Chronicle.Reactors.Reactor`. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/reactors.md#starting-at-the-tail. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Navigation regions in the language. A `layout` declares the application's `navigation topbar` and `navigation sidebar` with an optional `logo image "<path>" title "<text>"` and `item <id> title "<text>" [icon <name>] [target <Module>[.<Feature>]] [slot <name>]` lines; sidebar items nest sub-items. A `module` declares only what it changes: `navigation sidebar extends`, `navigation topbar overrides`, or `inherits` (the default when absent). The blocks are the Screenplay form of Scene's `NavigationModel`; `contribute to Navigation` stays valid and independent. See the new [Navigation regions](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/navigation.md) page.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Diagnostics `PLAY0740`-`PLAY0752` for malformed blocks and items, duplicate regions, application-only logos, modes where they are not allowed, topbar sub-items, and the findings that mirror Scene's `navDuplicateId`, `navTargetMissing`, `navSlotUnknown`, `navTargetOutOfScope` and `navModuleWithoutFeatures`, plus a warning for an override without items.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): The syntax tree carries `NavigationRegionSyntax`, `NavigationItemSyntax`, `NavigationTargetSyntax` and `NavigationLogoSyntax` on `LayoutSyntax.Navigation` and `ModuleSyntax.Navigation`. They are omitted from the JSON when empty, so existing documents keep their bytes. The printer round-trips every item, id, icon, slot, target and mode; the executable model reports the blocks as deferred (`PLAY0269`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Monaco and VS Code highlight, document and complete the new words and blocks; the Invoicing sample uses them.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): `template blueprint <Blueprint> <Name> [version "<range>"]` references a template a blueprint package ships, beside the own `template <Name>`. It is valid wherever `template` is: as a module, feature or slice assignment and inside a screen. The optional `version` is the semver range of the blueprint the author accepts - Scene's subset (`*`, an exact version, `^`, `~`, `>=`, `>`, `<=`, `<`), the same one Scene's `TemplateReference.version` and `resolveTemplateReference` use. `PLAY0721` reports a range Scene cannot read; `PLAY0722` reports a blueprint no `ui profile` lists under `packages` (checked only when a profile declares packages). Version drift and unknown blueprint templates are reported where the blueprint is installed (Scene `TemplateReferenceResolver`), because the compiler cannot see a package's catalog.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): `scopes slice statechange`, `scopes slice stateview` and `scopes slice statechange|stateview` narrow a slice-scoped template to slice kinds - Scene's `TemplateMetadata.sliceKinds`. Using a restricted template on an excluded slice kind, or on an automation or translate slice (which present nothing), is `PLAY0631`. The conformance vector `when_checking_template_applicability_against_the_scene_vectors` restates the cases of Scene's shared `template-applicability-fixtures.json` (`ApplicableTemplates`) as Screenplay source.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): `PLAY0720` (warning): a `layout`, `screen template` or `dialog template` without a `category`. A blueprint package build treats warnings as errors, so there it fails; an own template only warns. (#761)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): The new members round-trip through the C# and TypeScript compilers, the printer (including comments on `category`), the syntax schema and the MCP contract; keywords, hover documentation and diagnostics reach Monaco and the VS Code grammar; the TypeScript compiler and C# are held to the same `template-references` conformance vector. (#761)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.70.0)): `[StartAtTail]` attribute for .NET reactors. The first time Chronicle sees the reactor, it begins at the tail of the event sequence and observes only what is appended afterwards. A reactor that already has a position keeps it, so the attribute is safe to leave in place. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/reactors/start-at-tail.mdx. (part of Cratis/Direct#1875)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.70.0)): Optional `StartAtTail` field on the reactor definition in the gRPC contract. Clients that do not send it behave as before.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): Stage admits ESM v20 models: a concept that declares `generate` may carry validation rules, and the rendered concept has both its `New()` factory and its `ConceptValidator`. ESM v21 is still refused. (#393)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): A rendered specification that uses a rule-bearing generated concept must pin its generated value, and a pinned value that fails the concept's rules is refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (#393)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): A generated concept rule with warning or information severity is refused with `STAGE-ESM-038`, because the rendered application only rejects error-severity failures. (#393)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SliceInvoker` (`invoke(sliceId, payload, command)` returning an `InvocationResult` with `succeeded`, `validationErrors`, `message`, `payload`, `dryRun`), `SliceInvokerProvider` and `useSliceInvoker`. Without a provider the command is announced as `cratis.scene.command`, as a toolbar action always was (`createAnnouncingInvoker`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `createDryRunInvoker({ onInvoked, validate })`: validates, sends nothing and returns a result carrying the would-be payload, for the Studio preview's toast (PRV5).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SliceDialogView` and the `core:sliceDialog` component: a modal card with the button title, the subtitle `State Change · <Slice>`, a labelled field per command property, `Cancel` and a submit button labelled with the title. The first field takes focus, Tab stays inside, Escape cancels and focus returns to the button. Required fields are checked before invoking, and the validation errors an invoker reports are shown against their fields (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`) or for the whole form. Success closes the dialog and signals the result.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SliceCommandFieldsProvider`: the host tells the dialog a command's fields, so `@cratis/scene.react` stays free of Arc and transport imports.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `core:toolbarItem` resolves its slice against the navigation model, opens the dialog `sliceDialogFor` picked through the navigation host (`sliceDialogDestination`; an authored `dialogs` template wins over the built-in dialog), carries its `emphasis` as `data-scene-emphasis` and `scene-toolbar-button-<emphasis>`, and shows a warning badge for a button without a slice only when the host asks (`SliceInvokerProvider warnEmptySlices`, implied by a dry-run invoker). Without a navigation model it announces the command as before.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SceneNavigationHost` carries the dialog's parameters (`currentDialogParameters`) and names the dialog after its title.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `blueprint.default` styles the primary, secondary and quiet buttons, the warning badge and the dialog over its scrim, and has a Storybook story.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): Docs: "Running a slice button: the task dialog and the `sliceInvoker`" in `blueprints/application-hierarchy.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `NavigationRegionView` in `@cratis/scene.react`, driven by `effectiveNavigation(...)` for the module of the screen on the page, so a module swap recomputes the items in place without remounting the `nav`. A sidebar nests, collapses and expands the parents of the active item (`expand='all'` shows everything); a topbar is flat.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Active state follows the navigation host: an item is active (`aria-current`, `data-active`) when the screen it opens is shown in the slot it renders into, so items rendering into different slots are active at once.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Render-into-slot: activating an item calls `navigate` with `navigationItemDestination(item)` (module/feature identity, screen, slot as `outlet`). `navigationDestinations(...)` lists every destination so deep links resolve.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `SceneNavigationHost` takes `navigationModel` (structure, navigation, `screenFor`, `isVisible`) and a `frame` that wraps the primary outlet in chrome that stays mounted across navigation; `SceneNavigationModelProvider` does the same without a host.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Hooks for the layers around it: `isVisible(item)` hides items (never disables), items carry `data-origin` / `data-read-only`, and `renderAdornment(item)` lets Studio draw the lock of an inherited item. No design chrome is rendered.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Keyboard: roving tab stop, arrows (left/right in a topbar, up/down in a sidebar), Home/End, and right/left to expand/collapse a sidebar parent.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `blueprint.default` `Topbar` and `Sidebar` show the region as the shell menu (the `layout-menu` classes, with the active underline and pill of the specification) when a navigation model is in scope and the element has no `menu` / `content` slot of its own; `regionNavigation: false` opts out. A Storybook story shows the hierarchical sidebar and two slots.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Docs: "Rendering the regions" in `blueprints/navigation-regions.md`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): Warning PLAY0694 when a Translate slice without `direction` has a reaction (without a `from` route) to an event that a capture in the same slice appends, including appends in capture children and nested scopes. The message names the events and tells you to keep the capture in the Translate slice and move the reaction to an Automation slice; there is no automatic repair. Ordinary compilation is unaffected, but builds that treat warnings as errors (for example `--warnaserror`) fail on such models until the slice is split. Both compilers and the editors report it. See [decision 0079](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0079-warn-when-a-directionless-translate-slice-reacts-to-its-own-capture.md) and [PLAY0694](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/diagnostics.md). (#760)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): Kernel occurrence queue: triggers register with a JSON payload schema, `RecordOccurrence` stores each occurrence once per idempotency id (`Recorded`, `AlreadyRecorded`, `OccurrenceIdConflict`, `OccurrencePayloadInvalid`), and workers claim, renew, complete and fail occurrences under leases fenced by an epoch. A superseded worker gets `OccurrenceLeaseLost`; a repeated completion gets `AlreadyCompleted`. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): Failed occurrences back off and are quarantined after the configured maximum attempts without blocking later occurrences; `RetryQuarantinedOccurrence` re-arms one and records the authenticated principal. Retry settings live in the new `Occurrences` configuration section, documented at https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/configuration/chronicle-options.mdx. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): A disconnected client's leases expire, and occurrence queues resume after a kernel restart. `GetOccurrence` and `ObserveOccurrences` report state, attempts, lease, last error, next attempt and the receipts written for an occurrence, with their namespace. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): Effect receipts: every append command (`Append`, `AppendMany`, `AppendManyForEventSources` and their named-tag variants) accepts an optional effect receipt that is written in the same storage transaction as the events. A repeated effect returns `EffectAlreadyRecorded` with the stored outcome and response and appends nothing. A zero-event append with concurrency scopes records a `NoEvents` receipt only when the scopes hold. `RecordEffectReceipt` records a `NoEvents` or `Refused` outcome on its own, and `GetEffectReceipt` reads one back. An unknown commit result is settled by lookup, or fails retryably with `EffectReceiptUnsettled`. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/concepts/effect-receipts.md. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): The kernel advertises the `occurrences` and `effect-receipts` capabilities, and the new `Occurrences` gRPC service is part of the generated Kotlin, TypeScript and Elixir contracts. (part of #4911)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `ToolbarButton` (`id`, `title`, `slice`, `emphasis`, `icon`, `target`), `Toolbar` and `ToolbarEmphasis` (`primary`, `secondary`, `quiet`) in `Cratis.Scene.Model.Toolbar` and `@cratis/scene.model`, mirrored in `scene-model-shape.json`. A spec button is a `core:toolbarItem` with `kind: Action` plus `slice` and `emphasis`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `validateToolbarButtons` / `ToolbarButtonValidation.Validate` with `toolbarButtonSliceNotFound` and `toolbarButtonSliceNotStateChange` (errors) and `toolbarButtonSliceEmpty` (warning for a button without a slice).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `sliceDialogFor` / `SliceDialogs.For` and `resolveToolbarButton` / `ToolbarButtons.Resolve`: a slice-backed button resolves to its command and task dialog - the slice's own `dialogTemplate`, else the default `FormDialog` - so the dialog is decided once, in Scene.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `toolbarButtonFromItem` / `ToolbarButtons.FromItem` read a button from a `core:toolbarItem`'s properties, and `normalizeToolbarButton` converts a hand-authored `kind: Action` + `target` command to the one state change slice that accepts it. A command no single slice accepts stays a legacy button and still validates.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `SliceNode.command` and `SliceNode.dialogTemplate`, and `ApplicationStructureIndex.findSlicesByCommand`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The `core:toolbarItem` descriptor: title, `slice` (editor `slice`, kinds `StateChange`), `emphasis` (enum chips), `icon` and the legacy `target`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `toolbar-slice-fixtures.json` (state change ok, state view and automation rejected, missing slice, empty slice warning, dialog template resolution, conversion from a `target` command, reading buttons from items) asserted by both `Engine.Specs/for_ToolbarButtonValidation` and `engine/for_validateToolbarButtons`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The toolbar section of `blueprints/application-hierarchy.md` documents the button, validation, resolution and compatibility.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.174.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.174.0)): `RuleConsistency.Check(SemanticConcept concept, int? utcAssessmentYear = null)` returns the compiler's proof status for each validation rule of a generated concept, in rule order, from a deserialized executable model. A renderer can let a concept whose rules are all Proven use unpinned generation and keep requiring pinned fixtures only for Unproven rules. The status is deliberately not serialized: it is derived from the concept and the UTC assessment year. See [proof status for renderers](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/generated-values/strategies.md#proof-status-for-renderers). (#775)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `NavigationModel`, `ApplicationNavigation`, `ModuleNavigation`, `NavigationRegion` (`topbar`/`sidebar`, `inherit`/`extend`/`override`), `NavItem` (`id`, `title`, `icon`, `target`, `slot`, `children`) and `ApplicationLogo` in `Cratis.Scene.Model.Navigation` and `@cratis/scene.model`, mirrored in `scene-model-shape.json`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `effectiveNavigation` / `EffectiveNavigation.Resolve`: the ordered items of a region for the application or a module, each with `origin` (application | module), `readOnly`, a `reason` and its target resolved against the application structure (a module target goes to the module's first feature), plus the region's effective `mode` and `sources` tag (`defined here`, `inherited`, `Application + Sales`, `Sales only`). Sub-items are sidebar-only.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): Edit helpers `switchRegionMode` (inherit clears the module items; inherit → override with `copy` copies the application items first), `addNavItem` (adding to an inheriting region switches it to extend first; sub-items under a parent) and `setApplicationLogo` (refused for a module), with `applyNavigationEdits`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `diagnoseNavigationRegions` and the new diagnostic codes `navTargetMissing`, `navSlotUnknown`, `navDuplicateId`, `navModuleWithoutFeatures` and `navTargetOutOfScope`; `diagnoseNavigation` runs them when its graph carries `structure` and `navigation`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): The `replace` collection operation and `InstanceContribution.replaceOwnerItems`: an instance can hide the items it inherits so only its own remain. It is how `override` is expressed in the exposure model. Without `replace` exposed the contribution is ignored and reported, and the server check refuses a document that sets it.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): Shared fixtures `navigation-inheritance-fixtures.json` and `navigation-targets-fixtures.json`, the new codes in `navigation-diagnostic-fixtures.json` and two server cases in `document-change-fixtures.json`, asserted by both the C# and the TypeScript engines.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): The documentation page `blueprints/navigation-regions.md`; the exposure and navigation diagnostics pages are extended.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Sample-data model in C# and TypeScript (and `scene-model-shape.json`): `SampleDataPack`, `SampleShape`, `SampleShapeField`, `SampleFieldKind`, `SampleDataSelection` and `ScenePackage.sampleData` (a Blueprint package ships its packs as `sampleData/*.json`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `UiProfile.sampleData` selects the active pack once for the whole application (own or from a blueprint), plus the rare shape-to-read-model mappings and per-read-model bindings. There is no per-screen, slice or component selector; the preview strip's choice is the session-only `sessionActivePack` of the resolve context.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `resolveSampleData` / `SampleDataResolver.Resolve` implement the resolution order bound pack, UI profile active pack (or session override), blueprint default pack, generated data; nothing resolves unless `isDesignOrPreview` is true, so a Stage production build gets no sample data.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `generateSample` / `SampleDataGenerator.Generate`: a seeded xoshiro128** generator typed by field kind (ids, names, companies, emails, phones, cities, text, numbers, amounts, booleans, dates, enums), honouring nullability, `min`/`max` and pack locale (`en-GB`, `en-US`, `nb-NO`, `sv-SE`, `de-DE`), byte-identical in both engines for a seed.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `validateSampleDataPack`, `validateSampleDataSelection`, `validateSampleDataPackages` and `describeSampleDataCatalog` (C#: `SampleDataValidation`, `SampleDataCatalogs`) check what blueprint packages ship and build the selector content (generated, own, then each blueprint's group).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `findDescriptorSampleRowLiterals` / `findTemplateSampleRowLiterals` detect row literals in component descriptors and templates; `verify-package-contents.js` fails when sample data is packed outside `sampleData/`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `sample-data-resolution-fixtures.json`, asserted independently by `Engine.Specs/for_SampleData` and `engine/for_sampleData`, and the documentation page `blueprints/sample-data.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `ApplicableTemplates.Compute` / `applicableTemplates(library, target)`: own templates by category, then only the blueprints that have at least one applicable template (TPL2). A target is a scope, an optional slice kind, the container whose slots the template must fit, and the chain of templates already chosen above (no loops, TPL6). It composes `TemplateApplicability` and `ScreenTemplateResolver` with the per-level defaults of spec 4.2; explicit `scopes`/`sliceKinds` override and category never implies a scope. `ApplicableTemplates.Validate` / `validate*ForTarget` give the reason a template does not apply.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `TemplateReference` (`source` Own | Blueprint, `id`, `blueprint`, `version` semver range, default `^<installed>`) and `TemplateReferenceResolver.Resolve` / `resolveTemplateReference` with `Resolved`, `Malformed`, `UnknownBlueprint`, `VersionNotSatisfied` (drift) and `UnknownTemplate`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `TemplateMetadata.sliceKinds` (reusing `SliceKind` from the application structure) narrows which slice kinds a template applies to.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): Catalog entries carry `warnings`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): `ComponentDescriptor` gains `category`, `icon` (an `IconReference`), `badge`, `keywords` and `designMode`. `ComponentCategory` holds the eight categories of the spec (Navigation, Data display, Input, Actions, Layout, Feedback, Overlay, Forms); a package may add its own.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): `ComponentDesignModeDescriptor` declares, as data, where a component's items are (`itemsPath`, a `Collection` property), the "add" affordances and the floating item toolbar. `availability` and `readOnlyReason` are rule names, never functions; every edit is an ordinary collection edit, so undo/redo and the dirty state need nothing of their own. `navigationDesignMode()` and `toolbarDesignMode()` build the actions of spec DM3.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): The property-editor vocabulary (`PropertyEditorKind`: `text`, `number`, `boolean`, `enum`, `select`, `color`, `icon`, `list`, `reference`, `navigationTarget`, `renderingSlot`, `slice`, `inheritanceMode`, `template`, `sampleData`, `field`, `columnList`, plus the structured kinds that already existed) and `PropertyDescriptor.editorOptions` (`multiline`, `placeholder`, `unit`, `step`, `kinds`, `reference` filter, `fieldKinds`, `tokensOnly`).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): Engine rules shared by every host: `validateComponentDescriptors` (`componentCategoryMissing` error, `componentCategoryUnknown` warning, `componentDesignModeInvalid`, `propertyEditorOptionsInvalid`), `matchComponentQuery` (the picker filter: name/keyword substring, category by word start, highlight ranges, keyword hint), `rankFrequentlyUsedComponents` (top 6, count then recency) and `componentBadge`. All are asserted against the shared corpus `component-metadata-fixtures.json`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): Every component of the core, Cratis.Components, PrimeReact and both blueprint packages (164 components) now has a descriptor with category, icon, keywords and display name; Topbar, Sidebar, Toolbar and the navigation bar declare a design mode. `validateComponentMetadata(bundle)` checks a bundle (CMP1) and is run by a specification in each package and by a repository-level specification (`registry.conformance`) that enumerates all package registries.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): The third-party fixture `Acme.Inspections` declares a category, icon, keywords and design mode using only public packages; documentation in `Documentation/editing/descriptors.md` and `design-time-extensions.md`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `ApplicationStructure`, `ModuleNode`, `FeatureNode`, `SliceNode` and `SliceKind` (`StateChange`, `StateView`, `Automation`) in `Cratis.Scene.Model.Structure` and `@cratis/scene.model`, mirrored in `scene-model-shape.json`. The structure is a reference input supplied by the host (Stage's converter, Studio); it is not persisted in a scene document.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `ApplicationStructureIndex` in `Cratis.Scene.Engine.Structure` and `engine/structure`: `findModule`, `findFeature`, `firstFeature`/`firstFeatureId`, `findSlice`, `kindOf`, `findFeaturesByName` and the diagnostics `structureDuplicateId` and `structureAmbiguousName`. Pure, no DOM.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `application-structure-fixtures.json` (qualified lookup, nested features, first feature, module without features, unknown/wrongly typed ids, same-named features in different modules, duplicate ids, ambiguous names) asserted by both `Engine.Specs/for_ApplicationStructureIndex` and `engine/for_applicationStructureIndex`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): The documentation page `blueprints/application-structure.md` explains the input and who supplies it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold emits a `<Project>.Specs` project next to the web project. It compiles the generated slice specifications in place, so slice files keep their paths, and runs them with `dotnet test`. (part of #401)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold enables central package management: `Directory.Packages.props` pins every package version and the projects reference packages without versions. (part of #401)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold's `Directory.Build.props` sets nullable reference types, the latest C# version, warnings as errors and code-style enforcement for every project, and an `.editorconfig` that allows specification-style names in specifications. NuGet's machine-dependent restore warnings (`NU1507`, `NU1900`–`NU1904`) stay warnings. (part of #401)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): The scaffold adds a `README.md` with run instructions for Chronicle, the backend, the frontend and the specifications. (part of #401)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): Read protection for units of work: an enforcing unit owned by one invocation, protected decision reads that enrol before they return (`IDecisionReads.GetFor`), guards on every public read and append entry point, owner-only completion, read allowances for advisory reads, and the append conditions a commit sends (`GetAppendConditions`) (#4888)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): Protected decision reads of reducers and self-reducing read models, folding with the handler's declared event generation; filtered reducers and reducers with constructor dependencies are refused (#4888)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): `CHR0062` reports an event source definition or stream whose concurrency leaves out `EventSourceId`, with a code fix (#4888)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): `CHR0063` reports an explicit concurrency scope for one stream passed to an append routed to another stream of the same event source (#4899)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): A documentation page on read protection, and decision read documentation for reducers, pinned delivery and detached reads under read protection (#4888)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): The Screenplay generator recovers a generated concept's validator rules together with its built-in generation strategy, using Screenplay ESM v20. Generated values, identities, responses, mappings and pins are no longer withheld just because the concept has a `ConceptValidator`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Rules the compiler proves against the strategy are emitted.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Rules it cannot decide are kept and reported as `SP0078`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): A refuted rule withholds that concept's generation and is reported as `SP0073` with a counterexample.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Below an ESM v20 version cap, the generation is withheld but the rules are kept.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Concepts can generate values from a `pattern` with digits, letters, ASCII classes, bounded repeats and UTC date segments (`{yyyy}`, `{yy}`, `{MM}`, `{dd}`), optionally followed by a `checksum luhn`, `mod11` or `mod97` check digit; generation draws uniformly from the exact set of values the pattern and checksum can produce, and a pattern whose set is empty for any date in the next hundred years, or too large to count, is a binding error (`PLAY0692`, `PLAY0691`) (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Int concepts can generate a `random <min>..<max>` value, and `generate rule Name` names a value generator implemented in code, which receives the current time and tenant (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): `validate checksum luhn|mod11|mod97` checks a String concept's check digits, and is proven to hold for values the matching `checksum` strategy generates (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Rules on concepts with these strategies are proven like the other strategies' rules, including length and `min`/`max` against `random`, and specification fixtures are checked against the pattern, its date segments (using `given clock`) and the checksum (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): Executable model version 21 carries these strategies and checksum validations; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (part of #616)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.1)): The agent worker result contract is published as a JSON Schema, a sample payload and a checker under [Source/AgentHarnesses/contracts](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/tree/main/Source/AgentHarnesses/contracts), so the harness and Studio test against the same artifact. (#711)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): `cratis screenplay validate` reads the validation gate a repository declares in `screenplay.json` at the model root: the `checks`, `warningsAsErrors`, `scope` and `openQuestions` the standalone `screenplay` tool also reads, through the same Screenplay library. A command-line option overrides only its own setting, and a missing file keeps today's defaults. An invalid or unreadable file is refused with its location and exit 1 before anything compiles. See [the screenplay.json reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md). (#347)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): `cratis screenplay validate --print-gate` prints the effective gate and where each value came from without compiling, `--no-gate-file` ignores the file for one run, and the JSON result carries a `gate` object in the same shape as the standalone tool's. (#347)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): `cratis render` accepts Screenplay ESM v18 models: a specification that queries with an out-of-rule key or filter value and expects `then error` renders as a specification asserting the query's validation failure. Shapes Stage cannot reproduce exactly are refused with Stage diagnostics; ESM v20 models are still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.171.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.171.0)): A specification drives a capture over events by presenting the consumed event itself: `given <Event>` and `when append <Event>` for an event a capture reads `from`. The capture keeps one state per key, folding earlier events in order, and appends what its rules map: the first event for a key counts as added, an event that changes nothing appends nothing, and `when <field>` and transitions hold only on a change. `for` on such a fixture is optional. See [capturing from public events](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/captures.md) and decision [0077](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0077-present-consumed-public-events-to-captures-over-events.md). (#483)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Stage admits Screenplay ESM v18. A specification that queries with an out-of-rule key or filter value and expects `then error` renders as a companion specification asserting the query's validation failure, and Stage's semantic runs and the Stage Host reproduce the reference's `Validation` rejection. (#370)
- **cli** ([Release v3.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.64.0)): `cratis run --engine <eventmodel|semantic>` starts the Stage sandbox on the chosen runtime engine. `semantic` runs the Screenplay executable model, enforcing validation, requirements, authorization, conditional production and constraints, and running models with named event sources and streams. Without the option, Stage's default engine runs as before. (#368)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Kernel storage for application occurrences in MongoDB, SQL and InMemory. A replayed ingress id is recognized, and a mismatched payload is refused. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Kernel storage for fenced occurrence leases. A worker whose lease epoch has been superseded can no longer renew, complete or fail the occurrence. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Kernel storage for schedule definitions and cursors. The start time is written together with the first registration, and each cursor moves forward in versioned, atomic turns. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Effect receipts stored in the same transaction as the events they cover, in every provider, including receipts for batches without events. (part of #4911)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): SQL migrations that create the occurrence, schedule and receipt tables when the kernel upgrades. (part of #4911)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.0)): The Stage sandbox skill describes an optional behavior walk after V3: a domain expert walks each persona's commands and screens on Stage's semantic engine and records what changes, producing findings for the model owner (actions that change nothing visible, screens nothing feeds, work queues that never empty, refusals the model lacks) without changing code or the model. (#682)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.0)): The legacy-extraction skill explains when Arc's Screenplay generator states literal-row specifications as one case table and why it otherwise reports `SP0076`. Requires Arc.Screenplay 22.99.0. (#707)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.0)): The fresh-identifier guard now also covers events written directly through the command's unit of work: `IUnitOfWork.AddEvent`, `AddEventWithNamedTags` and `AddEvents`, and `eventLog.Transactional.Append`, `AppendMany` and their named-tag variants. It covers nested commands too. Writing to an occupied generated identifier fails with `ConcurrencyViolation` and `GeneratedIdentifierCollision`, and the whole unit of work is rolled back. See [Fresh identifiers](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/fresh-identifiers.md). (#3245)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.0)): `ICommandExceptionMapper` lets an integration turn an exception thrown while a command runs into a `CommandResult`. Mappers are discovered by convention. (#3245)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.105.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.105.0)): A slice that declares screens now gets its own `Screens.scene.json` in its folder, listing that slice as its source, so a consumer such as Studio's slice Preview can show a slice's screens. The application's `scene.json` is assembled from the same fragments and stays byte-identical. Slice- and feature-scoped plans emit the selected slices' fragments, byte-identical to an application plan, and leave out the application-wide Scene files with an informational diagnostic, so a scoped write never leaves them partial. Screen ownership supplied by a caller is checked against the compiled model: an owner that doesn't declare the screen, or a stale screen payload, fails planning. (#400)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.66.0)): Event store subscriptions can now opt in to forward every event into the target inbox in the generation the target application declared. This applies to live forwarding, catch-up, replay and partition retries. Opt in with `WithGenerationDelivery(EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned)` on the .NET subscription builder; typed registrations send their declared generation, and `WithEventType(eventTypeId, generation)` names one explicitly. The kernel stores those generations with the subscription and selects, decrypts and, when needed, migrates each event to that generation before appending it to the inbox. Subscriptions that do not opt in are unchanged. See [explicit subscriptions](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/subscriptions/explicit-subscriptions.mdx) (part of #4710)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.99.0)): The Screenplay generator states a scenario run over a fixed set of literal cases once, as a Screenplay case table. An abstract base specification with primary-constructor parameters and body-less concrete rows (`class Ada() : Registering("Ada")`) becomes one specification with typed `parameter` lines and one `case` row per concrete class. Rows are folded only when substituting each row's values into the base reproduces that row's steps, outcomes and caller exactly; otherwise every row is stated individually as before and Information `SP0076` names why. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3197)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Decision reads for the dynamic consistency boundary. `Chronicle.ReadModels.DecisionReads` `get/2`, `get_detached/2`, `admit/2` and their stream-scoped variants enroll a read into the unit of work, and `Chronicle.decide/2` commits it, returning `{:error, %Chronicle.ReadModels.DecisionConflict{}}` when a concurrent append invalidated the read. Passive reducers can be read this way too. See [Dynamic Consistency Boundary](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/dynamic-consistency-boundary.md). (#140)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): `Chronicle.Testing`: an in-memory kernel with concurrency and constraint checks, in-process reactors and reducers, `wait_for_observers/1`, `assert_appended`/`refute_appended`, `Chronicle.Testing.Case`, an isolated-store helper for integration specs, and `Chronicle.Behaviour` so the `Chronicle` facade can be mocked with Mox. See [Testing](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/testing.md). (#149)
