- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Captures: `use Chronicle.Captures.Capture` declares a capture that compiles to Capture Declaration Language, with compile-time errors for a missing source, key or append condition. `Chronicle.Captures` registers, starts, stops, lists and validates captures, and the client registers them through `captures:` or `otp_app:` discovery. (#148)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Discovery report and artifact identity drift: `Chronicle.Client.discovery_report/1`, `identity_drift/1`, a `drift_policy: :ignore | :warn | :fail` option (default `:warn`, never fails startup), a one-time warning for reactor, reducer and projection ids defaulted from the module name, and `mix chronicle.discover`. (#147)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Standalone constraint modules with `use Chronicle.Events.Constraint`, registered through `constraints:` or discovery, plus `remove_constraint/2` with per-value removal properties, `:scope` and `:event_sequences` on `unique_event_type`, violation message callbacks, and `Chronicle.Events.ConstraintViolation.details/1`. (#143)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Projections: a one-line `children` form, `removed_with_join/2`, `clear_with/2`, a default `:on` for joins inside `children`, and compile-time errors for a root `join` without `:on`, a duplicate property target and an unknown field. (#127)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Read models: shared, self-reconnecting subscriptions with `Chronicle.ReadModels.subscribe/2` and `unsubscribe/1`, `:filter` and `:sort` on `observe/2`, `observe_stream/2`, and `Chronicle.Projections.query/2`, `preview/2` and `wait_for/3`. (#144)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): `ConcurrencyScope` pipe helpers (`with_sequence_number/2`, `with_event_types/2`, `with_event_stream_id/2` and more) and `at_tail/2`, and an opt-in client-wide `concurrency: [default: :optimistic, check_first_append: true]` strategy. (#134)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.13.0)): Prepared events with `EventLog.prepare/2` and `append_prepared/3`, `get_metadata_at/2`
- **AI** ([Release v7.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.55.0)): `cratis-screenplay-modeling-lifecycle` ships `scripts/model-first-status.mjs`, which decides whether a repository is opted in to model-first work: it reports the model root, why it is or is not opted in, drafts and model changes in progress, with exit 0 when opted in, 1 when not and 2 when it cannot decide, plus `--json`, `--root` and a self-test. Test and specification fixtures, samples and documentation `.play` files do not opt a repository in. (#677)
- **AI** ([Release v7.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.54.0)): Event modeling skill: a section and a reference on event log hygiene (no ticks, signal events, "last looked" timestamps, telemetry or non-answers; orchestrate with jobs, state machines, reminders, command operations or `ICommandPipeline`), zero repeats, `…Changed` naming, and measuring the log for identical consecutive events.
- **AI** ([Release v7.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.54.0)): Event modeling skill: a reference for rolling out a release that removes or reshapes many event types (scale to zero, backup, dry run with fuses, wipe the whole schema registry, BSON `NumberLong`, replay verification).
- **AI** ([Release v7.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.54.0)): Glossary entries for observation command and signal event.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.170.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.170.0)): `PLAY0690` warns when a reducer-backed read model cannot be keyed the way it is queried: a single-instance query looks the view up `by` a type that is not the event-source identity type of the events the reducer observes, or the reducer observes events whose producers have different identity types. Reducer instances are keyed by each event's event-source id, so the warning points to a projection with property keys, or a join, instead (part of #132)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.170.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.170.0)): The warning compares declared identity types only where every producer is known (commands with `for`, implicit or generated identifiers, and reactions with an explicit `for`), applies the reducer's `from` filters first, and stays silent for events from reactions without `for`, captures, seeds, code or other applications; `screenplay check`, MCP diagnostics and the editor host report it, and `--warnaserror` turns it into an error (part of #132)
- **AI** ([Release v7.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.53.0)): Every skill now has trigger evaluations (should-trigger requests and near misses that name the sibling skill that should win) and output evaluations with checkable assertions, covering the 70 skills that had none; the list of unevaluated skills is empty, so new skills cannot ship without evaluations. (#676)
- **AI** ([Release v7.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.53.0)): The legacy-extraction skill explains when Arc's Screenplay generator writes `then denied` for a specification asserting `ShouldNotBeAuthorized()`, and why it otherwise withholds the specification with `SP0039`. Requires Arc.Screenplay 22.98.0. (#699)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.168.0)): `screenplay_declaration_details` describes a generated concept's rules: `generation.derivedPattern` is the anchored regular expression the strategy provably produces (or null with `derivedPatternUnknownReason`), and `generation.rules` lists every validation rule in authored order with its proof status (`proven`, `refuted` with a witness value, or `unproven` with a reason), plus `diagnosticCode` and `repair` when the compiler reported PLAY0688 or PLAY0689 at that rule. The proofs come from the same rule-consistency check the compiler uses. See [declaration details](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/reference.md). (part of #618)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.167.0)): The canonical corpus publishes the reference clock's schedule behavior as a versioned JSON fixture, `clock-schedules.v1.json`: the rules for epoch-counted intervals and daily, weekly and monthly UTC schedules, and boundary vectors with their expected due instants, including starts before the epoch, offset normalization, months without a 31st, 29 February in leap and non-leap years, the last calendar day and the occurrence limit (#754)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.167.0)): `ClockScheduleVectors` in `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` exposes the fixture bytes with its version and SHA-256, so a consumer can pin it by hash; the file can also be fetched from the repository at the release tag (#754)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): `cratis-multi-service-architecture`: designing several Cratis services with their own event stores on one Chronicle kernel: store and namespace boundaries, public contract events through the outbox, the three ways a consumer subscribes, at-least-once delivery and consumer idempotency, personal data across stores, Arc per service, and what the products do not provide. (#693)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): `cratis-multi-service-local-orchestration`: running several services from one repository with a contracts project, a .NET Aspire AppHost using `Cratis.Chronicle.Aspire`, or Docker Compose, with explicit connection strings, ports, health checks, startup order and per-store `cratis` CLI contexts. (#693)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): The Screenplay boundaries skill explains how to model several services: one model folder per service, public events, foreign event copies, directed Translate slices and what Stage renders today. (#693)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): Screenplay `then no events` and `when redelivered` specifications (ESM v17). (#668)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): Query argument validation against concept rules and `then error` after a query (ESM v18). (#684)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): Reaction refusal branches and `$refusal` values (ESM v19). (#681)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): One origin per capture and the `PLAY0687` error. (#663)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): The Screenplay MCP proposal summary, authoring diagnostic counts and exact-source proposals. (#666)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.98.0)): The Screenplay generator states a command specification asserting `result.ShouldNotBeAuthorized()` as `then denied` when the denial is explained by the stated authorization: the command has an emitted `authorize` gate with exactly recovered roles or policies, the scenario has an exactly recovered `given caller`, the pinned Screenplay reference runner denies that caller, and no other outcome is asserted. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3185)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): A repository can declare its Screenplay validation gate in a committed `screenplay.json` at the model root: the `checks`, `warningsAsErrors`, `scope` and `openQuestions` the `screenplay` check command runs, so scripts and CI no longer repeat the options. The model root is the nearest folder at or above the target that holds `screenplay.json` or `.screenplay/`. A command-line option overrides only its own value, `--no-gate-file` ignores the file for one run, and a file with an unknown key or check name is refused with its location rather than ignored. See [decision 0076](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0076-declare-the-validation-gate-in-a-model-root-file.md) and the [`screenplay-v1` JSON schema](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/screenplay-v1.schema.json). (#739)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): The `screenplay` check command gains `--format json`, which writes one JSON report with the diagnostics, open questions and the effective gate, and `--print-gate`, which prints the effective gate and where each value came from without compiling. (#739)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): `ScreenplayValidationGate.Read`, `ValidationGateRoot.Resolve` and `ValidationGateResolver.Resolve` in `Cratis.Screenplay` let other tools read and apply the same gate. (#739)
- **cli** ([Release v3.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.63.0)): `cratis render` accepts Screenplay ESM v17 models: `then no events` renders for commands and single production-only reactions, and `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` renders for a single named reaction trigger with event productions or one supported command invocation. Fan-out, deeper cascades and other shapes Stage cannot render yet are refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (#365)
- **cli** ([Release v3.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.63.0)): `cratis render` accepts Screenplay ESM v19 models: reaction invocations can declare `on refused`, `on refused by validation`, `on refused by constraint [Name]` and `on refused by authorization` branches that `produce` events from `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message` or `acknowledge` the refusal. Shapes Stage cannot reproduce exactly are refused with `STAGE-ESM-039`. Models newer than v19 are refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`. (#365)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.97.0)): The Screenplay generator extracts reactors that handle command refusals with `Invoke.Command(...).OnRefused(...)` and `Acknowledge(...)` into `invokes … on refused [by validation | authorization | constraint …]` branches. Branches appear in declaration order. Branches that produce events map `CommandRefusal.Message` to `$refusal.message`, and the constraint name to `$refusal.constraint` under constraint selectors. A reactor whose callbacks cannot be proven pure, or whose output is capped below ESM v19, keeps its `file` reference and reports `SP0071`. (#3094)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.97.0)): New analyzer `ARCCHR0027` warns when an `OnRefused` branch returns something the runtime rejects, such as a command, a non-event object or an empty collection. See [ARCCHR0027](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/code-analysis/ARCCHR0027.md). (#3094)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Concepts that declare `generate` can carry validation rules: the compiler proves each rule against the generation strategy, binds a proven rule silently, reports a rule the strategy can break as `PLAY0688` with an example value that breaks it, and reports a rule it cannot decide as a `PLAY0689` warning with the reason (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Length, `not empty`, `equal`, `not equal` and `matches` rules are decided exactly for the regular-expression subset described in the generated-values documentation; `matches` is proven only for patterns a backtracking regex engine matches without ambiguity, so a proven rule never times out on a generated value (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Generated and specification fixtures are checked against their concept's rules as well as the strategy's shape; a fixture that breaks a rule is a contract violation (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): A repair replaces a refuted rule with the `matches` pattern derived from the strategy (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): Executable model version 20 carries rules on generated concepts; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (part of #616)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.0)): `RegistrationOutcome.IsRegistering` says that the .NET client is registering its artifacts again, after a reconnect or a manual `RegisterAll`. The other members still describe the previous run, so `HasRun` and `IsSuccess` keep their meaning. (#4875)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.163.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.163.0)): `ScreenplayWorkspace.Rehome(documents, RehomeScope.Subset)` re-homes a workspace's identities onto a document set that holds only part of its model, such as the features a host has generated so far. Every declaration the documents contain keeps its semantic ID and its event contract ID, revision and origin; assignments for left-out declarations are dropped, so `ScreenplayModelRoot.Open` and `screenplay baseline admit`/`check` accept the state. Declarations the workspace does not know and documents that do not compile on their own are still refused. `Rehome(documents)` keeps its behaviour. See [Call these from your own tooling](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md#call-these-from-your-own-tooling). (#749)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.103.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.103.0)): Rendered specifications now cover `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` for a single named reaction trigger with productions, or with one supported command invocation. The generated scenario records the givens with Chronicle's `History` and redelivers the located occurrence with its original sequence number, correlation and stream routing, so the input is never appended again. Fan-out, deeper cascades, constraints, read-model or query expectations, and redelivery combined with refusal branches are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, which names the reason. (#371)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.96.0)): The Screenplay generator reads the application's react-router route table (`<Routes>`/`<Route path element>` declarations, or `createBrowserRouter`, `createHashRouter` and `createMemoryRouter` calls with literal route objects) and states navigation between screens: a menu item's `navigate('/literal')` becomes `item <name> navigate to <Screen>`, and a `CommandDialog` `onSuccess` navigation becomes the action's `navigate to <Screen>`. Required route parameters such as `:id` are bound with `parameter id from literal "42"`, and a screen in another slice is referenced by its qualified name. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3203)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.96.0)): Navigation is stated only when the destination is proven: the route table is the application's root table (it renders or creates the router, or it is the only file declaring routes), and exactly one known route can match the literal path. Ambiguous, computed, lazy, wildcard and nested route tables, `<Link to>`, relative navigation and navigation with options stay reported with SP0028. (#3203)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Stage accepts Screenplay ESM v19 models. A reaction's invocation can declare ordered `on refused` branches (any, validation, constraint, a named constraint, or authorization) that acknowledge the refusal or produce events from `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message`. Rendered reactors use Arc's `OnRefused`/`Acknowledge`. Stage's semantic runs and the Stage Host settle them as Screenplay does: the first matching branch handles the refusal and stops the rest of that trigger, and a bare `on refused` never catches authorization. (#214)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Refusal branches Stage can't reproduce exactly fail with `STAGE-ESM-039`, which names the reason. That covers several refusing triggers on the same event, `$refusal.message` from a command with localized messages, and `$refusal.constraint` when several constraints can fail together. (#214)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.162.0)): `ScreenplayWorkspace.Rehome` re-homes a workspace's identities onto a different document split of the same model, so a host that writes its model to a repository in its own folder layout can produce a `.screenplay/identities.json` that `ScreenplayModelRoot.Open` and `screenplay baseline admit`/`check` accept. Every semantic ID and event contract ID, revision and origin is kept; re-homing refuses added or removed declarations, source that does not compile, and any document set a model root could not hold and discover again. See [Call these from your own tooling](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md#call-these-from-your-own-tooling). (#747)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.162.0)): `ModelRootDocumentKeys.For` returns the stable document key model-root discovery assigns to a path. (#747)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.161.0)): `then error` after `when query` (or after `then query … arguments` in a specification with no action) asserts that the query was rejected with a validation error, for example a lookup by an invoice number its concept's rules refuse. The form is admitted in executable semantic model version 18, and only models that use it select that version. (#682)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.0)): When a command appends to an event source whose id was generated by a `[GeneratedValue]` parameter in the same invocation, the append requires the whole event source to be empty. If it already has events, the command fails with `ConcurrencyViolation` and `ReasonDetail` `GeneratedIdentifierCollision`, and `Handle()` is not run again. This covers returned events (including `EventForEventSourceId`, `(id, event)` tuples and `EventsWithConcurrencyScopes`) and aggregates. See [Fresh identifiers](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/chronicle/commands/fresh-identifiers.md). (#3129)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.0)): `[GeneratedValue(Freshness = GeneratedValueFreshness.Unguarded)]` opts a generated value out of the guard. (#3129)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.0)): The .NET client declares the global seeds it discovers from `ICanSeedEvents` as its program's authoritative set, keyed by `ChronicleOptions.ProgramIdentifier`, or by the entry assembly name when that is not set. Removing a seeder, or moving its seeds into `ForNamespace(...)`, therefore stops them reaching namespaces created afterwards. Events already appended stay where they are. See [seeding with C#](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/event-seeding/seeding-with-csharp.mdx) (#4869)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.0)): An authoring rule for skills, rules, agents and prompts that loads when one of them is being written or reviewed: what a skill is, how a description routes (what it does, when to use it, what it is not for), size targets and progressive disclosure, timeless text, instruction style, one source of truth, MCP tool naming, scripts for deterministic steps, evaluations, what belongs in always-on files, and a review checklist. Harnesses without path-scoped rules find it through `general.md`. (part of #674)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.0)): Verification checks that every skill description says when to use the skill and is written without "I" or "you", that every file in a skill is reachable from its `SKILL.md`, that `general.md` stays within 200 lines, and that every skill either has evaluations or is listed as unevaluated with a reason; new skills cannot be added to that list. (part of #674)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.0)): Warnings, ahead of a later switch to failures, for descriptions without a "Not for" boundary or with process words, skills close to the size cap, time words, versions and issue references in rules, agents and prompts, and provenance pages in skills. (part of #674)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.94.0)): The Screenplay generator now writes a screen's `action`, toolbar `item … action` and `data … via query` references to commands and queries of another slice, qualified with the shortest slice scope that names exactly one declaration (for example `action Authors.Registration.RegisterAuthor`). The owning slice is found through the component's import. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3204)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.93.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0023 reports a read model parameter that Arc cannot match to a single command property by its key type (#3251)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.93.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0025 reports a command with no event source key that injects a read model. The read model is looked up by a newly generated id on every call and is never found (#3251)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.93.0)): Analyzer ARCCHR0026 reports a command or command validator that reads a Chronicle-backed read model through `IMongoCollection<T>` (#3251)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.63.0)): `[ReactorSideEffect]` marks a type as a reactor side-effect return type, so analyzer CHR0054 accepts reactor handlers that return it synchronously, or return an array or `IEnumerable<T>` of it. Hosts that register an `IReactorSideEffectHandler` for their own return types, such as Arc's command invocations, put it on those types. The marker only informs the analyzer; a registered handler still processes the value. See [CHR0054](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/code-analysis/CHR0054.mdx) (#4882)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Stage accepts Screenplay ESM v17 models. Stage's semantic specification runs execute `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>`: the given event is delivered again to the named reaction only, without being appended again, and what follows is settled. They also execute `then no events` for commands, reactions and appends. (part of #371)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.101.0)): Rendered command specifications and single production-only reaction specifications with `then no events` assert that nothing was appended or produced. Rendered redelivery, fan-out, deeper cascades and appends with no observer are refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`, which names the reason; rendering redelivery needs https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4873. (part of #371)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.92.0)): `IAsyncCommandExecutionScope`: a command execution scope can now await work in `BeginAsync`. It runs after the command context is built and before filters and validators. Validate-only calls do not run execution scopes (#3257)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.92.0)): `CommandScenario` can simulate a competing append with `AppendConcurrently` in commands that do not use decision reads (#3257)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.100.0)): Unique constraints now render on generated text values (`uuid`, `uuidv7`, `ulid`, `typeid`, `nanoid` and `bytes` concepts backed by String). A collision rejects the command with the named constraint violation, as in Screenplay. Each new attempt generates a fresh value; nothing is retried automatically. Specifications that assert a collision pin the generated values involved. (#377)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.2)): Observers report when their position moves past a live event with no evidence that it was delivered: the `chronicle-observer-events-suspected-skipped` counter and a rate-limited warning log name the observer, partition, sequence number and reason. Failed queue dispatches to an observer are counted by `chronicle-appended-events-queue-events-possibly-undelivered`. Delivery behaviour is unchanged; the troubleshooting guide explains how to investigate and recover a suspected skip (#4820)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.0)): Units of work accept enrollment contributors: `IUnitOfWork.AddEnrollmentContributor(IUnitOfWorkEnrollmentContributor)` returns a handle that removes the contributor when disposed. A contributor states the concurrency scope an event source must carry before any event for it is staged, through `AddEvent`, `AddEventWithNamedTags`, `AddEvents` and every `eventLog.Transactional` append. Equal scopes merge; a conflicting scope is rejected with `ConflictingEnrollmentScope` and nothing from that call is staged. This lets a host such as Arc guard appends that user code makes directly on the unit of work. See [Transactions](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/events/transactions.mdx). (#4861)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.2)): A guide, [Generated values](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/guides/generated-values.md), that follows a small shop model from `.play` to rendered code and runtime. It covers choosing a strategy and when not to generate a value, and every snippet comes from Stage's real output. The generated-values rendering reference links to it. (part of #175)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): Reaction refusal branches are executable: `invokes <Command>` can carry ordered `on refused [by validation | by constraint [Name] | by authorization]` branches that `produces` events or `acknowledge`; the first matching branch wins, a bare `on refused` covers validation and constraint refusals but never authorization, and a handled refusal stops the trigger's remaining invocations while other reactions continue (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): `$refusal.reason`, `$refusal.constraint` and `$refusal.message` can be mapped in branch productions: the refusal kind, the first violated constraint's name, and the error messages joined with a newline exactly as authored (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): Executable model version 19 carries refusal branches as `onRefused` on invocations and refusal values as `refusal` expressions, written only when used; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): The Invoicing sample records a refused payment reconciliation as `PaymentReconciliationRefused` (#433)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Legacy extraction keeps its analysis history, where each feature lives in the code and the decisions that span a feature as committed model notes, with open assumptions as anchored questions, so re-runs and drift checks have a baseline after the working area is gone; line-level code locations stay in the evidence file, and a client can keep evidence out of the repository. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Legacy extraction maps how the existing system realizes each slice today, with targeted ways to read the code for it, and adds pass discipline and question wording for expert interviews. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Model review explains an existing feature or walks a planned change through the model, and the Screenplay Reviewer has a walkthrough mode. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Discovery reads documents with certainty marks and exact numbers, keeps source documents unchanged, records suspected source typos with their locator, and can produce a read-only blueprint summary for people who don't read models. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Method skills add story order when declaring slices, decision state with one field per fact a scenario branches on, guidance for choosing a durable process or a plain automation, and further slicing, scenario and stream-design checks. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Delivery guidance covers redelivery against commands that refuse duplicates, temporal and fan-out cases for automations, reading all of a command's scenarios together, frontend slice isolation, query-isolation specifications, append-only committed migrations and authenticated webhook captures with a published inbound contract. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): The quality gate reports which slices a change touches, as advisory output that never fails the gate. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Hand-written code changed since it was last verified is investigated per delivery, with findings routed to restoring the code or to a model change request. (part of #672)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.61.0)): `ReactorScenario` can redeliver a recorded occurrence with its original context. `Given.ForEventSource(id).History(...)` (also after `OnStream(...)`) records events without invoking the reactor and returns a `RecordedOccurrence` with the event and its `EventContext`; `scenario.Redeliver(occurrence)` delivers it again with the same sequence number, correlation, occurred time and stream routing, so a `ReactorDelivery` parameter sees the same identity as for the original delivery. Observer filters, read-model seeding and produced-event recording behave as for `Events(...)`, for class-based and fluent reactor scenarios. See [Reactor scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/testing/reactors/scenario.mdx). (#4873)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.60.0)): Webhooks can opt in to receive every event in the generation they subscribed to, on live delivery, catch-up, replay and partition retries alike. Opt in with `WithGenerationDelivery(EventGenerationDelivery.Pinned)` on the .NET webhook builder, or set `GenerationDelivery` to `Pinned` when registering webhooks directly. The kernel records each subscribed event type's generation with the webhook and selects, decrypts and, when needed, migrates the event to that generation before posting it; the payload envelope is unchanged. Webhooks that do not opt in are unchanged. See [webhooks](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/webhooks/index.mdx) (part of #4710)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.159.0)): `ScreenplayModelRoot.Open` and `ScreenplayModelRoot.OpenForReleaseBaselineCheck` in `Cratis.Screenplay` open a model root read-only with its persisted identities and release baseline, or return a typed failure such as `PendingOperation`, `PersistedIdentitiesRequired`, `IdentityStateConflict`, `MetadataPermissions` or `UnreadableModelRoot`. Renderers and tools can now assess a release baseline on exactly the model they compile, through the same code the MCP server and `screenplay baseline check` use. See [Call these from your own tooling](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/tool.md#call-these-from-your-own-tooling). (#740) (#741)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.159.0)): `PersistedIdentityStateSerializer` reads and writes `.screenplay/identities.json`, so consumers no longer need to copy its format. (#741)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.0)): `cratis render` (publish, `--check` and `--scope`) reports Stage's release-baseline warnings when a render would change (`STAGE-BASELINE-001`) or drop (`STAGE-BASELINE-002`) an event revision admitted to the model's release baseline, or when the baseline cannot be read (`STAGE-BASELINE-003`) or checked (`STAGE-BASELINE-004`). The warnings cover the whole application, also for scoped renders, and never change the exit code, the written artifacts or the receipt; keep `cratis screenplay baseline check` as the gate in CI. See [Release-baseline warnings](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md#release-baseline-warnings). (#356)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.0)): `cratis render --workspace <FILE> --baseline <FILE>` selects the release baseline for workspace input; without it, render uses `.screenplay/release-baseline.json` beside the workspace file. PATH input reads the baseline from the model root's `.screenplay/` folder. (#356)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): Legacy extraction reads tests as evidence for rules, error messages, permissions and example data, and re-runs read only what changed since each feature was last analyzed, which also checks that an agreed freeze held. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): Legacy extraction works breadth first: a whole-system outline, then one feature per pass with a detail budget, steered by the domain expert; features not yet extracted are listed in the loss report. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): The extraction evidence keeps a decision trail, a code-term to business-term glossary and where each feature lives in the code, and covers generic engines, reference data, event order and outbound outcomes. Experts verify through plain-language retellings and a keep, change, new or remove view per slice. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): Discovery marks how certain each item taken from a document is, keeps numbers and dates exact, and records contradictions as questions. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): Command cards name the values each rule reads, field lineage is checked while editing, and a business-rule test decides which rejections and duplicates earn a specification. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): Model review offers an optional stressor analysis and a trace of one piece of information through the whole model. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): Render and gap-fill checks hand-written code that changed since it was last verified against the accepted model, and keeps a per-slice record of where each declaration lives in code. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): The Slice Implementer looks for an existing realization before writing one, mirrors the nearest delivered slice, and records non-behavioral assumptions instead of stopping on them; slice specifications explain how to turn a `.play` specification into C# specs. (part of #672)
- **AI** ([Release v7.49.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.49.0)): The write guard hook stops agents from editing their own settings, `.mcp.json`, hook configuration and, in repositories with an installed corpus, the managed `.cratis/ai` files and installed adapters. (part of #672)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): A conditional production renders as a guarded event in the generated command handler. The condition is evaluated over the command's values and generated values, and productions whose condition does not hold are skipped. When every production is skipped, the command is accepted without appending. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): Freshness guards on generated event sources (ESM v15) apply only to productions that are taken, so a skipped production never causes a false concurrency rejection. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): The Stage Host and the spec runner execute only taken productions, matching Screenplay's reference evaluation. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): A state-change slice may declare several commands; each renders with its own record, validator and response, alongside the slice's shared events and read models. (#360)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): `cratis screenplay baseline check [ROOT]` and `cratis screenplay baseline admit [ROOT] --release <LABEL> [--from-esm <FILE>]` check a model against its committed release baseline and admit a release into it, using Screenplay's own baseline rules. They exit 0 when clean or admitted, 1 on violations and 2 when they could not run, so a CI job can gate on `check` and a release job can run `admit --release $TAG`. See the [Screenplay command reference](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md). (#351)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): `cratis screenplay validate --open-questions report|fail` reads the model's open questions from its notes. `report` prints them and keeps the validation result; `fail` exits 5 while an open question remains, and unreadable notes exit 1. (Cratis/Screenplay#694)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): `cratis render` admits ESM v16: an event's `id` pin keeps its stored event type name when you rename the event. (#353)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.90.0)): Arc.Chronicle recognizes Chronicle self-reducing read models, so injecting one into a command, validator or `Provide()` reads it through Chronicle, including personal-data release, instead of falling back to reading MongoDB directly. Requires Chronicle 19.58.0 or later in the application. (#3233)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.90.0)): Screenplay recognizes self-reducing read models as reducer-built, including reducers whose event parameter is an interface or base class, and reports when it cannot list every observed event. (#3233)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): `StageReleaseBaseline.Check` builds a `ReleaseBaselineAssessment` from a Screenplay workspace or a compilation and a release baseline, using Screenplay's own baseline comparison. `SemanticModelLoader` reads `.screenplay/release-baseline.json` from the model root (it never writes it) and exposes the result as `ReleaseBaseline`. Callers that already hold an assessment pass it on `ArtifactRenderRequest.ReleaseBaseline` or through `PlanFrom`. (#375)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): Render plans that carry an assessment report warnings for the whole application, whatever the render scope: `STAGE-BASELINE-001` when an admitted event revision changed, `STAGE-BASELINE-002` when it is missing, `STAGE-BASELINE-003` when the baseline cannot be read or belongs to another application, and `STAGE-BASELINE-004` when the check could not run or was made for a different model revision. A plan with only these warnings still succeeds with the same artifacts. Without an assessment, plans are unchanged. (#375)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): The rendering guides explain where the baseline comes from, how these warnings relate to the `screenplay baseline check` gate and to the publication check, and that callers that pass only an executable model get no baseline warnings. (#375)
- **Components** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `TablePaginator` can show numbered page links with an ellipsis between *previous* and *next* (for example ‹ 1 2 [3] 4 5 … 12 ›). Turn it on with `pageLinks` (or `pageLinks={{ siblings, boundaries }}` to choose how many pages show around the current one and at each end); it is off by default, so existing tables render as before. The current page carries `aria-current="page"`, and the `info` text («2 / 4») is not shown while page links are on. (#421)
- **Components** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `DataPage`, `DataTableForQuery` and `DataTableForObservableQuery` pass `pageLinks` through to their paginator. (#421)
- **Components** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): New paginator parts `pages`, `page` and `ellipsis` for styling through `pt`, and new paginator messages `page(n)` (the accessible name of each page button, «Page 3» by default) and `pages` (the name of the page list), so the labels can be localized (for example «Side 3»). (#421)
- **Components** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `paginatorPages(page, pageCount, siblings, boundaries)` and `PaginatorPageItem` are exported, for apps that render their own pager with the same page window. (#421)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): `then no events` is executable: it asserts that the action produced no new facts, including everything its reactions appended, and after `when append` it asserts that no reaction appended anything (part of #433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): `when redelivered <Event> to <Reaction>` is executable: the located given event is delivered again to the named reaction only, without appending it again, and the ordinary cascade settles; an unhandled refused invocation stays assertable with `then error` (part of #433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): Executable model version 17 carries both forms as `thenNoEvents` and `whenRedelivered` members, written only when used; models without them keep their version, bytes and outcomes (part of #433)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.89.0)): The Screenplay generator recovers event source and stream model names from Stage-rendered `{Model}EventSource` classes and from directly referenced stream constants, and states a differing stored name with `id`. (#3218)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.89.0)): Reactions that append through an explicit `EventForEventSourceId` destination, including list accumulator bodies, become reaction productions with their route when the route is provable. (#3218)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Events with a stored name that differs from their model name (`id "Original"` in the model, carried as `storedName` in ESM v16) render as `[EventType("Original")]`, while the generated class follows the current name. Unpinned events and models below v16 render exactly as before. The Host registers, appends and rebuilds these events under the stored name. (#372)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): `CratisRendering.CheckPublication` refuses a plan that would change the Chronicle `[EventType]` id of an existing event class in the destination, or whose existing id cannot be read. The new outcome is `CratisPublicationCheck.ChangesEventTypeIds`, which lists each change and reports `STAGE-EVENT-002`. Publishers that switch over `CratisPublicationCheck` must handle this new case. For scoped plans the aggregate policy layout check still runs first, so publishers that refuse only `RequiresApplicationScope` keep refusing those plans. `cratis render` adopts the new refusal in Cratis/cli#354. (#372)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): A plan where two events would get the same Chronicle event type id, for example a pinned `id "RecordedEvent"` beside an unpinned `Recorded_event`, fails with `STAGE-ESM-012`. (#372)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Protected observable list queries now render their success specifications with Arc's `QueryScenario` and its default Chronicle read models. Scoped projections and protected snapshot list successes are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (part of #278)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.4)): `Cratis:Chronicle:Shutdown:DrainTimeout` (environment variable `Cratis__Chronicle__Shutdown__DrainTimeout`) (default 8 seconds) bounds how long the kernel spends finishing in-flight observer work when it stops; the effective budget is capped below the host shutdown timeout and logged at startup (#4790)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.95.0)): Command specifications with a `given clock` now render and run instead of being refused as unsupported time. The rendered specification fixes Arc's scenario clock to the stated instant, and Stage's semantic specification runs use it as the command's occurrence. So `$context.occurred` values can be asserted exactly, and generated time-ordered values are checked against that instant. The clock must be a whole number of milliseconds and no earlier than 1970. `when clock`, clocks on reaction specifications, and query-only and direct-append specifications are still refused. (#381)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): A shared reference on choosing how a rule holds, reached from the decision-read, command, validation, constraint, read-model, reducer and Screenplay consistency skills. It contains:
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): routing steps and a decision matrix by rule shape, with what never to use and what is too much
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): plain reads by backing, and constraint limits
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): Arc and Chronicle traps, costs, and a checklist for generated code (#661)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): Evaluation cases for the common traps (#661):
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): a plain or passive read used as a guard
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): a validator used as a guard
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): `concurrency: true`
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): rules that span two keys
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): uniqueness through a constraint
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): refused decision-read shapes
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): unit-of-work commit timing in ASP.NET Core
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): when not to use decision reads
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.154.0)): `ReleaseBaselineCommands.Check(root)` and `ReleaseBaselineCommands.Admit(root, release, fromEsmPath)` in `Cratis.Screenplay.Mcp` run the same baseline check and journaled admission as `screenplay baseline check|admit` and return a `ReleaseBaselineOutcome` with the exit code, violations, refusal reason, and the admitted entry count and baseline revision. (#720)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.154.0)): `ModelNoteQuestions.Read(root, scope)` returns the open questions that `screenplay check --open-questions` reports, including orphaned questions and absent or unreadable notes, with `GetExitCode(failOnOpenQuestions)` for report and fail modes. (#694)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.88.0)): `Invoke.Command(command)` returns a command invocation from a reactor, with `.OnRefused(Refused.X, refusal => …)` branches that append the returned events and `.Acknowledge(Refused.X)` to record that a refusal was handled. The first matching branch wins, and a handled refusal stops the remaining invocations of that reactor call (#3058)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.88.0)): `Refused.ByValidation`, `Refused.ByConstraint(name)`, `Refused.ByAnyConstraint`, `Refused.ByAuthorization` and `Refused.Any` select refusals; `Refused.Any` covers validation and constraint refusals but not authorization (#3058)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.88.0)): `CommandResult.GetRefusal()` classifies a refused command as a validation, constraint (with all violated constraint names) or authorization refusal. Concurrency conflicts, exceptions, authorization configuration failures and framework failures are not refusals, so they still fail the observer and are retried (#3058)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.88.0)): Decision record https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/decisions/0010-declarative-reactor-command-refusals.md describes the contract (#3058)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.93.0)): Stage's semantic specification runs (SpecRunner `--engine semantic` and `POST /stage/semantic/specifications/run`) now execute `every` and `all` projection blocks whose mappings set event-source identities, with the same results as the Screenplay reference runner. Literal, empty and child-scoped blocks are still reported as unsupported. (part of #15)
- **AI** ([Release v7.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.46.0)): "Choosing a strategy" and "When not to" sections in the Fundamentals concept, Arc command, Screenplay command-surface, model-authoring, specifications, legacy-extraction and Stage rendering skills, built on a shared shop example: `CustomerId` (`cus_` + ULID), `OrderId` (TypeID, time from the generation context), `InviteCode` (NanoID) and `ApiKey` (32 random bytes kept as a secret). Each skill shows only what its product releases; context-based and secret generation in Screenplay are marked as not yet available (part of #576)
- **AI** ([Release v7.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.46.0)): Guidance for recovering generated values from legacy code, gated on the Arc.Screenplay adapter version the CLI bundles (part of #576)
- **AI** ([Release v7.46.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.46.0)): Evaluations for choosing a strategy, refusing generation for a gapless invoice number, writing a pinned Screenplay specification and recovering legacy generation (part of #576)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.0)): `EventScenario.ReadModels` reads and observes the read models built from the scenario's own events: projections, including children and joins, and reducers. Tools that build on an `EventScenario` can now call `GetInstanceById`, `GetInstances` and `Observe<T>()` over the same store they appended to, instead of the shared default store, where model-bound children failed with "Could not resolve parent key". See [event scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/testing/events/scenario.mdx) (#4816)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): `screenplay baseline admit <root> --release <label>` records every current and historical event revision with its resolved stored shape in `.screenplay/release-baseline.json`, a committed sidecar with a published JSON schema. `--from-esm <file>` admits from the canonical model a release shipped. Admission only ever adds entries, refuses a changed revision, and never runs implicitly. (#686)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): `screenplay baseline check <root>` exits 1 when a baselined revision changed or is missing and 2 when the baseline is unreadable, so CI can gate edits made outside the MCP tools. (#686)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): MCP: `screenplay_list_release_baseline` pages the admitted revisions with their current status, `read-proposal view=release-baseline` shows a proposal's effect on them, and `open-workspace` and `workspace-state` report the baseline's status, revision and violations. (#686)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.152.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.152.0)): ESM v16: an event contract carries `storedName` when its `id` pin differs from its name. Models with such a pin select v16, and consumers must explicitly admit v16; earlier strict readers refuse it. A pin equal to the event name changes nothing and keeps the model's version and bytes. (#704)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.58.0)): Self-reducing read models: a class that does not implement `IReducer` and declares `public static Reduce(TEvent @event, TSelf? current[, EventContext context])` returning `TSelf` or `Task<TSelf>` is discovered as a reducer for itself. No marker attribute or new registration API is needed; `[Reducer]`, `[Passive]`, event-sequence, event-store, tag and route attributes on the model configure it, and the existing `RegisterReducer<T>()` and `RegisterReducer(Type)` accept these models. (#4731)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.58.0)): Optional static replay hooks on a self-reducing read model: `BeginReplay`, `EndReplay`, `BeginReplayPartition(Partition)` and `EndReplayPartition(Partition)` returning `Task`, with trailing services resolved from the notification's scope and no model instance created. (#4731)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.58.0)): Analyzers for self-reducing read models: `CHR0058` warns about near-miss `Reduce` signatures, `CHR0059` reports malformed replay hooks, `CHR0060` reports equally specific `Reduce` methods, and `CHR0061` reports a model that is also maintained by a projection or another reducer. (#4731)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.10.0)): `FastForward` on the observation service and its `FastForwardResult`. Requires Chronicle 19.56.0 or later.
- **cli** ([Release v3.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.58.0)): `cratis chronicle observers fast-forward <observer-id>`: skips every event the observer has not yet handled and discards its failed partitions. Requires Chronicle 19.56.0 or later.
- **cli** ([Release v3.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.58.0)): A fast forward action (W) on the observers view of the TUI workbench.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.52.0)): `observers.fastForward(observerId)` and the `FastForwardResult` it returns. Requires Chronicle 19.56.0 or later.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): `store.observers.fastForward(...)` by identifier or by reactor/reducer class, the `FastForwardResult` it returns, and a Java bridge. Requires Chronicle 19.56.0 or later.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.12.0)): `Chronicle.Observers.fast_forward/2` and the `FastForwardResult` it returns. Requires Chronicle 19.56.0 or later.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.56.0)): Fast forward for observers: an observer can be moved to the tail of its event sequence, skipping every event it has not yet handled. Its failed partitions are discarded and the state it already built is kept.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.56.0)): A Fast forward action in the Workbench observers view.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.56.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.56.0)): `FastForward` on the .NET `IReactors`, `IReducers` and `IProjections`, and `Observers.FastForward` on the gRPC contract.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.Events.EventContext`, shared by reactors and reducers: the causing identity (`caused_by`, with `on_behalf_of`), `causation`, `correlation_id`, `tags`, `hash`, `subject`, event type, event stream and `replaying?`. It is still a map, so existing pattern matches keep working. (#125)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `delivery_id` in the reactor context: a stable identity of one delivery of an event to a reactor, the same on every redelivery, built like the .NET `ReactorDelivery.Id` for idempotency keys. (#137)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Reactor replay control: `once_only: true` registers a reactor as not replayable, `once_only: [Event]` skips those events while replaying, and an optional `handle_replay/2` handles replayed events. (#136)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `get_for_event_source/3` and `get_from_sequence_number/2` accept `decode: true` and return `Chronicle.Events.AppendedEvent` structs holding the event struct, its context, the raw payload and redaction details. (#130)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `append_with_result/3` and `append_many_with_result/3` return the assigned sequence numbers and structured constraint and concurrency violations, plus `wait_for_completion/2`. (part of #133)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `ConcurrencyScope.expecting_no_matching_event/1` for creation-only guards. (part of #134)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.Compliance.allow_new_encryption_key_for/2`. (part of #135)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Event type options `subject:`, `tags:`, `tombstone:`, `public:` and `compensation_for:`; the declared subject is resolved on append. (part of #138) (part of #146)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `children` blocks in read models and projections for child collections (nested, with `from`, `join`, `removed_with`), and `clear:` to set a scalar back to no value. (part of #127)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `closes_stream` declarations on event types, unique constraint `mode: :per_value`, and `Chronicle.EventSequences.Streams` to list, close, reopen and check closed stream scopes, read event metadata and verify stored content. (part of #142) (part of #143)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.ReadModels.query/2` options `:filter` and `:sort`, evaluated by the client with a `:max_scan` limit. (part of #126)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.ReadModels.observe/2` for collections and `Chronicle.ReadModels.Reactor` for reacting to read model changes. (part of #144)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.Middleware` for reactors and reducers, configured globally or per module. (part of #145)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.Observers.retry_partition/3`, `clear_partition_quarantine/3` and `clear_observer_quarantine/2`. (part of #141)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): `Chronicle.Client.registration_status/1`, `await_registration/2` and a `:registration` option for backoff, jitter and maximum attempts; an invalid definition now fails once instead of retrying forever. (part of #131)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Operations: `observer_lag:` publishes a `[:chronicle, :observer, :lag]` telemetry event per observer, and `watc
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.150.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.150.0)): A warning, PLAY0686, on an event `id` pin: the pin is not yet carried to renderers, so generated code stores the event under its new name. `screenplay_propose_rename` reports the same caveat for event renames, and the VS Code extension and Monaco editor show it. (part of #704)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.0)): `QueryScenario` can run observable queries backed by Chronicle read models: a query returning `IReadModels.Observe<T>(filter)` emits the read models built from the scenario's given events and given read models, and emits again when a command in the scenario appends events (#3221) (part of #3154)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.0)): A given read model whose key cannot be read from the instance cannot be observed, and `Observe` fails with `PinnedReadModelCannotBeObserved` explaining how to seed it instead (part of #3154)
- **AI** ([Release v7.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.43.0)): The Screenplay model-authoring skill teaches the MCP surface for concept generation strategies: reading a concept's `generation` summary from `screenplay_declaration_details` (strategy, modifiers, entropy, collision estimate and whether it is executable), setting or removing `generate` through typed proposals, and the PLAY0683/PLAY0684 repairs that raise entropy or add a unique constraint. The `cratis screenplay mcp` bundled in CLI 3.57.0 does not have this surface yet (#645)
- **AI** ([Release v7.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.42.0)): `cratis-cleanup-worktrees.sh` removes linked worktrees and local branches only when their work is verifiably on the default branch (merged, merged with upstream gone, or a merged PR at the same commit). It never forces and reports what it kept and why.
- **AI** ([Release v7.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.42.0)): Claude Code and Pi run it after delivering commands such as `gh pr merge` and at session start, so orphans from earlier sessions are collected.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.55.0)): `Workbench:BasePath` (`Cratis__Chronicle__Workbench__BasePath`) serves the Workbench page, its assets and the API it calls under a path prefix, for a reverse proxy that routes by path. It works whether the proxy strips the prefix or passes it through. See https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/hosting/configuration/workbench.md (#4822)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.55.0)): `MaxReceiveMessageSize` and `MaxSendMessageSize` on the kernel, set through `Cratis__Chronicle__MaxReceiveMessageSize` and `Cratis__Chronicle__MaxSendMessageSize`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.41.0)): Concept generation strategies (ESM v15): UUID, UUIDv7, ULID, TypeID, NanoID and bytes, with their encodings, entropy diagnostics (PLAY0683/PLAY0684), strict fixtures and generated-value freshness, and what Stage 4.89.1 renders of them (#643)
- **AI** ([Release v7.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.41.0)): Response names in UI continuations: a form's `on submit` and `execute ... on success` can use the command's returned names, with the scoping rules and PLAY0685; rendered applications do not bind them yet (Cratis/Scene#53) (#642)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): `screenplay_declaration_details` returns a `generation` object for concepts that declare `generate`: the strategy, its modifiers, the exact entropy in bits, the number of values at a 1% collision probability, and whether the compiler admits it (`admitted`, `refused` with the diagnostic codes, or `notAssessed` with a reason). Setting, changing and removing `generate` go through the existing typed proposals and report the compiler's refusals. (part of #618)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): Two repairs for low-entropy generation through `screenplay_propose_repair`: raise a `nanoid` size or `bytes` count to at least 64 random bits (PLAY0683, PLAY0684), and add a unique constraint for the generated property (PLAY0683). `screenplay_repair_capabilities` lists them. (part of #618)
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.13.1)): `maxReceiveMessageSize` on `ChronicleOptions`, and `withMaxReceiveMessageSize()` to change it. Pass `null` to keep grpc-java's own default.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.51.1)): `maxReceiveMessageSize` and `maxSendMessageSize` on `ChronicleOptions` and its factory methods. Pass `null` to keep gRPC's own default.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.91.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.91.0)): Screenplay `all` projection blocks whose mappings set event-source identities now render as Chronicle `FromAll`, including for events of unrelated types and event sources. `all` blocks with literal mappings (Cratis/Chronicle#4663) or with no mappings still fail with `STAGE-ESM-017`. (part of #117)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): An `observable` collection query renders as an Arc observable query returning `ISubject<IEnumerable<T>>` from Chronicle's `IReadModels.Observe`. A `by` key and ESM v14 equality filters become the observation's filter, and a missing required text filter is still refused. (#278)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): A snapshot collection query renders as a one-time query returning the current list, with or without a `by` key and equality filters. (#278)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.86.0)): The Screenplay generator states a concept's generation strategy from its `New()` factory: `GenerateValue.UuidV7()` and `Guid.CreateVersion7()` become `generate uuidv7`, `Ulid()` becomes `generate ulid`, `TypeId("p")` becomes `generate typeid "p"`, `NanoId(size, alphabet)` becomes `generate nanoid` with its size and alphabet, `Bytes(count, encoding)` becomes `generate bytes` with its encoding, and `RandomNumberGenerator.GetHexString(n, lowercase)` becomes `generate bytes n/2 hex` with its case. Plain UUID factories keep no `generate` line (part of #3116)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.86.0)): `[GeneratedValue]` parameters of concepts with a recovered strategy, including string-backed concepts, become `generated` properties, and scenarios can pin them with text values that match the strategy's shape (part of #3116)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.89.0)): A reaction `from <Source>` renders `[EventSourceType("<Source>")]` on its reactor, and `from <Source>.<Stream>` renders `[FromEventSource<TSource>("<Stream>")]`, the same attributes filtered reducers use. Events the reaction produces stay unrouted. (#297)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.89.0)): Rendered `when append` reaction specifications take the filter into account. An append the filter excludes is refused with `STAGE-ESM-011` instead of rendering a specification that cannot pass, and a filtered-out sibling reaction no longer counts as fan-out. (#297)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.87.0)): Concepts with a v15 generation strategy render `New()` with the matching Fundamentals `GenerateValue` factory, including `prefix` and `case` modifiers on String-backed concepts. (#357)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.87.0)): At v15, a command that produces facts on a generated event source guards it with a `BeforeFirst` concurrency scope, so a source that already has facts rejects the whole command with `ConcurrencyViolation`. This matches Screenplay's freshness rule; earlier facts without a destination never make a source stale. (#357)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.87.0)): The Stage Host generates values with the concept's strategy and reports the freshness rejection as a concurrency violation. The specification executor checks v15 fixture shapes and freshness with the reference messages. (#357)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.54.0)): `IReadModels.Observe<T>()` returns an `ISubject<IEnumerable<T>>` that emits the whole read-model collection and emits again whenever it changes, on MongoDB, SQL and in-memory storage. It uses Chronicle's own collection naming and keys, so read models need no `Id` property, and personal data arrives decrypted. Pass a filter expression to observe a subset. Return it from an observable query to keep a list current. See [observing read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/read-models/observing-read-models.mdx) (#4759)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.54.0)): The testing services support `IReadModels.Observe<T>()` too: they rebuild the read models from the scenario's events and emit again after each append (#4759)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.86.0)): A reaction with `runs as` renders its Chronicle reactor with Arc's `[ExecuteCommandsAsSystem(roles)]`, so the commands it invokes run as the system with exactly the declared roles. (#292)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.86.0)): A `when append` specification whose appended event fires one reaction renders as a Chronicle `ReactorScenario` chained into an Arc `CommandScenario`. It asserts the events the reaction produces (payload, destination and an exact count) or the invoked command's outcome. The invoked command runs under the identity declared on the reactor itself, and the specification's caller is not forwarded. (part of #207)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.85.0)): The Screenplay generator states a collection query parameter as a `filter <name> <Type>` line when the query provably compares it for equality with the same-named read-model property: in LINQ `Where` and MongoDB `Find`/`Where` chains, Arc's observable `Observe` queries, and Entity Framework Core chains awaited through `ToListAsync`, `ToArrayAsync` or `AsAsyncEnumerable`. A nullable parameter whose comparison is skipped when it is null becomes an `optional` filter. Models with filters bind as Screenplay ESM v14 (#3196)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.85.0)): Query parameters that are not provable equality filters (other names, `!=`, ranges, `Contains`, computed values, repeated comparisons) stay out of the model and are reported with `SP0019`, and a `MaximumExecutableModelVersion` below v14 withholds filters with a located `SP0019` (#3196)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.148.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.148.0)): Names returned by a command can be used in a form's `on submit` and in the `on success` continuation of `execute`: `with … from`, the `on submit navigate to … by` shorthand, `set … to` and message operands resolve a bare name to the command's response once it is not a form field or behavior parameter (#303) (part of #300)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.148.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.148.0)): PLAY0685 reports a returned name used under the returning command's `on failure`, where the command returned nothing; an outer command's successful response stays available there (part of #300)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.148.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.148.0)): Editor completion and hover for returned names inside `on submit` and `on success` (part of #300)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.83.0)): A `[GeneratedValue]` parameter whose concept implements `IGeneratableInContext<T>` from Cratis Fundamentals 7.24 is created with a `GenerationContext` holding the command's received time and the current tenant (the default tenant maps to `GenerationContext.DefaultTenant`). When a concept implements both `IGeneratable<T>` and `IGeneratableInContext<T>`, the context form is used. A command without a received time fails instead of falling back to the system clock (#3165)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.83.0)): `CommandScenario.Clock(DateTimeOffset)` and `CommandScenario.Tenant(TenantId)` fix the time and tenant a scenario's commands run with, including nested commands. Set `Clock` before the first `Execute` or `Validate` and `Tenant` before the first `Execute`; later changes throw (#3165)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.83.0)): `ARC0027` warns when a generated parameter's type implements both generation interfaces, since Arc only calls the context form (#3165)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.83.0)): `ARC0028` reports a generatable concept created inside `Handle` (`X.New()` or `GenerateValue.*`) and offers a code fix that moves it to a `[GeneratedValue]` parameter, so scenarios can pin the value (#3165)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.83.0)): `ARCCHR0022` warns when a command injects an aggregate keyed by the command's event source while appending its events to a generated event source (#3165)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.85.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.85.0)): The Stage Host (Play and the spec runner) now protects personal-data and secret concept values in its own event store, as rendered applications do. Chronicle encrypts them at rest and releases them on read, and personal data can be erased through Chronicle's key deletion keyed by the event source. Events stored before this release stay in plain text. (#346)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.84.0)): Equality filters render on observable collection queries, with or without a `by` key. Required filters become non-nullable query parameters and are refused when missing. Optional filters default to `null` and narrow only when supplied. All filters and the key combine with AND. (#348)
