- **Stage** ([Release v4.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.84.0)): The semantic Stage Host passes query filters to Screenplay's reference evaluator, which applies them to live queries. (#348)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.84.0)): Generated query specifications pass filter values by name and assert the number of returned rows for filtered queries. (#348)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): A concept can declare `generate uuid`, `uuidv7`, `ulid`, `typeid "<prefix>"`, `nanoid [size]` or `bytes <count> base64url|hex|base32`, with the `prefix`, `case` and `alphabet` modifiers; `generated` properties now accept any generatable concept, not only `Uuid` concepts (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): Concepts with a declared strategy bind into the executable model as ESM v15, as an optional `generation` member on the concept; models without one, including an unmodified `generate uuid` on a `Uuid` concept, keep their exact executable-model bytes and revision (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): From ESM v15 the reference evaluator checks every generated fixture against its strategy's exact shape, and rejects a command that appends to a generated identifier whose event source already has events, with the new `Concurrency` rejection (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): Entropy diagnostics: a generated value below 64 random bits warns with the number of values at a 1% collision probability, unless a unique constraint covers it, and a generated identifier below 64 bits is refused (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): `generate pattern`, `random` and `rule` parse and print in both compilers but are not yet executable, and `generate sequence` is reserved (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): New diagnostics PLAY0672 to PLAY0684 for malformed strategies, modifiers, prefixes, alphabets and sizes, in both compilers (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): Editor completion, hover with entropy and strategy notes, and syntax highlighting for `generate` (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.0)): Documentation: generated values, choosing a strategy and a strategy reference, plus ESM v15 in the interoperability table, golden vector and canonical corpus (part of #616)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.83.0)): The generated-React and live-custom-package browser scenarios assert the responsive shell (`browser.responsive.*`): at 1440px the navigation sits beside the content with no horizontal overflow; at 390px it is parked off the canvas, nothing overflows, the "Open the menu" toggle exists, pressing it brings the navigation into the window and the toggle then offers to close it. (part of #237)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.82.0)): The Screenplay generator states a Cratis Components `DataPage` menu item as a `toolbar` item (`item <name> action <Command>`, with its literal label) when the item's command provably opens the `CommandDialog` of a command in the same slice; that dialog is then not repeated as a separate screen action. (#3049)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.82.0)): The Screenplay generator reports `SP0077` for screen composition Screenplay cannot express yet: a `detailsComponent` pane, a screen opened as a dialog through `useDialog`, and menu item enablement such as `disableOnUnselected`. (#3049)
- **cli** ([Release v3.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.55.0)): `cratis run --package-set <file> [--packages <folder>]` hands the approved package set (the same document `cratis render --package-set` takes) to the live Stage: the set is mounted read-only at `/packages/approved-packages.json` and the folder of each approved package, `<packages folder>/<npm name>`, read-only at `/packages/<npm name>` - never the folder that holds them. The Stage loads each package at runtime from its browser (ES module) build, with the Stage's own React, and refuses to render when a package is missing or differs from its declaration. A package without a folder, a set that is missing or invalid and `--packages` without `--package-set` are refused before Docker starts. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.55.0)): `cratis run` resolves the `ui profile` of the model before Docker starts. A package that is neither shipped (`core`, `scene.web`, `Cratis.Components`) nor approved is refused with `CLI-RENDER-007` (exit code 5) and says to approve it with `--package-set`; nothing is started, so a typo or a forgotten approval no longer produces a sandbox that shows a placeholder where the package's component belongs. A model that does not compile is left to the Stage. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.55.0)): The packed-CLI check in CI now also runs the same package set live: `cratis run` without the set refuses with `CLI-RENDER-007`, and with the set the Stage container renders the custom component, its property and style sheet beside the shipped components in a browser, using the same scenario as the rendered application. (part of #284)
- **cli** ([Release v3.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.55.0)): Documentation: `run.md#custom-packages` and the `screenplay.md` custom package section describe the live package folder, the browser-build contract and what is refused.
- **AI** ([Release v7.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.39.0)): A preferred-syntax reference for Screenplay: for each construct, the form the corpus uses, the older forms Screenplay still accepts, and the diagnostic that flags them; corpus verification now fails when an example uses a legacy, redundant or deprecated form, selected from the pinned Screenplay contract so new codes are picked up automatically (#636)
- **AI** ([Release v7.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.39.0)): Running a proposal's specifications read-only before `apply` with `run-specifications` and `proposalId`, and the `screenplay test` exit codes (#633)
- **AI** ([Release v7.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.39.0)): Caller-supplied equality filters on collection queries (ESM v14, Screenplay 4.143.0), with the remaining `PLAY0268` refusals, query arguments in specifications and order-insensitive collection results; rendering waits for a Stage that admits v14 (#626)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.146.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.146.0)): `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` ships the golden executable semantic model vectors for every released ESM version (`full-esm-v1` to `full-esm-v14`, plus the `policy-negation-v7` extension). `EsmGoldenVectors.Versions` lists them, and `EsmGoldenVectors.Get(version)` returns the exact released bytes and their semantic revision, so consumers can test against the published contract instead of copying files from a tag. New ESM versions add their vector automatically (#677)
- **cli** ([Release v3.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.54.0)): `cratis render` admits ESM v11 (lossless Double-mode number literals), v12 (explicit enum member numbers) and v13 (concept compliance markers such as `pii` and `secret`). ESM v14 query filters stay refused with `CLI-RENDER-004` until the bundled renderer admits them. (#332)
- **cli** ([Release v3.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.54.0)): `cratis screenplay mcp` serves the model-notes tools from Screenplay 4.144: `list-model-notes`, `propose-model-notes`, `read-proposal` with `view: "notes"`, and `run-specifications` with a `proposalId` to run a retained proposal's candidate model before `apply`. The reference explains that `.screenplay/notes.json` is owned by the server, committed with the model, and never blocks opening a model, and shows where to gate on open questions with `screenplay check --open-questions report|fail`. (#340)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `IGeneratableInContext<TSelf>` in `Cratis.Concepts`: a concept implements `static TSelf New(GenerationContext context)` when its value depends on the operation's time or tenant; when a type implements both interfaces, callers use the context form (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `GenerationContext(Now, Tenant)`: the instant the operation was received and the portable tenant identifier, with `GenerationContext.DefaultTenant` (the zero GUID) for the default tenant (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `GenerateValue.Ulid()`: 26 uppercase Crockford base32 characters with a millisecond timestamp and 80 random bits (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `GenerateValue.TypeId(prefix)`: a TypeID with a validated lowercase prefix and a UUID v7 suffix (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `GenerateValue.NanoId(size, alphabet)`: characters drawn uniformly from the default URL-safe alphabet or a custom one (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `GenerateValue.Bytes(count, encoding)`: random bytes as unpadded base64url, lowercase hex or unpadded RFC 4648 base32 (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): `GenerateValue.UuidV7(context)`, `Ulid(context)` and `TypeId(prefix, context)` take their timestamp from `context.Now`, so a pinned clock pins the time part of the value (part of #1176)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.0)): Documentation on choosing a strategy and on context-aware generation: https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/blob/main/Documentation/csharp/generating-values.md (part of #1176)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): The specification executor settles event-triggered reactions after each command or appended event: `where` guards, productions (checked against constraints), invocations, `runs as` system identities, `from` filters, triggers that both produce and invoke, cascades and the 1000-fact limit. An invoked command never receives the specification's caller. (part of #207)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.81.0)): Projection checks cover every event a reaction can append, so a projection over reaction-produced events is either verified or refused, never built from mismatched keys. (part of #207)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): The Host reads a packages folder (`Stage:Packages:Path`, `/packages` in the image): `approved-packages.json` and one folder per approved package, named by its npm name. The set is validated with Scene's `ApprovedPackageSetJson` (malformed sets and a set reusing a shipped package name are refused), the package entry module is resolved from its `package.json` (`exports`, `module`, `main`, or `index.js`), and `GET /stage/packages` describes the set, where each module is served from, the style sheets and any diagnostics. Only the files inside the approved packages' folders, of the types a package ships, are served under `/stage/packages/files/**`; the set document and other folders are not reachable. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): The frontend loads the packages before the first render through Scene's `loadApprovedPackages`, with an importer for each package the Stage reported - never an address a scene supplied - and renders them next to the shipped Cratis components with the package catalog resolving `Package.component` names. A package that is missing, does not load, lacks its export or differs from its declaration stops the Stage with the reasons on the page; a profile is never rendered with a package missing. A Stage handed no set renders the shipped packages exactly as before. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): A shared import map in the Stage page maps `react`, `react/jsx-runtime`, `react/jsx-dev-runtime`, `react-dom` and `react-dom/client` to shim modules emitted by the build that re-export the Stage's own React, so a custom package's hooks and contexts are the Stage's. (part of #237)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): `StageShippedPackages` in the contracts is the one list of shipped package names the renderer's catalog and the live package host both use.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): `Verification/live-custom-packages` runs the built image against a model naming `Acme.Charts` with the package mounted (the authored component renders from the package, its property, its style sheet, shipped components beside it, no diagnostics) and again with the package folder missing (the Stage refuses to render and names the package), and a new `live-custom-packages` CI job runs it.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): Documentation: `Documentation/reference/custom-packages.md` describes the live Stage's package folder, endpoints and import map in place of the former limit.
- **AI** ([Release v7.38.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.38.2)): The corpus verification job runs the edited model with `cratis run` and checks the edited column and a created row in Chromium, using a pinned Playwright (part of Cratis/AI#536).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.22.0)): `PropertyEditorKind` (`stringList`, `numberList`, `stringOrList`, `scalar`, `stringMap`) names the editor for a value that has no fields to describe: a list of words or numbers, a word-or-list, a scalar, a record under the author's own names.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.22.0)): `CollectionItemDescriptor.positional` lets a collection of authored data (chart datasets, tree nodes, columns) hold records without an `id`; items are told apart by position.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.145.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.145.0)): `run-specifications` accepts an optional `proposalId` and runs a retained proposal's candidate model, including its identity changes, through the same reference evaluator before `apply`. It is read-only: the proposal stays applicable, and continuation pages pin the candidate's source revision (#610)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.145.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.145.0)): The MCP docs gain an "Edit a specification" recipe: find the specification with `read-ast`, change its steps, cases or examples with `propose-ast`, check changed expected outcomes with `read-proposal` `view: "semantic-diff"`, run the candidate with `run-specifications`, then `apply` (#610)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.80.0)): The Screenplay generator states the caller of a command scenario as `given caller`: a specification that registers a substituted `ICurrentPrincipalAccessor` returning a `ClaimsPrincipal` built from constant claims becomes `authenticated`, `role` and `claim` lines in the same order. A scenario of an authorized command is stated only when its caller and every policy the command uses are recovered exactly; otherwise it is left out with `SP0039`. A scenario of a command without authorization whose caller setup cannot be stated keeps the scenario without a caller and reports `SP0075`. (#3098)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.80.0)): The Screenplay generator reports `SP0076` for every `[Theory]` scenario, including reactor scenarios, instead of leaving them out silently. Case tables for such scenarios are tracked in https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/issues/3197. (#3098)
- **AI** ([Release v7.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.38.0)): The Screenplay skills teach model notes (Screenplay 4.144.0): recording open modeling questions as `question` notes anchored to model elements with `list-model-notes` and `propose-model-notes`, citing and resolving them through external decisions, re-anchoring orphaned notes, keeping secrets and personal data out of committed notes, and the `--open-questions fail` gate of the standalone tool (#627)
- **AI** ([Release v7.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.38.0)): Copilot path instructions are generated as `.cratis/ai/harnesses/copilot/instructions/<rule>.instructions.md` with `applyTo`, so GitHub Copilot discovers the path-scoped rules (#631)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.79.0)): Analyzer `ARCCHR0020` (warning) reports an `EventForEventSourceId` whose literal routing names an event source definition together with a stream that definition does not declare, so a typo no longer sends events to a route the command does not describe. Routes that differ from the command's route on purpose stay supported. (part of #2987)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.79.0)): Analyzer `ARCCHR0021` (info) reports legacy string routing on an `EventForEventSourceId` that spells out a declared definition stream, and offers a code fix that rewrites it to `EventSource = typeof(...)` and `EventStream`, so the definition and its concurrency dimensions are kept. (part of #2987)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.79.0)): The semantic Stage Host settles event-triggered reactions after each command, including `where`, `runs as`, `from`, productions, invocations, mixed effects and cascades, using Screenplay's reference settlement. The command's events and the reactions' events are appended in one tail-guarded batch; a rejected, denied or unsupported reaction refuses the whole command and appends nothing. (part of #207)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.144.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.144.0)): Model notes: `question`, `task` and `note` entries anchored to a model element's semantic ID or the application, with an open or resolved state, free-text author, and references to external decisions such as `D123`. A question can be resolved by a specification, a description, a decision, an external decision, or dismissed. Notes on an element without its own identity attach to its owning slice, feature, module or the application (#391)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.144.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.144.0)): MCP tools `list-model-notes` (filter by anchor, scope, kind, state or reference; `view: "findings"` returns one `model-note.open-question` finding per open question) and `propose-model-notes` (add, edit, resolve, reopen, reanchor and remove), reviewed with `read-proposal` `view: "notes"` and written by `apply` under the same recovery journal as the model (#391)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.144.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.144.0)): Source, AST, rename, move and property proposals report `orphanedNotes` when they would retire an element that notes are anchored to. Orphaned notes are kept and listed until they are re-anchored or resolved (#391)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.144.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.144.0)): `screenplay check --open-questions report|fail` lists open questions and, with `fail`, exits 1 while any remain in the checked scope (#391)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.144.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.144.0)): The notes file format is published as `notes-v1.schema.json` (#391)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): `RegisterEventType<T>()` and `RegisterEventType(Type)` register event types, with optional `WithId`, `Generation`, `Public`, `Private`, `ForEventStore` and `PublishTo` settings. Defaults match the identifiers markers produce today. (#4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): `RegisterReactor`, `RegisterReducer`, `RegisterConstraint`, `RegisterEventSource` and `RegisterMigration` register plain classes without `IReactor`, `IReducerFor<T>`, `IConstraint`, `IEventSource` or migration markers. A reducer's read model is inferred from its methods, and a constraint only needs `Define(IConstraintBuilder)`. (#4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): A plain migration class with typed `Upcast` and `Downcast` methods can be registered explicitly, and `EventTypeMigration<TUpgrade, TPrevious>` gains a constructor that takes the store's `IEventTypes`, so migrations between unmarked event generations resolve their identifiers from the owning store. (#4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): `IReactors` and `IReducers` gain `Type`-based `Register`, `GetStateFor` and `Replay` operations, plus generic extension methods for plain observer classes. (#4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): `IJsonSchemaGenerator` gains overloads that take the store's `IEventTypes`, so compensation schemas resolve explicitly registered target events. Existing generators keep their current output. (#4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): `ReadModelScenario`, `ReactorScenario` and `EventScenario` honor explicitly registered artifacts, and `ReactorScenario` accepts plain reactor classes. (#4732)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.52.0)): Analyzers CHR0001, CHR0002, CHR0003, CHR0005 and CHR0007 accept event types registered through `RegisterEventType` in the same compilation. (#4732)
- **AI** ([Release v7.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.37.0)): New skills: `cratis-fundamentals-telemetry` (generated metrics and spans with Cratis.Metrics.Roslyn, OpenTelemetry export), `cratis-orleans-jobs` (durable jobs, steps, storage and lifecycle in Cratis.Orleans), `cratis-components-common-controls` (ComboBox, ToggleGroup, Tabs, TagGroup, Breadcrumbs) and `cratis-components-data-surfaces` (PivotViewer, TimeMachine, Canvas, Chat) (#624)
- **AI** ([Release v7.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.37.0)): Trigger and behavior evaluations for the Kotlin and Java skills (#625)
- **AI** ([Release v7.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.37.0)): Go evaluations covering older Go minimums, wrapped errors, canceled streams, early iterator exit, borrowed gRPC connections, ambiguous append retries, HTTP HEAD/JSON and v2 import migration, and specs proving the Go rules and skills reach every supported harness (part of #479)
- **AI** ([Release v7.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.36.0)): The Screenplay model-authoring skill teaches `propose-property` in full: add (Screenplay 4.142.0) with owner, source hops and fixture values, alongside rename and remove, how to choose between a new event generation and `eventNeverPersisted`, how to read `incomplete`, `needsDecision` and `chainCandidates`, and the add refusals; evaluations cover add, rename, remove and a refused event change (#597)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.78.0)): The Screenplay generator recovers generated values from `[GeneratedValue]` Handle parameters. A parameter whose concept creates a UUID v4 (`Guid.NewGuid()` or `GenerateValue.Uuid()`) becomes a `generated` property, or `generated identifier` when the command returns it as the event source, and `PinGeneratedValue` pins in scenarios become `for "<guid>"` and `generated name = "<guid>"` fixtures, so those scenarios round-trip into the model. Other strategies (UUID v7, hex bytes, custom `New()` bodies, generation context) keep their source form and report information diagnostic `SP0073` until Screenplay can state them. Commands that create identifiers inline without `[GeneratedValue]` keep today's output. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (part of #3116)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.78.0)): Event-triggered reactions render as Chronicle reactors: `[Reactor("<semantic id>")]` keeps the observer stable across renames, `[OnceOnly]` keeps replays from repeating effects, a `where` condition becomes a guard, invoked commands are returned for Arc to execute, and produced events are appended to the triggering event source without a stream. (part of #207)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.78.0)): Automation slices render their own commands (zero or more), events, read models, projections, reducers and queries. (part of #207)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.78.0)): `STAGE-ESM-039` refuses a reaction whose condition, mapping or destination cannot be rendered without changing its meaning, such as a condition on an optional, composite or enumeration value, or a production that does not map every event property. (part of #207)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.78.0)): The rendering reference has a new page on reaction rendering, covering the reactor identity, replay, ordering and how live retries differ from Screenplay's reference evaluation: https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/reaction-rendering.md (part of #207)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.143.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.143.0)): Equality `filter` clauses on collection queries bind into the executable model as ESM v14: a filter keeps the rows whose same-named read-model property equals the supplied value, combines with `by` as AND, and an optional filter may be omitted (part of #140)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.143.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.143.0)): Only models with a query filter select ESM v14; models without filters keep their exact executable-model bytes and revision (part of #140)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.143.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.143.0)): Specifications supply filter values as query arguments in both `then query … arguments` and `when query`, and a query call is identified by its query, key and supplied filter values (part of #140)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.143.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.143.0)): ESM v14 golden vector and query filters canonical corpus for consumers to verify against (part of #140)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.143.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.143.0)): Decision record 0069 on admitting equality query filters as ESM v14: https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0069-admit-equality-query-filters-as-esm-v14.md (part of #140)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Stage admits exact ESM v13 language/semantic pairs (Screenplay 4.141.0) in the renderer, the Host runtime and the specification executor. Later and mixed versions are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-016`. (#345)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Generated concepts carry their compliance from the model: personal data renders `[PII]` (also when the concept is a secret too), and a secret renders `[Encrypted]` with its scope plus `[NotAudited]`. `[ComplianceDetails]` is not emitted from the executable model. (#345)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): Command inputs that contain personal-data or secret concepts, directly or inside collections, optional values and nested types, render `[property: NotAudited]`, so their values stay out of the causation chain. (#345)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.77.0)): `STAGE-ESM-040` refuses personal-data or secret concepts used as identities (command and read-model identifiers, event destinations, projection, join and child keys, event-source and stream identifier types) and on enumeration concepts. (#345)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.142.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.142.0)): `propose-property` can add a property along its chain with `action: "add"`: name the owning declaration, the `type`, whether it is `optional`, and the `hops` that add the property to other declarations or map it in a `produces`, `from` or `append` block. A projection that AutoMaps the same name needs no hop. Producers or projections left without a mapping refuse with `SourcesRequired`, and a required property needs a `fixtureValue` for every existing specification and example occurrence, or the proposal refuses with `FixtureValueRequired`. Adding to an event needs `newGeneration` or `eventNeverPersisted`, as rename and remove do (#389)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.77.0)): The Screenplay generator reports `SP0071` when a reactor executes a command and handles its outcome in code (reading, matching or discarding the `CommandResult`), and `SP0072` when a reactor recovers from redelivery through `ReactorDelivery` receipts, instead of keeping its `file` reference without saying why. (part of #3094)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): A product sync ledger (`.cratis/ai/product-sync.json`) records, for each Cratis product, the release the corpus was last synced to and the skills and rules that depend on it; verification keeps it complete and well formed (#619)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): A drift report (`yarn workspace @cratis/ai-verification run drift`) and a weekly workflow keep one "AI corpus product drift" issue up to date with the releases not yet read into the corpus; private repositories without access are listed as not checked, and an optional `PRODUCT_DRIFT_TOKEN` secret lets the workflow check them
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): New skills: `cratis-arc-hosting`, `cratis-chronicle-hosting`, `cratis-chronicle-decision-reads`, `cratis-arc-testing-kotlin`, `cratis-arc-identity-and-tenancy-kotlin` and `cratis-arc-spring-data-kotlin`
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Experimental Go guidance: `cratis/language/go`, `cratis/engineering/go`, `cratis/chronicle/client-go` and `cratis/arc/go` profiles with Go rules and skills for Chronicle.Go 0.9.1, Arc.Go 0.1.1 and Fundamentals.Go 0.3.2 (part of #479)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): An `elixir.md` language rule, so the Elixir language profile carries guidance
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The Screenplay skills teach `propose-property` rename and remove (Screenplay 4.140.0) (part of #597)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Specification examples typed by an event, command or read model imported from another document, with PLAY0520 (#604)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): The auth skill names AuthProxy and its header contract; the application profile points to `dotnet new cratis` templates; the C# rules name the Cratis architecture analyzers (CRARCH)

## Changed

- **Stage** ([Release v4.178.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.178.1)): The semantic Host's `STAGE-ESM-028` refusal for a command that generates a `generate rule` value now names the concept's rule as well as the command, property and concept, and the documentation states that this refusal is by design: the Host never runs implementation bodies, so render the application to run a rule generator. The supported Host generation subset is the built-in strategies (`uuid`, `uuidv7`, `ulid`, `typeid`, `nanoid`, `bytes`, `pattern` and `random`). (#510)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.229.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.229.0)): The specification documentation now defines where a `given` event without `for` belongs: the event source the specification acts on. It also covers how constraints count it, when the reference evaluator reports `Unsupported` instead of inventing an identity, and how runners that append to real event streams should place it. See [Givens without an event source](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/specifications.md#givens-without-an-event-source). (#579)
- **cli** ([Release v3.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.88.0)): Bundles Stage 4.177.2 (from 4.177.1); `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.177.2` by default, and `cratis render` plans with the same renderer. The renderer now takes projections keyed by a payload property or with several `from` blocks (the warehouse movement history of Acme Commerce), which it refused with STAGE-ESM-008 and STAGE-ESM-009 before; the five Acme Commerce domains render, build and pass their 459 generated specifications.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.26.0)): `OnStopped` also runs when the resume hook of a stopped job throws, so it can release whatever the failed hook began.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.26.0)): Custom job storage providers must persist the new `StopFinalized` property of `JobState`.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.26.0)): Jobs stopped before this release run `OnStopped` once more on the first rehydration after upgrading.
- **AI** ([Release v7.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.89.0)): The application rules now cover recurring design failures: whether an action is allowed is a read-model property rather than a client-side derivation, settings events record one decision each instead of a whole configuration object, each consumer scenario gets its own read model and dispatch never reads a UI model, provider clients stay behind reactors, a reactor never reacts to its own consequences, prompts live as content rather than string literals, and the third copy of a job shape becomes a shared template. (#411)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): A Screenplay table (`core:table`) draws a row for each row its host supplies for the read model it shows (`typeName`), and a summary (`core:summary`) draws the first row's value beside each field's label. A host supplies rows as query results keyed by the read model's name; with none supplied the table still draws only its headings, as before, so a running application whose host binds data its own way is unchanged.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.72.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.72.0)): The preview host gives each read model a Screenplay screen shows (the `typeName` of its data, table and summary elements) the rows of that read model from the sample data pack in effect - a pack's shapes are the rows of a read model by its name - and gives the query a data element names (`core:data` `query`) the same rows. A preview of the Acme Commerce screens now lists the sample products, orders and shipments instead of "Nothing here yet" over bare column headings. (`collectTypeNames` is exported beside `collectQueryNames`.)
- **AI** ([Release v7.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.88.0)): The Stage rendering admission reference states what the renderer now takes in projections: a constant key at the root of a scope, running totals (zero start, optional totals, concept-to-number and concept-to-concept conversions, what is still refused), the event source identity in a block keyed by an event property, joins inside children, events declared in a state-view slice, and commands that append to several event sources
- **cli** ([Release v3.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.86.0)): Bundles Stage 4.177.1 (from 4.174.0) and Screenplay 4.226.0 (from 4.216.0); `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.177.1` by default, and `cratis render` plans with the same renderer. `cratis render` now admits ESM v29 and v30 models. The renderer takes running totals that start with a zero add or may be absent, running totals that add a concept into a number or into another concept, projections keyed by an event property that read the event source identity, joins inside children, events a state-view slice declares, and commands that append to several event sources; a product operand of a running total is still refused with STAGE-ESM-050 and published events and Translate slices are still refused. All five Acme Commerce domains (Catalog, Storefront, Sales, Purchasing, Warehouse) render with these: the generated application builds with 0 warnings and its 447 generated specifications pass.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): A running total that may be absent (an optional whole number or concept) now renders for add, subtract, increment and decrement; it is refused only when the same total is also cleared or set from a value that may be absent, which Chronicle cannot add to (part of #558)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): A running total can add a concept into the number it holds, a number into a concept, and a concept into another concept of the same number (the target concept then declares the implicit conversion); a concept into an optional number, or a total of any other type, is refused as STAGE-ESM-017 because Chronicle reads the added event property from a direct member access (part of #558)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): A projection keyed by an event property can read the event's source identity (`sku = $eventSourceId`) when every command that produces the event addresses it with a value of the target's type; otherwise it is still refused as STAGE-ESM-017 (part of #558)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): A join inside a children block renders (Chronicle takes the element identity as the join key, so no `On` is emitted) and its mappings count towards the required properties of the element, whichever of the element's own event and the joined event arrives first; recursive children stay refused (part of #558)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): Events a state-view slice declares are generated with the slice, so a projection can remove with an event no command of the slice produces (part of #503)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): Projections that add or subtract a product of two event values (`add total by unitPrice * quantity`) are refused with `STAGE-ESM-050`, because Chronicle's projection engine adds one event property; the Host and the reference run them.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): An application toolbar button in a layout's `navigation topbar` is reported as omitted with `STAGE-SCENE-NAV-001` until Scene's navigation regions can carry buttons.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.176.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.176.0)): The comparer's "expected Accepted, got Rejected" failure now carries the rejection details like the reference does.
- **cli** ([Release v3.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.84.0)): Bundles Stage 4.174.0 (from 4.167.1); `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.174.0` by default, and `cratis render` plans with the same renderer. The renderer now renders models whose generated C# names collide (a command property named like its command, a query named like its read model or a property of it) by escaping the colliding member with a trailing `_` and reporting it as STAGE-ESM-050, instead of refusing them with STAGE-ESM-012. It also renders read models with composite keys, scalar absence assertions (`then no <ReadModel> <key>`), per-run projections with optional scalar properties, per-production event source and stream overrides, and live (`observable`) queries that push updates to the generated frontend.
- **AI** ([Release v7.87.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.87.1)): The GitHub Actions rule now allows `[skip ci]` on a bootstrap commit that changes only bootstrapped wrapper workflow files whose content the source repository's CI already verified, once the same content has landed with CI running in one canary repository. Package-update bots and any commit touching other files keep normal CI. (#809)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.174.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.174.0)): The conformance matrix no longer lists STAGE-ESM-012 refusals for the renderer where names are now escaped.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.173.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.173.0)): Two production route shapes are still refused with STAGE-ESM-036 until they are proven equivalent to the reference: an override onto an unkeyed stream under a keyed command route, and an override whose literal stream id is `Default`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.128.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.128.0)): With command read protection enabled, a missing required read model now rejects the command with reason `rule` and the message `<ReadModel> '<key>' does not exist.`, instead of `dependencyUnavailable`. Nullable parameters still receive null when the model is missing, and unprotected reads retain their existing behavior. (#3347)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.90.0)): The .NET client now handles different partitions of a reactor or reducer concurrently, while batches for one partition still run in order. Catch-up deliveries no longer time out while they wait behind other partitions in the client. Handlers for different partitions can run at the same time, so a handler that shares non-thread-safe state across partitions must protect it, or set `MaxConcurrentPartitions` to `1` to keep one-at-a-time handling. (#4664)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): `/stage/routes` marks live query routes with `?waitForFirstResult=true`, and a plain GET of a live query answers with its current result.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): A live query whose subscription is refused, or whose runtime faults, shows the error instead of loading forever or keeping stale rows.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.170.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.170.0)): Specifications and the Stage host now run per-run projections whose read models have optional scalar properties. An optional value that no event sets reads back as null instead of 0, false or an empty UUID. Read models with an optional identifier, collections or composite-type properties are still refused, each with its own reason. (part of #512)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.126.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.126.0)): The new capability refuses anything it cannot certify: cloned, wrapped or root providers, disposed scopes, application-registered readers or validator factories, collections of readers or read tokens, and dependency graphs with cached or opaque services. It runs on JIT only and is refused where dynamic code is unavailable. Parameterless validators, commands with read protection switched on and unprotected commands behave as before. (#2831)
- **cli** ([Release v3.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.83.0)): Bundles Stage 4.167.1 (from 4.159.0); `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.167.1` by default, and `cratis render` plans with the same renderer. The release renders protected command reads on commands with append-time constraints, read-backed requirements next to validated request concepts, unique-value constraints fed by a read, projections that set a collection or composite whole, atomic reaction batches, and the specifications of commands whose own rejection or denial precedes a reaction, a refusal of protected reads of a view whose key is not named `id` (Chronicle's guard admits only `id`), the read-absence rejections of reaction-triggering commands, and the replacement of the seeded content of a template slot a screen fills (Scene 4.70.1).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.169.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.169.1)): Stage now builds against Screenplay 4.209.0. (part of #543)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.226.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.226.0)): A command can append to several event sources: a production's `for` may name any required scalar command property, so one accepted command appends an event to the order and another to the product (#859)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.226.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.226.0)): A specification can pin the generated values a reaction-invoked command takes with `given generated <Command>.<property> = <value>`, so a reaction that creates a purchase order with a generated id is specified, not left unsupported (#858)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.226.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.226.0)): A capture over events carries a list across a context boundary whole (`lines = $.lines`) or fans out one private event per element with `children lines identified by sku`, and `nested` scopes into an object (#857)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.226.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.226.0)): The golden, canonical corpus, contract probes, conformance syntax vector and documentation carry them; several event sources and pinned values select ESM v30 (decision 0098)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.226.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.226.0)): The Acme Commerce sample uses all eight admissions (decimals, products, concept conversion, application toolbar buttons, published events, several event sources, pinned generated values, list fan-out) and its workarounds are gone: 173 of 173 specifications pass with no unsupported and no warnings
- **Stage** ([Release v4.169.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.169.0)): Stage now builds against Chronicle 19.83.4.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.225.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.225.0)): A `Decimal` concept or property can limit its decimal places with `decimals <= N` (0 to 28, trailing zeros do not count), so a third decimal on `Money` is refused where it enters (#867)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.225.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.225.0)): A projection `add` or `subtract` can take the exact product of two event values with `by <a> * <b>`, so a basket total needs no precomputed line total (#864)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.225.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.225.0)): A value of one concept maps to a property of another concept over the same primitive, when the target declares no rule or compliance the source lacks, so an id keeps its own concept on each side of a context boundary (#861)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.225.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.225.0)): The layout's `navigation topbar` region takes application-level `button <id> title "..." slice <Slice>` lines with an optional indented `authorize <Policy>` (#868)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.225.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.225.0)): A command specification can state the public event its outbound Translate slice publishes, and `PLAY0285` no longer calls it unreachable (#866)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.225.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.225.0)): The conversions, `decimals <= N` and products select ESM v29; the golden, canonical corpus, contract probes, conformance syntax vector, Monaco completion and documentation carry them (decision 0098)
- **AI** ([Release v7.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.87.0)): Documents the Screenplay ESM v29 and v30 admissions of decision 0098: the `decimals <= N` rule, `add`/`subtract` by a product, mapping between concepts over one primitive, application toolbar buttons, command specifications that state the published event, several event sources, `given generated` for reaction-invoked commands, and list copy and fan-out in captures over events (#805)
- **AI** ([Release v7.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.87.0)): Corrects the statements that fanning out to another event source does not run and that reaction-invoked generated values cannot be pinned
- **Stage** ([Release v4.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.168.0)): Modeled specifications and the semantic Host now run models whose generated C# names would collide, such as a command property named like its command or a read-model property named like one of its queries. Only the Cratis renderer still refuses these, naming the colliding declaration; the run surfaces keep refusing property names that differ only by case and names Chronicle cannot map. (part of #502)
- **AI** ([Release v7.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.86.0)): The pull request rule's guidance on grouping related changes now points to the consolidation guard. (#413)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.167.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.167.1)): The Cratis renderer refuses `STAGE-ESM-044` for a command that reads a view whose key property is not named `id`. Chronicle's decision-read guard looks for an `id` property in the read model's schema and refuses anything else (`KeyConversion`), so the rendered command could never succeed: every execution failed with "give the read model a string or Guid Id". The refusal names the command, the read and the key property, and says to rename the key property and the query's key argument to `id`. (#557)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.167.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.167.0)): A specification that expects a protected read to find nothing (`<ReadModel> '<key>' does not exist.`) renders although the command's event triggers a reaction: a refused command appends nothing, so no reaction runs (`STAGE-ESM-011` no longer refuses it). The refusal stays when a command a reaction invokes reads the same read model, because the message is then ambiguous. (#503)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.88.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.88.1)): The kernel's MongoDB, SQL and in-memory storage can now hold the state that observer type-set changes and failed-partition recovery will need. That is type-set membership and per-connection registrations, retirement results, the pending type-set change, and the recovery cursor, owner and handler holds for failed partitions. It also covers definition-change progress, restart plans, inventory and terminal outcomes. Nothing writes this state yet, and existing observer and failed-partition data keeps loading unchanged. SQL gets one nullable observers column and a definition-change rows table. (part of #4955)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.88.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.88.1)): The kernel refuses to activate an observer whose stored state needs a newer kernel. That is an observer that has started or finished type-set initialization, or one in an event store whose observers are initialized. The kernel logs an error naming the observer instead of reading the state. Existing stores are not affected. (part of #4955)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.88.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.88.1)): Updated Cratis.Orleans to 1.23.4. Job recovery retries failed manager initialization after a rolling restart, and resuming a job continues starting other steps when one step fails to start. Stopping or removing a job after a kernel restart now works, and a job cancelled without a result no longer reports an empty partial result.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.166.0)): The Cratis renderer emits projections that set a collection or composite read-model property whole from an event property of exactly the same required type (`lines = lines`, `shipTo = shipTo`) instead of refusing them with `STAGE-ESM-017`; a whole composite `Set` now counts as establishing the composite for the required-properties check. Optional collections and element-level mappings stay refused. (#558)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.166.0)): A command can map a required collection or composite value read through a protected read into an event property of the same type instead of being refused with `STAGE-ESM-044`. (#557)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.166.0)): A specification that expects the caller to be denied by the triggering command's authorization renders although the command's event triggers a reaction: a denied command appends nothing (`STAGE-ESM-011`). (#503)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): Stage loads a model whose execution plan has problems confined to one slice instead of refusing the whole model with `STAGE-PLAN-004`. Specification runs, the Host and the renderer run the supported artifacts and refuse only the affected commands, queries and read models with a typed `STAGE-PLAN-004` refusal; problems that affect the whole model, such as concept validation, still refuse loading. (#501)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): A keyed query with cardinality `One` is refused per query with a typed refusal instead of blocking every other slice; a specification can no longer pass on such a query only because it has no events and expects no results. (#501)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.165.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.165.0)): The Host's read-model browser API and snapshot queries report a refused read model as refused instead of returning it as empty. (#501)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.125.0)): On JIT, a composite key type without a generated accessor or registration is still validated through reflection, and Arc now logs a warning once per such type naming it, so it can be registered before strict validation becomes the default in a future major release. (#3392)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.162.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.162.0)): The Cratis renderer now renders ESM v22 models that declare `reaction batches atomic`. Each generated reactor handler returns a trigger occurrence's direct productions as one event collection, which Chronicle appends as one all-or-none batch; a selected invocation refusal branch appends as its own batch. Atomic reactions with several production triggers on the same event, and routed direct productions, still refuse with `STAGE-ESM-043`, naming the reaction. Models without the declaration render unchanged. (#422) (part of #503)
- **cli** ([Release v3.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.82.0)): Bundles Stage 4.159.0 (from 4.154.1); `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.159.0` by default. `cratis render` and Studio keep rendering with the same Stage. The release carries the arrangement conversion Studio needs to export an own shell with its arrangement (Cratis/StudioIssues#816) and the Chronicle 19.83.0 pin of the generated solutions
- **Stage** ([Release v4.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.161.0)): Rendering refuses a Scene with error conversion diagnostics instead of failing with an exception, and the scene synthesizer and Host routes collect the diagnostics so an unsupported construct no longer aborts the request. (#532)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): The Cratis renderer no longer refuses `STAGE-ESM-044` for a command that has protected reads and produces an event under an append-time constraint. The rendered application, the Stage executor and the reference evaluator fail in the same order: a missing read, then the requirements, then the constraint. Still refused with `STAGE-ESM-044`: a read path in a `produces when` condition or destination, a read-path mapping to an optional/collection/composite/differently typed property, and reads combined with generated-value freshness scopes. (#557)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): A command whose event takes a unique-value constraint's value from a protected read renders (the validator no longer assumes the constrained mapping is a command property).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): A protected command may now combine its read-backed requirements with validated request concepts (`STAGE-ESM-005` no longer refuses it). Requirements of a protected command are guards in `Handle`, so the order is concept validation, then the reads, then the requirements, the same as the reference. Commands without reads keep the refusal. (#409)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): A specification that expects the triggering command's own rejection (a message only that command declares) renders although the command's event triggers a reaction: a rejected command appends nothing, so no reaction runs (`STAGE-ESM-011` no longer refuses it). (#503)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.159.0)): `ArrangementConverter.ToSyntax` converts a Scene flow or freeform arrangement (layouts, screen templates and dialog templates) back into the Screenplay `ArrangementSyntax` it is written as, the inverse of `ArrangementConverter.Convert`, so a host that holds Scene declarations (Studio's own shell exported as a screen template) can write the arrangement as typed Screenplay instead of dropping it (part of Cratis/StudioIssues#816)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.220.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.220.1)): A query with a `performer` block is now an opaque read: it is listed as code the model needs (`PLAY0270` information) and left out of the executable model, instead of making every declaration and specification of the application `unbound` (`PLAY0268`). A specification that reads such a query is left out; everything else keeps running with `screenplay test`. (#862)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.220.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.220.1)): The Acme Commerce Purchasing dashboard is now composed by a performer; the workaround is gone.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.84.0)): The .NET client sends several guards for one event source only to a kernel that advertises support for them. Against an older kernel it keeps refusing with `IncompatibleConcurrencyScopesForEventSource`, and never drops a guard. (#4818)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.124.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.124.1)): The proxy generator no longer re-reads every type of every project assembly for each type it collects, and no longer scans lists for membership or all generated files per index directory, which made generation grow quadratically with solution size (#3400)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.124.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.124.1)): Embedded Screenplay document generation is skipped when none of the sources, references, analyzers, settings or generator it depends on changed since the last build; the previous documents are reused. A build that reported a warning always runs again so the warning is shown again (#3399)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.124.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.124.1)): Embedded Screenplay documents for each scope are produced in parallel, and sources and references are parsed and loaded in parallel, keeping the same output and diagnostic order (#3399)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.124.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.124.0)): The generated Screenplay now omits properties whose type a Screenplay document cannot name — dictionaries, constructed generics such as `KeyValuePair<TKey, TValue>`, interfaces, abstract or plain classes, and collections of them — with Warning `SP0030` naming the property and its original type, instead of substituting another type or a name nothing declares. Representable sibling properties remain. Commands, projections, reactions, queries, screens and scenarios that depend on an affected type are withheld with `SP0030` or `SP0039` rather than restated over fewer fields. See [Properties without a Screenplay type](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md#properties-without-a-screenplay-type). (#2527)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): Specifications whose seeds cannot be represented faithfully are refused with a typed reason instead of running: partial or null seeds of projected read models, and seeds of read models built by an opaque reducer. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): Stage now uses Chronicle 19.83.0, and the Host image runs the matching Chronicle kernel. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): A Stage release now publishes the npm runtime package before the NuGet packages, and the release fails when any of its npm, NuGet or Docker publications fails. (part of #499)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.218.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.218.1)): When a query policy names one of the query's filters, PLAY0273 now says that policies read only the query's `by` argument, not filters, and suggests declaring that argument with `by`, instead of reporting that the path does not resolve. (#789)
- **cli** ([Release v3.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.81.0)): Bundles Stage 4.154.1 and Screenplay 4.216.0; `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.154.1` by default (#402)
- **cli** ([Release v3.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.81.0)): `cratis render` admits ESM v1 through v28: Uuid `==` and `!=` comparisons in `require` and `produces when` render; ESM v29 and later are refused with `CLI-RENDER-004` (#402)
- **cli** ([Release v3.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.81.0)): Resolved C# validation rule bodies now render into validators instead of being refused with `STAGE-ESM-005`; bodies that do not compile or read members Stage does not supply report `STAGE-ESM-019`, `STAGE-ESM-022` or `STAGE-ESM-015` (#402)
- **cli** ([Release v3.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.81.0)): The Stage Host defaults to the semantic engine and the `eventmodel` engine is deprecated; `cratis run --engine eventmodel` still works but Stage warns at startup (#402)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.123.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.123.0)): Generated TypeScript proxies take their file header from the `CratisProxiesFileHeader` MSBuild property, or else from the project's `.editorconfig` `file_header_template`, and are written without a header when neither is set. Setting `CratisProxiesFileHeader` to `none` turns the header off. Projects that relied on the Cratis MIT header without configuring one now get no header; add `file_header_template` to `.editorconfig` to keep it. See [Proxy generation configuration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/proxy-generation/Configuration/basic.md). (#3329)
- **AI** ([Release v7.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.83.0)): The toolchain, specifications and scenario-coverage skills describe reference-runner refusals of unadmitted projections (`UnsupportedEventContext`, `UnsupportedProjectionBlock`) as scoped per specification: only specifications that reach the projection or its read model report unsupported, the rest run, and `planIssues` lists the deferred issues (#779)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.156.0)): The specification executor and the semantic Host now run ESM v22 models that declare `reaction batches atomic`. Each trigger occurrence's direct productions, and each selected refusal branch, are validated as one unit before anything is appended: a mapping failure, constraint violation, projection refusal or exhausted fact budget appends none of that unit, while earlier successful units remain in the executor. Invoked commands stay separate units, constraint refusals keep their authored message and constraint name, and property-value claims reserved within a batch stay reserved until the batch ends. The live Host keeps its stricter boundary and rolls back the whole initiating command if any unit fails. Models without the declaration keep per-fact reaction execution. The Cratis renderer still refuses atomic batches with `STAGE-ESM-043`. (part of #422)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.122.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.122.1)): `Cratis.Arc.Chronicle` and `Cratis.Arc.Chronicle.Testing` now require Chronicle 19.82.0.
- **cli** ([Release v3.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.79.0)): `cratis chronicle jobs stop` and `jobs resume` now report why the kernel refused the request (job not found, already concluded, not running, not persisted, nothing took it forward) in text and JSON output, and exit non-zero. They no longer claim success when the kernel refused. The Workbench job actions show a refusal as a failure too.
- **cli** ([Release v3.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.79.0)): Updated to Chronicle 19.83.0 client packages.
- **AI** ([Release v7.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.81.0)): The contribution guide asks for changes to rules, skills, agents, hooks and prompts to be reviewed with the same care as code, and explains what the new check flags. (#755)
- **AI** ([Release v7.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.80.0)): The corpus pins Screenplay 4.213.0. The model-authoring skill teaches that application, module, feature and slice containers get identities before the model binds, so notes anchor to them while modelling. It also covers:
- **AI** ([Release v7.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.80.0)): reading the identity and incomplete-document reports from `open-workspace` and `workspace-state`;
- **AI** ([Release v7.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.80.0)): why the notes tools refuse targets;
- **AI** ([Release v7.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.80.0)): preferring `propose-rename` and `propose-move` over manual renames, which need `semanticRenames`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.79.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.79.0)): The code, security and performance review skills, the reviewer agents and the review prompts and coordinators share one report shape: Blockers, Suggestions, Dismissed (with reason), Verdict, and what the review did not examine. An empty Blockers section is a complete review, a blocker states its consequence, and the blocking marker is reserved for issues that must be fixed before merge. Each skill keeps its own risk categories, mapped onto the shared verdict. (#366)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): Command rules and concept rules with a resolved C# body that returns `bool` now render into the generated command and concept validators through an app-local typed context, instead of being refused with `STAGE-ESM-005`. A rule without a message gets the default `A value does not satisfy the '<kind>' rule.` (part of #513)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): A validation rule body that reads `Tenant`, `CausedBy` or `Occurred` is refused with `STAGE-ESM-015`, naming the member; bodies must pass the same pure gate as reducer and policy bodies. (part of #513)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): Rendering an inline `generate rule` factory requires the implementation requirements and resolved contents from the same Screenplay compilation; the documentation now says so. (part of #513)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): Command and concept code-validation bodies written as iterators (`yield return` of messages) now render into the generated validators; interpolated messages are formatted with the invariant culture so they do not change with the host culture. Other constructs the pure gate refuses stay refused. (part of #513)
- **AI** ([Release v7.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.78.0)): Stage skills cover releases through 4.146.0. This adds exact ESM schema admission through v27 and rendered chronological command comparisons (`DateOnly`, `DateTimeOffset`, explicit offsets), including the comparison shapes Stage still refuses. (#773)
- **AI** ([Release v7.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.78.0)): Arc and Chronicle skills cover Arc through 22.117 and Chronicle through 19.81.1. New guidance covers occurrence command execution, storage resolution, namespace removal, projection rollout and reconciliation, `StartAtTail`, and conflict details with refusal recovery. Corrected guidance covers internal static query exposure, validation member casing, React query-key state, and all-event child mappings. (#773)
- **AI** ([Release v7.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.78.0)): The Screenplay skills explain model notes split across files and checks aligned with Screenplay 4.212. (#768)
- **AI** ([Release v7.78.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.78.0)): The corpus pins Screenplay 4.212.3. The toolchain skill documents PLAY0801 `DuplicateEventInProjectionScope`. The projections skill explains that a projection uses each event type once per level (the root, one `children` block, one `nested` block or one variant). If the domain has a genuinely different second fact, model it as its own event instead of reusing one event in two `from` blocks. (#769)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.152.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.152.0)): The Stage runtime executes every command through the registered Arc command proxy, configured like `useCommand`, instead of its own `fetch`. Arc now checks required command properties in the browser before the call, the same rule the server enforces. (part of #499)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.152.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.152.0)): The live Stage's `/stage/routes` also returns each command's JSON schema, keyed by the command's full name, so the Stage can build a proxy for every command, including commands with the same name in different modules. (part of #499)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.151.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.151.0)): Plans rendered without the opt-in, and their digests, are unchanged; a partial plan's digest includes its refusals. (part of #500)
- **AI** ([Release v7.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.77.0)): Twelve skill descriptions have sharper, distinct triggers and point to their sibling skills, so similar skills no longer compete. This covers the Chronicle Elixir, Kotlin and TypeScript clients, Arc validation and queries across languages, the React and slice specification skills, and the CodeScene refactoring and safeguarding skills. (#754)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.150.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.150.0)): Admit ESM v28 models (Screenplay 4.206.0): Uuid `==` and `!=` in command `require` conditions and `produces when` guards now run in the semantic Host and specification executor, comparing canonical lowercase hyphenated Uuids ordinally, including through concepts and protected read paths; non-canonical Uuid input is rejected before any requirement runs. The Cratis renderer renders these comparisons as Guid comparisons, including protected read paths in `require`, and turns a Uuid literal into `Guid.Parse(...)` instead of comparing a Guid with text. The legacy engines refuse Uuid comparisons with `STAGE-ESM-049`. (#487)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.121.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.121.0)): Only `public` static methods on a `[ReadModel]` become query endpoints and get generated proxies. `internal`, `protected` and `private` static methods are no longer exposed, also when they carry the query marker. A `public` static query on an `internal` read model type still works. The new analyzer warning ARC0029 reports a non-public method that looks like a query, so an endpoint that disappeared after upgrading is explained at build time. (#3368)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): A read model whose projection uses an unsupported construct (for example a projection `sequence` or `variant`) is kept without its projection and records the projection's name; each unsupported construct is reported as an error. Additional projections on a read model are now reported as a warning instead of being dropped silently. (#531)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): Unknown arrangement nodes, container kinds, place sizes and size classes are reported as diagnostics, and render planning reports an unsupported arrangement as a finding instead of throwing. Rendering refuses a model with conversion errors. (#531)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.83.0)): `ReadModelScenario<T>`: `Given.ForEventSourceId(id).ReadModel(instance)` now seeds the starting state for that read-model key. Events for the key apply on top of the seeded instance (setters, arithmetic and children), a reducer receives it as its current state for the key's first event, other keys start from the normal initial state, and a seeded key is returned by `Instances` and `InstanceForEventSourceId` even without events. Previously the seed was only returned by `IReadModels.GetInstanceById` and was ignored by the fold. Read models without a projection or reducer keep the lookup-only behavior. (#4846)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.83.0)): `ReadModelScenario<T>`: seeding the same key twice throws `ReadModelAlreadySeeded`. (#4846)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.148.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.148.0)): The Stage Host (and the Stage Docker image) runs the semantic engine when no engine is configured. To keep the previous behavior, start the Host with `--engine=eventmodel` or set `Stage__Runtime__Engine=eventmodel`. The SpecRunner default is unchanged. (part of #23)
- **AI** ([Release v7.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.76.0)): The corpus pins Screenplay 4.212.4. The projections skill explains that `automap` or `no automap` on a joined event decides auto-mapping for that joined event alone; without one, it follows its level. Warning PLAY0380 is described as covering only `all` inside `children` or `nested`. (#772)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.119.0)): The query health endpoint (`QueryHealth.ObserveHealth`) now requires an authenticated user outside Development, over HTTP and both observable-query hub transports. It stays anonymous in Development. Set `Cratis:Arc:QueryHealth:RequireAuthentication` to `false` to keep it anonymous everywhere, or set `Roles` to restrict it further. (#2873)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.82.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.82.2)): An append whose stored outcome the kernel cannot confirm now fails with the `AppendOutcomeUnknown` error instead of reporting success or a plain failure. The events may or may not be stored, so do not retry blindly. Read the event source's events and append again only if none carries the attempt's correlation id. See [Appending events](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/events/appending.mdx). (#4895)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.82.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.82.2)): While a unique or closing constraint index that governs an append still needs repair, the append fails with the `ConstraintIndexUnavailable` error and nothing is stored. Retry it unchanged after a short delay. (#4895)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.82.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.82.2)): A constraint reindex no longer requires appends to stop. The rebuild hands its progress back to the event sequence, which restores claims and closures written while it ran. (#4895)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.213.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.213.0)): Modules, features and slices get identities as soon as the model parses, even when it does not bind yet, so model notes anchor to the element you name while you are still modeling. The identity an element has before binding is the one it keeps once the model binds; renames and moves before the first binding keep it through migrations, as for bound models. See [decision 0093](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0093-give-containers-identities-before-the-model-binds.md). (#885)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.213.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.213.0)): A container address claimed by more than one declaration, or whose identity collides with another, gets no identity and is reported; note requests that target it are refused. While a document does not parse or place, no new identities are assigned and the incomplete documents are reported. (#885)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.213.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.213.0)): Renaming an identified module, feature or slice by hand through `propose-ast` or `propose-source` needs explicit `semanticRenames`, now also on models that do not bind. (#885)
- **AI** ([Release v7.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.75.0)): The general rule, the Pi quality-gate tool description and the hooks README name both configuration files:
- **AI** ([Release v7.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.75.0)): the shipped defaults in `.cratis/ai/hooks/scripts/quality-gates.json`;
- **AI** ([Release v7.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.75.0)): project overrides in `.cratis/ai/quality-gates.project.json`.
