The Cratis C# target binds Fundamentals' `Cratis.Concepts.GenerationContext`, so compound bodies such as `return $"{context.Tenant}:{GenerateValue.Ulid(context)}";` compile, and `Cratis.Screenplay.Contexts.GenerationContext` stays dependency-free. The [context reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/context.md) lists the portable subset and the target-bound uses (#799)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **Shared composition and host.** `App.tsx` composes every screen through `composeApplicationScreen` and runs it in `ApplicationHost` (`@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` 4.38). The Stage decides only what is its own: shell chrome, the command form, the status line, data sources (`decorate`), and a `scope` providing its data, interactions, theme and slice invoker around the screen and every dialog. Deleted: `FlowArrangementView`, `useSizeClass`, the `Stage:template` wrapper, `StageNavigationTree` and its CSS - Scene owns them (REN2).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **Navigation regions.** A model with `navigation topbar|sidebar` gets the blueprint shell's regions drawing the effective navigation (inherit / extend / override per module); module swap, active state in both regions, render-into-slot, first feature for a module target come from Scene. `screenFor` and `moduleOf` (Scene #134) are the Stage's: the screen declared in a feature, the first of a screen's `path`. A model with no regions keeps the authored `Navigation` contributions / flat screen list.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **Toolbar slice dialogs with the real invoker.** A toolbar button naming a State Change slice opens Scene's `SliceDialogView`; fields come from the command's JSON schema (`schemaPropertiesOf`, shared with `StageCommandForm`); submit posts to the route the Stage registered through `createStageSliceInvoker`, validation results return against their fields, success closes and refreshes data.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **Deep links.** Hash history (`createHashSceneHistory`) restores module, feature and slots on reload; asserted in a browser.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **Permission-hidden items.** `App` takes `isVisible(item)`, fed to the regions: hidden, never disabled, at every level.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **Wire.** `SceneApplication.Structure` is now serialized in the runtime document and the live scene when a toolbar button names a slice (otherwise byte-identical to before: plan digests move only for the Scene 4.38 pin and the frontend files); `commandSchemas` carries each command's JSON schema in the same case.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): **SD6.** `SceneApplication.ForRuntime()` and `StageSceneDocument` strip sample data packs, blueprint packs and the profile's selection from the runtime document and `/stage/scene`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): Scene packages pinned to 4.38.0 (frontend, scaffold package set, specs); NuGet `Cratis.Scene.*` stays on the latest published (4.36.0) until 4.38 is indexed.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.114.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.114.0)): Docs: `Documentation/reference/stage-rendering.md` (navigation regions, slice dialogs, no sample data, one composition). (part of #407)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Existing templates (`Dashboard`, `CrudList`, `MasterDetail`, `DetailView`, `FormPage`, `ModuleWorkspace`, ...) are unchanged and keep their names: the foundation set is added alongside rather than renamed, so nothing breaks. As with the components (see #105) the set lives in the published `blueprint.default` rather than a new package (a new npm package cannot be published until a trusted publisher exists).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.104.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.104.0)): A generated or scenario-pinned `[GeneratedValue]` now fails with `GeneratedValueRejected` when it breaks any rule of its concept validator, including Warning and Information rules. Before, those severities let the command run with the value. The server chose the value, so a failing rule is a server defect; this matches the Screenplay reference. Validation of caller-supplied input is unchanged. See [Generated values](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/commands/generated-values.md). (#3304)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.104.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.104.0)): Arc now depends on Cratis.Fundamentals 7.25.0, which adds `GenerateValue.Pattern`, `GenerateValue.Integer`, `GenerateValue.HasValidChecksum` and the `Checksum` enum. (#3308)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.103.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.103.0)): The Screenplay generator no longer accepts "Query reference ... is ambiguous across slices" as a known binder limit. Queries that share a name in different slices, such as `All` on several read models, now bind by slice, and a generated document that is still ambiguous fails verification instead of passing. Arc now depends on Cratis.Screenplay 4.180.1. See [Generating a Screenplay](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/Documentation/backend/csharp/generating-a-screenplay.md). (#3301)
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.113.0 and Screenplay 4.180.1 (including its MCP server); `cratis run` starts `cratis/stage:4.113.0` by default. Rendered applications are two-project solutions with a `<Project>.Specs` project, and validation rules on optional whole-number, decimal and Boolean concept properties now render.
- **cli** ([Release v3.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.66.0)): A render whose bundled renderer breaks its own contract is refused with `CLI-RENDER-005`, naming the error to report to Cratis/Stage, instead of crashing; nothing is published.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): `ScenePreviewHost`, `ApplicationHost`, `composeApplicationScreen`, `composeOutletScreen`, `composeDialog`, `diagnosePreview`, `resolvePreviewPlan`, `createPreviewSampleData`, `previewProfile`, the `Scene:template` wrapper and the flow arrangement view are exported from `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` instead of the unpublished `@cratis/scene.preview`. Nothing else about them changes; the specs moved with them.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): The preview host's stylesheet is part of `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default/styles.css`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): The docs page `Documentation/blueprints/preview-host.md` names the new import.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): The foundation set lives in the published `@cratis/scene.blueprint.default` rather than a new `blueprint.foundation` package: a brand-new npm package cannot be published by the release workflow until a trusted publisher is configured for it on npm, and a failed publish fails the run for every other change. The code sits in its own `foundation/` folder with its own barrel, so moving it into a package later is mechanical.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): The light theme's `text.mutedColor` is `#5b6472` (was `#6b7280`), which keeps muted text AA-contrast on the page background as well as on cards.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.0)): Models that declare an event stream refuse key-only reads and mismatched decision-read routes rather than mixing streams. (#4932)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.112.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.112.0)): Stage now renders command validation rules on optional whole-number, decimal and Boolean concept properties, which it used to refuse with STAGE-ESM-005. An absent value passes, a present value is compared by value exactly as Screenplay specifies, and Boolean equality compares the actual true or false value. `not empty` rules, collections and concepts with named values are still refused with a specific reason. (#419)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.2)): Returning `{:error, reason}` from the `Chronicle.decide/2` function now rejects the decision: nothing the function appended is written, and `{:error, reason}` is returned as-is instead of `{:ok, {:error, reason}}`. Code that matched `{:ok, {:error, _}}` should match `{:error, _}` instead. (#161)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.111.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.111.0)): Stage moves to Cratis.Screenplay 4.180.1. A protected query whose specification has no `given caller` is now refused by the language itself (`PLAY0389`) before Stage plans the model.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): Stage moves to Cratis.Scene 4.30.0 and Cratis.Screenplay 4.179.0, and the generated frontend pins the matching `@cratis/scene.*` packages.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.110.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.110.0)): The Billing sample demonstrates navigation regions and toolbar buttons.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): The skill now documents the reactor and reducer context as a `Chronicle.Events.EventContext` struct instead of a six-key map, and no longer says the client emits no telemetry.
- **AI** ([Release v7.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.57.0)): The Elixir rule and the product ledger are synced to Chronicle.Elixir v3.13.0.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.1)): The shared Chronicle pages show Elixir code for decision reads, the dynamic consistency boundary, closing streams, replay notifications and the testing scenarios.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.1)): The Dynamic Consistency Boundary page now says correctly that only the .NET client refuses reducer-backed decision reads.
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.3)): The Stage sandbox guidance now states exactly what the default engine checks: missing required properties and simple literal `validate` rules on top-level properties of commands that produce events. It also lists the rules it evaluates with the wrong meaning (numeric `min`/`max`, `not empty` on nested properties) and everything it skips (other rules, concept rules, `require`, constraints, command and query authorization, the `/validate` route). Skills previously said it enforced no validation at all. (#709)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.3)): The Arc command and decision-read skills teach that the fresh-identifier guard also covers events written directly through the command's unit of work (`IUnitOfWork.AddEvent`, `AddEventWithNamedTags`, `AddEvents`, `eventLog.Transactional.Append` and its variants) and nested commands, how a conflicting scope is reported as `ConflictingConcurrencyScopes`, and that Arc 22.100.0 requires Chronicle 19.65.2 or later. (#710)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.179.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.179.0)): Renaming or moving a slice, feature or module that a toolbar button names is refused with an explicit message instead of leaving the button's `slice` stale.
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): Contracts and the verified decision-guard profile move to Chronicle 19.70.0. Run a Chronicle server of 19.70.0 or later: an older server ignores `reactors.StartAtTail()` and the reactor catches up from the beginning, and decision guards are only supported against the verified 19.70.0 profile.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): Requires `io.cratis:chronicle-contracts` 19.70.0 and a Chronicle server of 19.70.0 or later. Upgrade the server first: an older server ignores `@StartAtTail` and the reactor catches up from the beginning.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.53.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.53.0)): Requires `@cratis/chronicle.contracts` 19.70.0 and a Chronicle server of 19.70.0 or later. Upgrade the server first: an older server ignores `@startAtTail()` and the reactor catches up from the beginning.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): Requires `cratis_chronicle_contracts` 19.70 and a Chronicle server of 19.70.0 or later. Upgrade the server first: an older server ignores the option and the reactor catches up from the beginning.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.178.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.178.0)): Renaming or moving a module or feature that a navigation item targets is refused with an explicit message instead of leaving the `target` stale. The TypeScript compiler recognizes module `navigation` blocks and skips them, as it skips layouts, so the reference checks are C# only.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): Every sample, canonical-corpus source and fixture that declares a layout or template now declares a `category`, so they compile warning-free.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.177.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.177.0)): Docs: `templates.md` (own and blueprint templates, category, applicability), `grammar.md`, `ui-profile.md`, `diagnostics.md`, the capability matrix. (#761)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.70.0)): Upgrade the Chronicle server before the clients: a server that predates this release ignores `StartAtTail` and the reactor catches up from the beginning.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): A value that Stage's Host generates and that fails its concept's shape or rules is reported as a server error after authorization and input validation, never as a validation error, and appends nothing. (#393)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): The specification executor reports a generated fixture that fails its concept's rules with the reference evaluator's message, checking each fixture's shape and then its rules in fixture order. (#393)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.69.0)): SQL stores get additive event-store and namespace migrations for trigger definitions and indexed effect receipts. (part of #4911)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.175.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.175.0)): Specifications of record captures (`source api`, polls and other outside sources) now treat the first record of a key as added, as Chronicle does: `when added` holds on it, while `when <field>` and `and`/`or` combinations of field changes need an earlier record of the same key from `given capture`. This applies at root, child and nested level; expression guards, `map` and unguarded appends are unchanged. A specification that relied on the old rule now reports the events it expected: add a separate append with `when added` for behavior on first sight and keep the field-guarded append for later updates, or seed the key with `given capture` when the scenario is an update. See [decision 0080](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/decisions/0080-treat-the-first-record-of-a-key-in-a-record-capture-as-added-only.md). (#767)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.108.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.108.1)): Generated applications now use Arc 22.100.1 and Chronicle 19.66.0, and the Stage Host and packages are updated to match.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.108.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.108.1)): Generated commands keep their freshness behavior on Arc 22.100. Stage still guards freshly generated identifiers itself, and generated values in older models stay unguarded rather than being refused by Arc's new default guard.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.174.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.174.0)): `RuleConsistency.Check(generation, primitive, rule)` assesses date patterns with the current UTC year, like the compiler, so it gives the same Proven, Refuted or Unproven status as the PLAY0688/PLAY0689 diagnostics. (#775)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `category` is required: `TemplateCatalog.Describe` / `describeTemplateCatalog` report a missing category (`templateCategoryMissing`) as a problem for a template in a `Blueprint` package and as a warning for an own template.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): Every template in `blueprint.default` (`Foundation / <type>`) and `blueprint.components` (`Business / <domain>`) now has a category; the components list/detail/command templates declare their slice kinds.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `template-applicability-fixtures.json` now covers each level, explicit scopes, slot-nesting failure, hidden blueprints, the slice-kind split, category error/warning and version-pinned references, asserted by both `Engine.Specs` and `engine` specs. `scene-model-shape.json` and docs (`template-applicability.md`, `template-provenance.md`) updated.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): Components that had no hand-written descriptor now have one with no editable properties (their name, category and icon), so a picker can list them. A package written before categories still loads: the category rule is checked by `validateComponentMetadata`, not by `validatePackageBundle`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.173.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.173.0)): `screenplay_declaration_details` describes the ESM v21 strategies `pattern`, `random` and `generate rule` as executable. Their authored values are now under `generation.arguments`, which replaces `previewArguments`: `pattern` and `checksum` are strings, `range` carries exact Int64 `minimum` and `maximum`, and `rule` is the rule name. See [generated values and responses](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/reference.md#generated-values-and-responses). (part of #618)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.173.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.173.0)): Pattern and random strategies report the compiler's exact `supportCount`, with `entropyBits` and the 1% collision count computed from it. Date tokens use the minimum support over the compiler's 100-year UTC horizon. Malformed patterns or ranges, support-budget exhaustion (PLAY0691) and empty support (PLAY0692) give null entropy and the compiler's message instead of an error. Rule generation reports unknown entropy because the implementation is opaque. (part of #618)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.173.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.173.0)): `generation.derivedPattern` covers `pattern` strategies, using the same expression the PLAY0688 repair writes, and `checksum` validation rules are listed with their proof status. (part of #618)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.108.0)): Stage now renders commands with an optional scalar concept property whose validation rules all accept absence (comparisons, `length` and `matches`). An absent value is accepted and a present value is validated exactly as Screenplay specifies. Optional concepts with absence-rejecting rules such as `not empty`, optional collections of validated concepts and optional named-value concepts are still refused with STAGE-ESM-005, and the message now says which of these applies. (#409)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.107.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.107.0)): A generated application ignores `.editorconfig` and `.globalconfig` files in parent folders, so it builds the same inside another repository. (part of #401)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): An explicit concurrency scope whose tail was unavailable now guards an empty stream instead of being skipped; `ConcurrencyOptions.SkipIncompleteExplicitScopes` restores the old behaviour (#4898)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): An explicit concurrency scope built for one stream of an event source is refused when passed to a single-route append to another stream of it; `ConcurrencyOptions.AllowRouteScopeStreamMismatch` restores the old behaviour (#4899)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): The concurrency and Dynamic Consistency Boundary documentation states that revising or redacting an event is not detected by append conditions (part of #4900)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Validators whose constructor registers rules as an expression body (`public MyValidator() => RuleFor(...)`) now have those rules recovered. (part of #3116)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.101.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.101.0)): Arc requires Cratis Screenplay 4.164.0 or later. (part of #3116)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): `PLAY0268` no longer refuses `pattern`, `random` and `rule` generation; generated secrets remain refused (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.172.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.172.0)): The generated-values documentation covers patterns, checksums, ranges and rule generators with the shop example (part of #616)
- **cli** ([Release v3.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.65.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.106.0 and Screenplay 4.170.0; rendered applications use Chronicle 19.64.1 and Arc 22.95.0, rendered queries take concept-typed keys and filter values again so out-of-rule arguments answer with a validation error, and `cratis run` starts the `cratis/stage:4.106.0` image.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.171.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.171.0)): New error `PLAY0700`: a capture over events must key by a property that every `from` event declares as required; a context key such as `key $eventSourceId` is refused. Chronicle fails the partition for an event without the key, so the model now says so at compile time. (#483)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.171.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.171.0)): New error `PLAY0701`: `given capture` and `when capture` cannot name a capture over events, because a record does not say which event arrived. When the capture reads exactly one event, the quick fix in the editors and through MCP rewrites the step to `given <Event>` / `when append <Event>` with the same lines. (#483)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Rendered queries take concept-typed `by` keys and filter values again, so Arc's concept validators answer an out-of-rule argument with a 400 instead of an empty result. Nested and collection concept values are validated too, so they are no longer refused. A valid key that matches nothing still returns no result. (#370)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): Stage builds against Screenplay 4.170.0. ESM v20 models remain refused with `STAGE-ESM-016`. (#370)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.106.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.106.0)): An optional concept key or filter with a `not empty` rule fails with `STAGE-ESM-010`, because Arc skips validation for a null argument while the reference validates it. Query rejection specifications with prior events or read-model state, and composite query arguments that leave out members, are refused with their own diagnostics rather than rendered differently from the reference. (#370)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.67.0)): Decision 0009 now records two clarifications. A unit of work with protected reads and no events stores its receipt through the same validate-only append, so the decision's scopes are still checked. Each schedule cursor tracks the revision registration announced (`RegisteredRevision`) separately from the one it has applied (`AppliedRevision`), so a schedule change is migrated exactly once. (part of #4911)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.105.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.105.1)): Commands that Stage can't render now say exactly why. Instead of one generic `STAGE-ESM-005: Command '<X>' exceeds the first Cratis command capability`, each failing condition gets its own `STAGE-ESM-005` diagnostic that names the event, property, validation rule, requirement or concept involved, explains why, and says what to change in the model. All failing conditions are reported, not just the first. For example, an optional event property now reads: Cratis events have no optional properties; make it required (in Studio's model board, tick "Required") or remove it. The same models are refused as before. (part of #409)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.0)): An enrollment scope that conflicts with a generated identifier's guard is reported as `ConflictingConcurrencyScopes`, without the identifier or scope values in the message. (#3245)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.0)): Arc requires Cratis Chronicle 19.65.2 or later. (#3245)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.66.0)): Registering an opted-in subscription fails, before anything is recorded, when it names a generation that the source or the target event store has not registered, or two generations of the same event type (part of #4710)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.66.0)): When the target event store does not have the pinned generation at forwarding time, the subscription appends nothing and fails the batch without moving past the failing event, so it is retried instead of being skipped. Events forwarded before the failure stay acknowledged (part of #4710)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.66.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.66.0)): Changing only the generation or the opt-in of an existing subscription rebinds it in place, without unsubscribing it first (part of #4710)
- **AI** ([Release v7.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.55.0)): Skills, rules, agents and prompts point to `general.md`'s Model first definition and the status script instead of restating the opt-in test, and verification fails when an instruction restates it. (#677)
- **AI** ([Release v7.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.54.0)): Vertical slices rule: observation commands append only changes, compared against a `[Passive]` model or the stream's own events, with two specifications each; counters built with `[Increment]`/`[Decrement]` need commands that refuse non-transitions.
- **AI** ([Release v7.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.54.0)): Reactors rule and skill: a reactor added in a release catches up the whole history on first registration and `[OnceOnly]` does not cover that; sweeps must not decide from read models that are replaying.
- **AI** ([Release v7.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.54.0)): Projection skill points to the counter transition requirement.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.104.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.104.0)): Reducer and specification artifacts in a render plan now also list their declaring slice among their sources, so a consumer such as Studio's slice Preview can find a slice's files by filtering on the slice id. Existing sources are kept, and the slice of an event a reducer reads never becomes an owner. Plan digests change; file contents don't. The [render plans reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/blob/main/Documentation/reference/render-plans.md) now lists which artifacts each slice owns. (part of #400)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.169.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.169.0)): The *Proposed* view marks what a proposal adds and changes, and the *Current* view marks what it removes, with an Added, Changed or Removed badge that also carries the text for screen readers. The Current and Proposed buttons show the counts, for example "Proposed · 3 added, 1 changed". Elements the board cannot badge on their own (queries, screens, specifications, triggers and persona lanes) mark the slice or module that contains them as changed. See [the board view](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/view.md). (#709)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.169.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.169.0)): The board opens scrolled to the first change, and scrolls to it again when you switch between Current and Proposed or the model is refreshed. (#709)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.169.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.169.0)): Bump `@cratis/event-models` and `@cratis/scene` to 0.172.0 in the MCP App, the event models package and the VS Code extension.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.103.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.103.2)): Generated applications use Cratis Arc 22.95.0. (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.1)): Documentation guidance and the technical-examples skill now say published pages may state version requirements and pin what the reader runs, but must not carry the author's verification log (exact SDK builds, image digests or SHAs, test dates, "tested with" lines, which samples were not run). Those belong in the change summary.
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): The skills check examples against the Screenplay 4.164.0 compiler and describe what Stage 4.103.0 and the cratis CLI 3.62.0 render. (#668) (#684) (#681)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): Arc and Chronicle skills describe Arc 22.97.0 and Chronicle 19.65.0, including reactor command invocation with refusal handling and read-model collection observation. (#695)
- **AI** ([Release v7.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.52.0)): Skills link to documentation pages with absolute `https://www.cratis.io` URLs, and verification fails on a documentation-site path that an agent cannot open. (#696)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.98.0)): Denial specifications that cannot be stated truthfully stay reported with SP0039, and the warning now names the reason: caller not recovered, policy not recovered exactly, the stated caller satisfies the stated authorization, the denial cannot be decided from the stated caller, the denial is combined with another outcome, no stated policy explains it, or the command's own code can return an authorization denial. (#3185)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.2)): `ConflictingEnrollmentScope` now carries the conflict as typed properties: `EventSourceId`, `Effective`, `Required` and, when known, `EventSequenceId`. Hosts that translate enrollment conflicts, such as Arc's fresh-identifier guard, can now classify them without parsing the message. (#4909)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.166.0)): The event model board in the MCP App takes the theme from the MCP Apps host when it connects and whenever the host changes it; a host that sends no theme, or one the board does not know, still gets dark. See [the view documentation](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/blob/main/Documentation/screenplay/mcp/view.md). (#710)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.166.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.166.0)): Bump `@cratis/event-models` and `@cratis/scene` to 0.171.14 in the MCP App, the event models package and the VS Code extension, and `@cratis/fundamentals` to 7.24.1, so the board's chrome uses theme tokens and the page loads one copy of `@cratis/fundamentals`.
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.5](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.5)): The legacy-extraction skill explains which navigation Arc's Screenplay generator recovers from react-router route tables (`item <name> navigate to <Screen>` from menu items and `navigate to <Screen>` after a command dialog succeeds), what it needs to match a route to a screen, and what still has to be written by hand. Requires Arc.Screenplay 22.96.0. (#692)
- **cli** ([Release v3.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.63.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.103.0 and Screenplay 4.160.0; rendered applications use Chronicle 19.64.1 and Arc 22.88.0, and `cratis run` starts the `cratis/stage:4.103.0` image.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.97.0)): `CommandInvocation` carries Chronicle's `[ReactorSideEffect]` marker, so reactors that return command invocations synchronously no longer raise `CHR0054`. (#3094)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.97.0)): Arc requires Cratis Chronicle 19.63.0 or later and Cratis Screenplay 4.160.0 or later. (#3094)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): `PLAY0268` no longer refuses rules on generated concepts (part of #616)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.164.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.164.0)): The generated-values documentation follows one shop example through strategies, choosing a strategy, rule consistency and when not to generate (part of #616)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.4)): Every skill body now stays under the 16,000-character authoring target: tables, extended examples and long procedures in 34 skills (most of the Screenplay skills, the Chronicle clients, reactors, reducers and projections, and the Arc command, query and identity skills) moved into `references/`, each with a pointer that says when to read it. No guidance was removed. (#686)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.4)): Verification warns when a skill body grows past the 16,000-character target, instead of at 18,000. (#686)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.4)): Verification fails when a relative link in a skill or one of its references does not resolve from the file it is in. (#686)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.0)): The ASP.NET Core Chronicle health check reports the configured failure status ("Registration is in progress") while the client re-registers. After a kernel restart, readiness therefore stays down until observers, projections and constraints are registered again, instead of routing traffic to a pod that is not ready. (#4875)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.0)): `WaitForRegistration` now waits for a registration that is in progress and returns its outcome, instead of returning the previous outcome at once. (#4875)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.103.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.103.0)): Stage, the Stage Host image (`cratis/chronicle:19.64.1-development`) and the rendered backend scaffold use Chronicle 19.64.1. (#371)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.3)): Verification fails when a skill carries release history: versions outside a `Requires` line, issue or pull request numbers, source-line citations, commit hashes or history wording. `SKILL_HYGIENE_MODE=warn` reports them without failing. (#662)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.3)): The authoring rule explains how to decide what a version marks before removing it: release history goes, while the release that introduced a behavior, or one that changed it, stays as a `Requires` line. (#662)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.102.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.102.0)): Stage uses Screenplay 4.160.0, and the rendered backend scaffold uses Arc 22.88.0. (#214)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.2)): Rules that only matter for certain files now load when those files are touched, instead of in every session; rules a task needs before any file is touched, such as committing, opening a pull request or writing a work record, stay loaded in every session. Harnesses without path-scoped rules reach each scoped rule through `general.md`. (#675)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.2)): Path-scoped profile rules cover every supported language and Screenplay models, and the Pi package keeps them for single-language repositories. (#675)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.2)): Verification warns when the rule bytes a harness loads at session start grow past the current budget. (#675)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.1)): The Screenplay method and language skills, slice conformance and slice specifications, the event-model diagram, reactor and read-model specification skills, and the React page skill describe current behavior without release history, probe results, source citations or issue numbers; a minimum version appears only as a `Requires` line, naming the release that introduced the behavior, where older releases behave differently. (part of #662)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.1)): Legacy extraction lists what `cratis screenplay generate` recovers from a slice's screens: file, data bindings, title, tables and columns, actions and toolbar actions from Cratis Components, with qualified references for cross-slice actions, toolbar items and data bindings. (part of #662)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.161.0)): A query's `by` key and every supplied filter value are now checked against their concept's `validate` rules after authorization, as Arc does at runtime. An out-of-rule argument is a validation rejection with the rule's message instead of a lookup that finds nothing; a valid key that matches nothing still answers no result, and an omitted optional filter is not checked. This applies to every executable model version. (#682)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.161.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.161.0)): `PLAY0465` also reports a query rejection that is combined with `then no result`, a result, a denial or more than one query. (#682)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.0)): A command that supplies an explicit `BeforeFirst` scope for a generated target keeps working. A conflicting explicit scope for that target is rejected with `ConflictingConcurrencyScopes` before anything is appended. (#3129)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.0)): Arc requires Cratis Chronicle 19.59.4 or later. (#3129)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.0)): When several programs share an event store, each one replaces only its own declaration. Seeds added by hand, and other programs' declarations, are kept. Identical seeds declared by more than one program are applied once, in the order they were declared. Set `ProgramIdentifier` when several programs share a store (#4869)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.0)): Global seeds stored by earlier versions are cleared by the first authoritative registration after both the client and the kernel are upgraded. To clear stale development seeds without resetting the store, upgrade both, remove the seeder or scope it with `ForNamespace(...)`, then start the application once (#4869)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.0)): If a seeder cannot be activated, the client keeps the previously stored declaration instead of replacing it with an incomplete one, and logs which seeders failed (#4869)
- **cli** ([Release v3.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.62.0)): `cratis render <PATH>` now renders with the model root's persisted identities when `.screenplay/identities.json` exists, so generated event and type ids match the ones Studio and the Screenplay MCP use. **Generated output changes for roots with persisted identities**: earlier releases ignored those identities and rendered temporary ones, which diverged from the rest of the toolchain. To see the change, render the same root with the previous CLI and with this one into two folders and diff them. Roots without persisted identities render exactly as before. Existing destinations still face the stored event type id check (`CLI-RENDER-016`). See [Render options](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/blob/main/Documentation/reference/screenplay.md). (#361)
- **cli** ([Release v3.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.62.0)): For a root with persisted identities, `--name` must equal the saved application name; a different name is refused with `CLI-RENDER-018`. If the root cannot be opened (for example a pending operation or conflicting identities), render refuses with `CLI-RENDER-017` and the reason instead of falling back to temporary identities. (#361)
- **cli** ([Release v3.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.62.0)): Release-baseline warnings for PATH input now come from the same opened workspace the render uses, so their model revision always matches the rendered model. (#361)
- **cli** ([Release v3.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.62.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.99.0.
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.0)): The README's skill-authoring guidance and the skill review prompt follow the new rule; skills no longer carry version pins. (part of #674)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.94.0)): A screen binding is written only when its import resolves to a slice that declares the command or query. Imports from outside any slice directory, or from a module path that cannot be resolved, are still reported with SP0028 and left out, also when the screen's own slice declares a command or query of the same name. (#3204)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.4)): The [seeding documentation](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/event-seeding/seeding-with-csharp.mdx) now states the scope of global seeds:
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.4)): A global seed reaches every namespace of the event store, including ones created later, once.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.4)): Opening a store for a new namespace creates it, and the kernel then applies the stored global seeds. This usually happens before the application's first write, but the order is not guaranteed.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.4)): Development or sample data belongs in `ForNamespace(...)`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.4)): Removing a global seed from code does not remove the stored entry yet (part of #4869)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.3)): SQL storage records which physical database each event store, namespace and read-model set uses, in a new `DatabaseNames` table in the cluster database, and never moves a database once it is recorded. Existing deployments with short names keep their databases unchanged. An existing deployment whose PostgreSQL names were already truncated keeps the shared database where ownership is clear; where data could be stranded, the kernel stops with `DatabaseOwnershipUnresolved` and the [SQL database names](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/hosting/configuration/storage.mdx#sql-database-names) documentation describes how to move the data and record the binding. A configured cluster database name longer than the provider limit now fails startup instead of being truncated. (#4849)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.1)): The CLI bundles Screenplay 4.159.0.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.99.0)): Stage builds on Screenplay and its canonical corpus 4.159.0. Models that use ESM v17 (explicit no-event assertions and recovery redelivery, Screenplay decision 0074) are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-016` until Stage renders them (see #371). (#388)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.0)): A unit of work that has had a contributor keeps every required scope until it completes, accepts events for only one event sequence, rejects changes made from inside a contributor with `UnitOfWorkMutatedDuringEnrollment`, and refuses to commit with `UnitOfWorkEnrollmentNotificationFailed` when a contributor's `Enrolled` notification fails. Units without contributors behave as before. Custom `IUnitOfWork` implementations keep compiling and throw `UnitOfWorkEnrollmentContributorsNotSupported` only when a contributor is attached. (#4861)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): A mixed refusal is classified as authorization first, then constraint, then validation; concurrency conflicts, exceptions and unsupported outcomes are never refusals and keep failing the reaction, matching Arc reactors (#433)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.160.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.160.0)): `PLAY0268` no longer reports reaction refusal handling, and the editor no longer describes refusal branches as syntax-only (#433)
- **AI** ([Release v7.50.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.50.0)): Read models that mix kinds of computation are split by default; keeping them together needs a written reason. (part of #672)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.60.0)): Registering an opted-in webhook fails, before anything is recorded, when it names an unregistered generation or two generations of the same event type, the same rule that applies to opted-in reactors, reducers and projections (part of #4710)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.60.0)): When a pinned event cannot be released in its pinned generation, the webhook sends no request and fails the batch without acknowledging it, so it is retried instead of being skipped (part of #4710)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.97.0.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.7)): On startup the SQL storage upgrades existing per-event-source constraint tables to a unique index. On SQL Server the value column is bounded to 200 characters first; the change is skipped, never truncating data, when a table holds longer values or values stored before constraint values were hashed, and a warning names the table and links to https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4866. A table that already contains duplicate values keeps every row, keeps a non-unique index and logs a warning once per process; remove the duplicates and restart to get the unique index. (part of #3744)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.1)): Stage and the rendered backend use Arc 22.88.0, Chronicle 19.59.4 (the Stage Host image bundles `cratis/chronicle:19.59.4-development`) and Screenplay 4.156.1.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.1)): The daily package update no longer bumps the .NET reference pack that Stage embeds to check pure reducer transitions. That pack stays on its pinned version.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.91.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.91.0)): In the default scenario mode, `QueryScenario` observes Chronicle read models over the scenario's own storage, so read models built with `[ChildrenFrom<T>]`, joins and reducers emit correctly instead of failing (#3154)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.91.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.91.0)): Arc requires Cratis Chronicle 19.59.1 or later, and references Cratis.Metrics.Roslyn 7.24.0 and FluentValidation 12.2.0 as Chronicle requires (#3154)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.0)): `STAGE-ESM-004` now refuses only state-change slices without commands. `STAGE-ESM-006` still refuses expression conditions and enumeration operands in production conditions. (#360)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.4)): Agents now choose how to build a Chronicle read model with one ladder that matches the Chronicle documentation: model-bound attributes first, fluent `IProjectionFor<T>` when the mapping needs structure, and a reducer when the logic reads better as code, as a normal choice rather than a last resort. (#660)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.4)): Agents write Chronicle self-reducing read models (static `Reduce` methods on the read model, Chronicle 19.58.0 and later) as the default reducer form in .NET, use `IReducerFor<T>` when the fold needs injected services or must live apart from the model, and nominate a plain class only when the contract assembly cannot reference Chronicle. The guidance covers marker-free discovery on upgrade, near-miss warnings, replay hooks, ownership conflicts, testing, and that decision reads do not admit reducers. (#660)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.4)): Arc guidance keeps the read model, its `Reduce` methods and its queries on one record, with `RequireQueryAttribute` and `[Query]` only for Arc versions before 22.90.0. (#660)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): Every `cratis render`, at application scope and for selected scopes, including `--check`, now runs Stage's publication check against the destination before writing. A render that would change an existing Chronicle event type id, or cannot read one safely, is refused with `CLI-RENDER-016` and Stage's `STAGE-EVENT-002` diagnostic; nothing is written and `--force` does not bypass it. Pin the event's `id` to keep its stored name. (#354)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): An existing destination file that cannot be read for that check is refused instead of failing with an unhandled error. (#354)
- **cli** ([Release v3.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.60.0)): The CLI bundles Screenplay 4.154.0 and Stage 4.96.0, which brings Chronicle 19.55.2 and Arc 22.87.0 to rendered applications.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.90.0)): A static method on a `[ReadModel]` that takes the read model itself as a parameter, such as a self-reducing read model's `Reduce(event, current)`, is no longer exposed as a query endpoint or generated proxy. The rule applies the same way to runtime discovery, the TypeScript proxy generator, query metadata, Screenplay and the analyzers; marking such a method `[Query]` is reported as an error. `RequireQueryAttribute` keeps working as before. (#3239)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.90.0)): `ARCCHR0019` no longer warns that a self-parameter `Reduce` method would become a query endpoint. (#3239)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.3)): The `cratis-screenplay-toolchain` skill is rewritten as guidance for running and checking models: locating the model, the command behind each verdict and what a pass looks like, reading diagnostics, executable and renderable constructs, and traps. It no longer carries version tables, probe history or source citations; where a construct needs a minimum tool version, the reference that teaches it says so in one `Requires` line. (part of #662)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.3)): Skills that sent agents to the toolchain's version table now point to how to read the installed versions (`screenplay --version`, `cratis --version`) or to the capability reference that answers the question. (part of #662)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): `propose-source` with `formatting: "PreserveExactSource"` now writes your source text exactly as supplied, including structural edits such as an added `import` or an appended `feature`. Comments, column alignment, blank lines and line endings are kept, a replaced document keeps its BOM policy, and the proposal goes through the same parse, identity, reference and compilation checks as canonical printing. `CanonicalizeTouchedDocuments` still reprints touched documents canonically, and `propose-ast` is unchanged. (#708)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.158.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.158.0)): A `propose-source` structural change refused under `PreserveTrivia` now names `PreserveExactSource` as the way to keep the supplied text. (#708)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.97.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.97.0)): Stage builds on Screenplay and its canonical corpus 4.154.0. (#375)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.157.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.157.0)): `PLAY0268` now names only reaction refusal branches and `$refusal` values, which remain syntax-only (part of #433)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.1)): Stage no longer pins `Microsoft.Orleans.Reminders` directly. It now resolves together with `Microsoft.Orleans.Server` through `Cratis.Orleans` and the Chronicle testing packages, so automated package updates can't move it to a different Orleans version than the server. (#217)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Stage builds on Screenplay and its canonical corpus 4.152.0, Chronicle 19.55.2 and Arc 22.87.0. Generated applications, the Docker images and the spec runner pin the same Chronicle and Arc versions. (#344)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): A semantic `not empty` rule on text now rejects whitespace-only values, for both concept and command validation, following Screenplay decision 0071. (#344)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): Non-text `secret` concepts (`Int`, `Decimal`, `Bool`, `Date`, `DateTime`, `Uuid`) now render and run instead of failing with `STAGE-ESM-041`. Chronicle 19.55.2 encrypts them at rest and returns them in their original type. (#344)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.96.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.96.0)): The rendering guides now explain how event ids are chosen, which publishers enforce `STAGE-EVENT-002`, and that renaming an event without a pin still loses access to its stored events. They now recommend the Screenplay rename tool, which adds the pin. (#372)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.1)): The Stage rendering and sandbox skill reads as guidance only: how to render, what is admitted and refused with the diagnostic you will see, how to recover, and how to verify a render. Release history, probe results, source citations and per-version listings are gone, and the one version requirement is a single `Requires` line (part of #662)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.1)): Scaffold and CLI bundle version pins moved from the skill to a maintainer compatibility page (part of #662)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.156.0)): Every MCP proposal result now carries a short human `summary`, also returned as a second text block after the JSON: which documents change, which modules, features, slices, commands, events, read models, screens and reactions are added or removed, what the authoring checks reported, how many comments are dropped and whether the result is executable. (#711)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.156.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.156.0)): Proposal results explain their `authoringDiagnosticCount`: the new `authoringDiagnosticSeverities` splits it into errors, warnings and information with the distinct codes, and a note says it counts checks run while proposing, including the `PLAY0288` canonical-printing notice, while `after.readiness` counts diagnostics of the resulting source alone. (#711)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): The rules and skills no longer recommend a passive read model for command-side decisions without saying it guards nothing. Passive is current, not protected (#661)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): Corrected guidance (#661):
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): which command property Arc uses as the event source
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): when ARCCHR0002 fires
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): that `[RemoveConstraint]` also releases a `[Unique]` event type
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): what a decision guard detects when another writer has no guard
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.0)): what an empty protected commit validates
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.155.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.155.0)): A capture whose `source events` consume events from more than one origin is now refused at design time with `PLAY0687`, matching the runtime rule that one capture reads one event store; split such a capture into one capture per origin (#706)
- **AI** ([Release v7.47.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.47.1)): The shop example's `ApiKey` is identified by a separate `ApiKeyId`, because an event-source ID is plaintext metadata that cannot be encrypted; `ApiKey` stays a secret value property (part of #576)
- **AI** ([Release v7.47.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.47.1)): The generated-values reference explains how Chronicle checks generated-value freshness: a batch's scopes once, all or nothing, before the batch, and again on a storage retry that renumbers the append (part of #576)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.2)): The concurrency documentation explains how a generated identifier is guaranteed to be new with a scope that expects no prior events, how batches and storage retries check that scope, what happens on a collision, and when to use a unique constraint or a deterministic identifier instead (#4713)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.94.0)): Time-ordered generated values (`uuidv7`, `ulid` and `typeid` concepts in ESM v15 models) now take their time from the command's receipt time instead of the wall clock, in rendered applications and in the Stage Host. A value and `$context.occurred` of the same command come from the same instant. Rendered concepts implement `IGeneratableInContext<T>`, so hand-written code that called `New()` on one of them now passes a `GenerationContext`. (#376)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.94.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.94.0)): Rendered command specifications with an unpinned time-ordered value fix Arc's scenario clock and check that the value's time part matches the receipt time. (#376)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.154.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.154.0)): `screenplay baseline admit --from-esm` records the stored name that shipped ESM v16 carries for renamed events, instead of refusing every shipped event name that differs from the current pinned name; a shipped stored name that differs from the current one still refuses with `ShippedStoredNameMismatch`. (#719)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.88.0)): Validation results that Arc composes itself after stream completion use the new reason `frameworkFailure` instead of `rule` (#3058)
- **AI** ([Release v7.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.47.0)): Renaming a Screenplay event while keeping stored events readable: pin the old stored name with `id "<old name>"`, which ESM v16 now carries to renderers as the event's stored name. The skills cover the pin rules (PLAY0472), the end of the interim PLAY0686 caveat, and what Stage and the cratis CLI render today (#654)
- **AI** ([Release v7.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.47.0)): The Screenplay release baseline: run `screenplay baseline admit` in the release job and `screenplay baseline check` in CI, read the baseline through the MCP, and which event edits a baselined revision accepts; this replaces the earlier `eventNeverPersisted` guidance (#655)
- **AI** ([Release v7.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.47.0)): The standalone Screenplay tool pin and pinned language contract move to 4.153.0 (part of #655)
- **AI** ([Release v7.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.45.0)): `cratis-guard-writes` allows writes to dependency manifests, lockfiles, `global.json` and NuGet configuration by default when the file belongs to a repository whose `origin` is `github.com/Cratis/*`. Set `allowDependencyManifestWrites` in that repository's `.cratis/ai.json` to `false` to keep the block, or to `true` to allow these writes in any other repository. Generated files and `.env` files stay blocked everywhere, and `CRATIS_HOOKS_ALLOW_PROTECTED_WRITES=1` still allows a single protected write (#520)
- **AI** ([Release v7.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.45.0)): The dependency-manifest rule lets Cratis organization repositories bump versions as needed, without unrelated churn and with bumps called out in the pull request; other repositories still change them only when asked (#520)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): `screenplay_propose_property` and `screenplay_propose_rename` report each event revision's `mutability`. Without a baseline nothing changes. Revisions outside the baseline are edited in place without choosing `newGeneration` or `eventNeverPersisted`, and their renames add no pin. Baselined revisions refuse in-place edits and `eventNeverPersisted` with `BaselinedRevisionImmutable` and accept only `newGeneration`. (#686)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.153.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.153.0)): Every proposal and every apply checks the candidate against the baseline: a change to a baselined revision's stored shape, including through a concept, composite type or enum it uses, refuses with `BaselinedRevisionChanged`, a removed one with `BaselinedRevisionMissing`, and a baseline that changed since the proposal with `StaleReleaseBaseline`. An unreadable baseline blocks only proposals that change event contracts. (#686)
- **cli** ([Release v3.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.59.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.92.0, and `cratis run` starts the matching `cratis/stage:4.92.0` image.
- **cli** ([Release v3.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.59.0)): Observable collection queries render as Arc observable queries that push every change to subscribers, and snapshot collection queries now render instead of being refused, with or without a `by` key and equality filters.
- **cli** ([Release v3.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.59.0)): Screenplay `all` projection blocks whose mappings set event-source identities render as Chronicle `FromAll`; `all` blocks with literal mappings or no mappings are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-017`.
- **cli** ([Release v3.59.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.59.0)): Event sources with PascalCase names render as `{Name}EventSource` classes with a public constant per stream, and every generated stream reference uses that constant; the stored stream names Chronicle sees are unchanged. A constant that collides with another member or a C# keyword is refused with `STAGE-ESM-012`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.152.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.152.0)): Event `id` pins are validated: a pin must be an event-name identifier, every generation of an event must share one stored name, and two events in one application cannot share a stored name (PLAY0472). (#704)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.152.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.152.0)): Warning PLAY0686 is retired: event pins are carried to renderers instead of being reported as dropped, and `screenplay_propose_rename` no longer reports `eventPinWarnings` entries. (#704)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.152.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.152.0)): Generated-value freshness and fixture-shape checks in the reference evaluator now apply to models that declare concept generation, rather than to every model at ESM v15 or later, so a model that moves to a later version for an unrelated reason keeps its evaluation. (#704)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.58.0)): Upgrade effect: an existing class that does not implement `IReducer` and already has a `public static Reduce` method of exactly that shape becomes a self reducer after upgrading. A static `Reduce` that only nearly matches logs a warning and is skipped; types implementing `IReducer` or `IReducerFor<T>` keep their current behavior. With Arc, mark such a model `[ReadModel(RequireQueryAttribute = true)]` and its query methods `[Query]` so `Reduce` is not exposed as a query endpoint. (#4731)
- **Chronicle.Go** ([Release v0.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Go/releases/tag/v0.10.0)): Contracts are regenerated from Chronicle 19.56.0, and the verified decision-guard profile and the integration workflow image move from 19.32.3 to 19.56.0. The full integration suite passes against a 19.56.0 kernel.
- **AI** ([Release v7.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.44.0)): Screenplay specifications can pin a child projection event that arrives before its parent: the reference evaluator holds the child until the parent exists, and a child whose parent never arrives stays out of the read model (#648)
- **AI** ([Release v7.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.44.0)): A stream whose stored name is `All` is refused (PLAY0273), and an event `id` pin now warns (PLAY0686) because renderers do not carry it yet, so a renamed event renders under its new name (#650)
- **AI** ([Release v7.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.44.0)): `not empty` on text rejects whitespace-only values in every ESM version, while collections still need one element and identity parts are unaffected; specifications that relied on whitespace being accepted now fail (#652)
- **AI** ([Release v7.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.44.0)): The standalone Screenplay tool pin and pinned language contract move to 4.151.0 (part of #652)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.52.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.52.0)): Moved to `@cratis/chronicle.contracts` 19.56.1, filling the new `IsNotReplayable`, `IsInactive` and `GenerationDelivery` fields on registrations.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): Moved to `chronicle-contracts` 19.56.0.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.0)): Captures over public events name the events they consume with `from <Event>` lines, the same syntax as Screenplay; the inbox to read is derived from the events' originating event store (#4700)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.0)): Chronicle uses Cratis.Screenplay 4.149.0 and takes Screenplay updates again with the other scheduled package updates (#4799)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.151.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.151.0)): `not empty` on text now rejects whitespace-only text as well as null and empty text, for concept rules and command property rules alike and in every executable model version, matching Arc: whitespace is the Unicode White_Space set (U+0085 and U+00A0 count, U+FEFF does not). Specifications that relied on whitespace-only text being accepted now expect a rejection. Collections keep "at least one element", and event-source and stream-id parts still treat whitespace as a value (#680)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.92.0)): An event source whose model name is a PascalCase identifier renders as `{Name}EventSource`. Each stream with such a name becomes a public constant on that class holding its stored name, and every stream reference uses that constant: `EventStream` attributes, command stream attributes, reducer and reactor `from` filters, and generated specification routes. Chronicle sees exactly the same stored names as before. Other names render as before. (#368)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.92.0)): A stream constant that would collide with another member or a C# keyword fails `STAGE-ESM-012`. (#368)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.92.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.92.0)): The renderer guide and rendering reference warn that renaming an event in a model that already has stored data renames its Chronicle event type, until Screenplay carries the stored event name. (#368)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.150.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.150.0)): A stream whose stored name is `All` (its name, or an `id "All"` pin) is refused with PLAY0273, as the stored source name `Default` already is. Models that use it must rename the stream; events already stored under `All` read as source-only appends. This narrows what earlier releases bound. `all` and other spellings remain valid. (#700)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.0)): Arc requires Cratis Chronicle 19.54.0 or later (part of #3154)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.0)): In the default scenario mode, observing a read model built with `[ChildrenFrom<T>]` is not supported yet and fails with Chronicle's error; use decision mode (`EnableDecisionReads`) for those read models until https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4816 ships (part of #3154)
- **AI** ([Release v7.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.43.0)): The standalone Screenplay tool pin and pinned language contract move to 4.149.0 (part of #645)
- **AI** ([Release v7.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.42.0)): `ship-changes` ends with a mandatory cleanup step, which is the trigger for harnesses without hooks.
- **AI** ([Release v7.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.42.0)): The worktree rule points to the script instead of manual removal.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.55.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.55.1)): The kernel and clients build against Cratis Arc 22.86.0, Cratis Fundamentals 7.24.0, Orleans 10.4.0 and Cratis.Orleans 1.16.3, along with the other dependency updates from the reverted 2026-10-12 package update (part of #4799)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.1)): The `validate` help and the Screenplay reference list every `--check` name (including `personas`, `purposes`, `privilege` and `enum-pinning`) and the exit codes: 0 valid, 1 missing or invalid input or scope, 5 validation failed. (part of #339)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.55.0)): The kernel now accepts gRPC messages up to 100 MB by default, matching what every client allows itself to send. It was limited to 4 MB.
- **AI** ([Release v7.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.41.0)): Rendering guidance follows CLI 3.57.0 / Stage 4.89.1: observable equality filters, reaction `runs as` for role and authenticated identities, semantic observer filters and single-hop reaction append specifications now render; the refusals that remain are listed exactly as Stage emits them (part of #643)
- **AI** ([Release v7.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.41.0)): The rendered-application pin table lists the Stage 4.89.1 scaffold: Arc 22.83.0 and Chronicle 19.54.0 (part of #643)
- **AI** ([Release v7.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.41.0)): The `cratis/stage` sandbox section describes Stage 4.89.1, including Host encryption of personal-data values (part of #643)
- **AI** ([Release v7.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.41.0)): The standalone Screenplay tool pin and pinned language contract move to 4.148.0 (part of #643)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.149.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.149.0)): The `EventRoutes` canonical corpora use the stream `Archive` instead of `All`, so Cratis renderers can run them, and the observer-filter specifications assert through a projected read model that a reaction did not fire for unrouted facts or facts on another stream. (#699)
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.13.1)): `ChronicleOptions.copy()` and its constructor take one more trailing parameter, `maxReceiveMessageSize`; the existing constructor overloads are unchanged.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): The generated frontend asks observable queries for their first result (`waitForFirstResult=true`), so list screens load as before. Screens still refresh after commands; pushing live updates to the generated frontend and the Stage Host is tracked in https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/issues/366. (#278)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.90.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.90.0)): A protected observable list specification that expects rows is refused with `STAGE-ESM-011` until Arc's command scenario can observe read models (https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/issues/3221). Denial specifications still render. (#278)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.0)): The CLI bundles Stage 4.89.1 and Screenplay 4.147.1, and `cratis run` starts the matching `cratis/stage:4.89.1` image. (#345)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.0)): `cratis render` admits ESM v14 and v15: equality query filters render on observable collection queries, and concepts with a generation strategy (UUID v4/v7, ULID, TypeID, NanoID, random bytes) render with freshness scopes on generated event sources. Models newer than v15 are still refused with `CLI-RENDER-004`. (#345)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.0)): Reactions declaring `runs as` and reactions with a `from` observer filter now render through the bundled Stage instead of being refused with `STAGE-ESM-034`/`STAGE-ESM-035`; `STAGE-ESM-034` remains only for system-identity reactions that invoke commands whose authorization reads claims or uses opaque policies. (#345)
- **AI** ([Release v7.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.40.0)): Rendering guidance follows CLI 3.56.0 / Stage 4.83.0: render admission covers ESM v1 to v13, so models with UUID-concept generated values, command responses, explicit enum numbers and concept compliance (v13) render through the CLI. ESM v14 collection filters and later versions are still refused (`CLI-RENDER-004`) (#623)
- **AI** ([Release v7.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.40.0)): The refusals that remain are stated exactly as Stage 4.83.0 emits them, including event-triggered Automation limits, validated query keys and routed companion replay (#623)
- **AI** ([Release v7.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.40.0)): The rendered-application pin table lists the Stage 4.83.0 scaffold: Arc 22.78.0, Chronicle 19.45.0 and Scene 4.22.1 (#623)
- **AI** ([Release v7.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.40.0)): The `cratis/stage` sandbox section describes Stage 4.83.0: the semantic engine admits ESM v1 to v13 and runs event-triggered reactions (#623)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.89.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.89.1)): Stage, the Stage Host image (`cratis/chronicle:19.54.0-development`) and the rendered backend scaffold use Chronicle 19.54.0, which adds observable read-model queries. (part of #278)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.86.0)): Generation strategies need Screenplay ESM v15: models that use them select ESM v15, and a `MaximumExecutableModelVersion` below v15 keeps the previous output and reports the withheld strategy. In a v15 model, UUID pins must be version 4 UUIDs; other pins are left out with `SP0039` (part of #3116)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.86.0)): Factories the generator cannot read as one strategy call, generation that uses the generation context's time or tenant, and concepts with validation rules keep their legacy command and are reported with `SP0073` (part of #3116)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.86.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.86.0)): The Screenplay generator uses Cratis Screenplay 4.148.0 (part of #3116)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.89.0)): `STAGE-ESM-035` no longer applies to reactions in the semantic renderer. The legacy syntax engine still refuses observer filters with `STAGE-ESM-035`. (#297)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.88.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.88.0)): A command form element placed on a screen is now the runtime command form for its command in the position and with the id it was authored with: it takes the form the screen has for the command (or the form that follows the command's schema), keeps its authored properties such as its label, and that form is no longer appended a second time after the screen's other elements. Before, only a screen's own forms reached the runtime, at the end of the screen. (part of Cratis/Scene#7)
