- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.4)): When two versions of an application are connected at once and the newer one's observer registration replaced the older one, the observer no longer stops when the newer client disconnects: it hands over to the still-connected older client, which carries on from where processing stopped. The newest registration still wins while both are connected, and events of types only the departed client handled are not delivered to the client that takes over. (#4987)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.112.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.112.1)): `useQuery`: when two components read the same query with the same arguments, the one whose request was cancelled by the other now receives the result instead of staying "not ready" with no data. A failed request settles both components as failed, and neither keeps reporting that it is performing. `useQueryWithPaging` gets the same fix. (#3312)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.112.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.112.1)): `useQuery`: a component whose arguments, paging or sorting change no longer shares a query instance, or a cached result, with components that use the previous values, so a request for new arguments can no longer cancel or overwrite another component's request. (#3312)
- **AI** ([Release v7.69.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.69.1)): An AI pool now fails over when a member rejects a call outright, such as a 400 for a bad key, a retired model or an account with no credit. Before, the first rejection ended the whole completion and the remaining members were never tried, so one broken provider took down every agent that used the pool. The rejecting provider is remembered as failing so it ranks lower next time, and when every member rejects the call the first rejection is still reported.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.3)): Strongly consistent per-key projection reads (passive projections, stream-scoped reads, reads with the `*` key and session reads) now fold the key's events from the start on every read. Redactions, revisions and the erasure of personal data joined from another subject show up immediately. Before, a warm read kept returning the old content until the grain was deactivated. (#4844)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.77.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.77.1)): An event store of a namespace other than the default one asks the kernel whether the namespace still exists when it reconnects. If the kernel no longer lists it, the event store lets go of it instead of registering, and the client creates a new one if the namespace is used again. If the kernel cannot be asked, it registers as before (#4942).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.76.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.76.1)): `RemoveNamespace` stops listing the namespace before removing its observers, and unsubscribes and removes an observer again, up to five times, if a subscription already on its way subscribed it in between (#4942).
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.1)): Provider error bodies no longer leak secrets: the calling provider's configured key is withheld by exact value (also when echoed URL-encoded) before any pattern runs, long tokens containing `/` are withheld, and only the failed request's exact model and provider ids stay readable, so a short model id inside a longer secret no longer lets that secret through. (#575)
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.1)): A pooled chat call now treats HTTP 402 or a vendor `insufficient_quota` code as a spent quota and fails over to the next provider, instead of failing permanently. (part of #569)
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.1)): Agent-harness workers report a spent Claude Code allowance only for the known subscription-limit phrases, and a Copilot worker reads a spent quota only from its error or result events, not from the agent's own text. (part of #569)
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.1)): When the pull-request body guard cannot read a `--body-file`, it now says which path it tried, whether the file and its folder exist, the working directories and which checker script ran, instead of a bare `ENOENT`. (part of #517)
- **AI** ([Release v7.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.68.0)): The skills no longer say `cratis screenplay validate` lacks `--open-questions`; the CLI has it.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.76.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.76.0)): Returning `GetDecisionConflicts()` as a response body, for example with `Results.Conflict(...)`, no longer throws. A `DecisionConflict` now serializes with System.Text.Json as the read model identifier (`ReadModel`), the key and the conflicting event, without the CLR type (#4984)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.75.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.75.1)): `RemoveNamespace` now finds the namespace's remaining observers through the persistent reminder table and unsubscribes and removes them too, so no reminder is left to bring the namespace back (#4942).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.75.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.75.0)): A decision read refused because the kernel's projection definition did not match the client's no longer stays refused until the application restarts. Refusals are not cached, a read checks the kernel's definitions once more before refusing, and the agreement is checked again after the client connects or disconnects. Protected reads and Arc protected commands therefore recover by themselves after a kernel restart or upgrade. (#4992)
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): The tree layout of `SchemaEditor` no longer loses schema details when a schema is edited. A property the user did not change is written back exactly as it was read, including:
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): annotations such as `title`, `description` and `deprecated`
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `default`, `enum`, `const` and `examples`
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): string, number, array and object constraints
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `integer` and nullable type arrays
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `format` values other than `date` and `time`
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `$ref` and composition keywords
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): array `items` in every form
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): the order of keys and of `required`
- **Components** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Changing a property's type in either layout now drops only the keywords that no longer apply to the new type: annotations stay, and constraints stay when the new type belongs to the same family. Before, the tree layout dropped everything and the table layout left constraints from the old type behind. (#426)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.2)): The 19.74.0 and 19.74.1 kernels fail to start (`Unable to resolve service for type 'System.String' while attempting to activate 'Cratis.Chronicle.Configuration.StorageConnectionResolution'`). The storage connection resolution is now created only from the configured storage, so the kernel starts again with MongoDB and with SQL storage. (#4994)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.25.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.25.1)): The type discovery generator no longer emits `typeof` expressions for types nested in open generic types (for example `Key<T>.Part`) declared in referenced Cratis packages, which failed the build with CS0246 (#1188)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.134.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.134.0)): `slot side` no longer replaces the page in the primary outlet (Cratis/Stage#456).
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.18.1)): A job that was stopped and then resumed no longer stays `Running` forever when the silo dies before the resume has restarted its steps. Automatic recovery now restarts the steps that were stopped before the resume, using the job's own status history rather than clocks; a durable stop still wins. (#77)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.1)): **Event stores first created by a read now get their kernel projections.** Until now, if a read reached a new event store before the store was set up, the store was created without its kernel projections, including the event-type statistics projection. A common trigger is the .NET client's identity-drift check, which runs before artifact registration. Those projections then never handled any event, and waiting for completion after an append could stall on them until the kernel restarted.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.1)): **Setup:** it now runs exactly once per event store, even when several silos ensure the store at the same time.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.1)): **Existing stores:** stores that were already affected are repaired the next time a client connects.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.1)): **Reconnects:** reconnecting to a store that is already set up does no extra projection work.
- **Components** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The tree layout keeps every `x-` vendor keyword when the schema is edited: on properties, on array `items` and in nested objects. Root keywords, `definitions` and other root keys are kept, and so is the original key order. Before this, only `x-concept` and `x-key` survived an edit. Changing a property's type drops the annotations of its old array items. (#425)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): SQL table, column and index lookups only consider the schema the connection uses. Before, a table of the same name in another schema of a shared database could be taken for Chronicle's own. (part of #4946)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.74.0)): MongoDB clients are no longer shared between different credentials on the same servers. (part of #4950)
- **cli** ([Release v3.70.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.70.0)): The changed-profile planning spec follows the corpus' `root template` line instead of the legacy `layout` line it no longer contains.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.67.0)): Stage's `slot side` item no longer replaces the page in the primary outlet (Cratis/Stage#456).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.130.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.130.0)): Production runtime scenes still carry no sample data (asserted for the new profile members too).
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.1)): When a member removes their `cast.json`, their directory card falls back to GitHub data instead of keeping the old title and tagline (#12)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.1)): A merged pull request total that was not fetched from GitHub is labelled self-reported, never live (#12)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.1)): Taglines with accent markup show as text on directory cards and in the terminal, without literal asterisks (#12)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.1)): Stat numbers keep their suffix on the same line, GitHub-only intros read as sentences, the search field shows a focus ring, and the 404 heading fits on small screens (#12)
- **Cast** ([Release v0.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Cast/releases/tag/v0.2.1)): Verification badges show the since date as month and year, matching the directory cards (#12)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.200.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.200.0)): Spec line expectations of the root template specs (they pointed one line off) and the PLAY0799 spec now follows Scene: a dialog template that declares no `scopes` is not restricted at feature scope.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.31.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.31.1)): A signed-in user arriving on `/register` is now passed through to the lobby instead of starting another registration and being sent to sign in again, so the sign-up wizard opens after the provider login.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): MongoDB clients created through `IMongoDBClientFactory` are no longer shared between different credentials on the same server. (part of #3315)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.109.0)): The scoped `IMongoClient` and `IMongoDatabase` no longer capture the client factory in static fields, which leaked state across hosts. (part of #3315)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.73.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.73.1)): The Workbench keeps the selected namespace when the page is refreshed or a link is shared, because the address is now the only source of truth for the event store and namespace.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.73.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.73.1)): Switching namespace now restarts the page you are on, so open queries in event sequences and read models show the data of the namespace you picked.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.108.0)): ARCCHR0032 no longer reports `[ReadProtection(ReadProtectionMode.On)]` on a `[ProtectedDecision]` command as redundant: `On` changes how validators are built, protects plain read-model parameters and changes the `ReadChanged` refusal (#3330)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.108.0)): A read through an unsupported reducer now names what made it unprotectable instead of telling you to wait for reducer support (#3330)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.108.0)): The docs explain that refusal messages with status 500 reach clients only in Development or with `ExposeExceptionDetails`, show how a frontend handles `ReadChanged` and `ReadIncomplete` through `reasonDetail`, and correct the monthly-limit query and the overbooking repair examples (#3330)
- **cli** ([Release v3.69.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.69.0)): `cratis render --package-set` of an application that uses a domain blueprint no longer fails with `CLI-RENDER-009`/`CLI-RENDER-002`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.197.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.197.1)): A reaction `invokes` mapping such as `= $eventSourceId` (or `$eventContext.eventSourceId`) now binds to the triggering event's source instead of failing with PLAY0268, in both compilers and in the reference evaluator. Models that do not use it keep their executable-model bytes and version. (#790)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.197.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.197.0)): Part of Cratis/Screenplay#825; epic Cratis/Scene#157. Validator checks against Scene (template/option existence) follow with #826.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.60.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.60.0)): The SC4 acceptance asked for a Storybook story; it was missing from #165.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.72.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.72.1)): A reactor or reducer whose stream fails with `ClientIsNotConnected` after a kernel node restarts now makes the whole client reconnect, with a new connection id and every reactor and reducer registered again, instead of retrying the same stale registration every two seconds indefinitely (#4941).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Menu entries, crumbs, logo and user-menu entries were dead hash links in a preview (a click only changed the hash, or nothing); the sidebar/topbar items with no target did nothing and gave no hint.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.58.0)): Entries pointing at a screen the host does not render are now marked `navTargetMissing` (N8) instead of silently doing nothing.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.125.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.125.0)): `cratis render` of an application using a domain blueprint no longer fails with `CLI-RENDER-009` (dependency on `Cratis.Blueprint.Default` / `PrimeReact` unsatisfied) or `CLI-RENDER-002`.
- **Scene** ([Release v4.53.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.53.1)): `ApprovedPackageSetJson` refused the `blueprint` and `sampleData` members of a domain blueprint package's manifest, so the manifest of `@cratis/scene.blueprint.*` had to be hand-edited before a host could approve it. Both are design-time only: they are now read and left out of the canonical runtime document (sample data never reaches a rendered application). Covered by `when_reading_a_blueprint_manifest`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.2)): The Aspire hosting client no longer depends on MessagePack 2.5.302, which has a known vulnerability (GHSA-qhrr-8q5h-9q3h). It uses 2.5.307, so the NuGet packages build and publish again
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): A persisted `Running` job whose silo crashed is resumed: its unfinished steps are started again under the same job id, scope and namespace, completed steps stay completed and a stopped job stays stopped. Recovery runs when a silo becomes active and, on one surviving silo, when a cluster member is declared dead. A step that was interrupted runs again from the start, so step work must be safe to repeat. (#63)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): `Resume()` no longer reports `JobAlreadyRunning` for a job whose steps have no live work, and a job interrupted while it was still being prepared returns an explicit failure instead of success. (#63)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): A `Rehydrate()` that arrives while a rehydration is in progress now runs another pass afterwards instead of being dropped.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): A step outcome that reaches a job after it already completed no longer changes its progress or recreates its stored state.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.71.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.71.1)): Switching namespace in the Workbench now takes effect in the Sequences query editor and in Read Models. Previously an open query kept running against the namespace it was opened in, so a namespace with events could show "No events found"
- **Stage** ([Release v4.121.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.121.1)): Generated code no longer silently leaves out a validation rule whose kind Stage does not know. The renderer, the specification executor and the semantic host now refuse such a model with STAGE-ESM-005, naming the rule kind, instead of rendering or running it without that rule. (part of #448)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.121.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.121.1)): The default sandbox engine (`cratis run` without the semantic engine) now refuses a model that uses an unknown rule kind, with a clear reason, instead of ignoring the rule. The rules it already documents as not enforced are unchanged. (part of #448)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.51.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.51.0)): The page header of the foundation shell no longer overflows a phone when it carries several actions: the header and its action row wrap (found by the Chromium audit of the ticket page's four quick actions).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): `propose-ast` could not add the first child of a member the node JSON leaves out while empty: the first `navigation` region of a module, the first `button` of a toolbar, the first sub-item of an item and the first contribution of a screen were refused with "Unknown typed syntax member". The edit now accepts any member the node kind declares.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.188.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.188.0)): The TypeScript compiler reported `PLAY0022` or `PLAY0024` for a module or feature `template <Name>` assignment the C# language accepts. It now skips the line like the other constructs it does not model.
- **cli** ([Release v3.67.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.67.0)): `cratis render` no longer publishes sample data. The `scene.json` it writes is a content file of the generated application that the .NET SDK copies to the publish folder, and it carried the authored sample data packs and the profile's selection; it is now written without them (spec SD6: production output never includes sample data).
- **Scene** ([Release v4.45.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.45.0)): A `globalSearch` and the `dataList` sharing its address parameter did not follow each other (the address was replaced in place, which raises no event), so typing in a list's search box did nothing. Components writing an address parameter now tell every component using it.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.107.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.107.1)): With `CratisProxiesUseSourceFileAsOutputFile` enabled, generated proxies now import each type from the file generated for that type's full name. Previously, when two types shared a simple name in different namespaces (for example a type from a referenced package and an unrelated type in the application), an import could point at the other type's file and the frontend failed to compile with TS2307. (#3322)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.119.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.119.1)): The specification executor behind `cratis run` and the Stage spec runner now runs commands with composite inputs instead of reporting them as unsupported. Nested concept rules are checked in the same order and with the same messages as Screenplay's reference, malformed composite inputs are rejected before any rule runs, and only composites that are present are validated. (part of #441)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.119.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.119.1)): The renderer, the specification executor and the semantic host now decide which composite values they accept from one shared rule, so a model the renderer accepts is no longer refused when you run its specifications. (part of #441)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.2)): Commands with `==` or `!=` validation rules on a concept with named values now render instead of being refused with STAGE-ESM-005. The generated code compares enum members, including for optional concepts, where an absent value passes. (#420)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.2)): Requirements that compare a named-value concept with one of its members, or with another property of the same concept, now render, including combinations with `and` and `or`. Ordering, other rule kinds, collections and comparisons across different concepts are still refused with a specific reason. (#420)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.183.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.183.0)): A `matches` rule no longer rejects a valid value, and a generation check no longer reports PLAY0689, because the host was too slow to finish matching within a second. (#800)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.1)): Commands whose composite property contains an optional validated concept now render instead of being refused with STAGE-ESM-005, as long as every rule on that concept accepts an absent value. This includes optional parent composites, collections of composites and recursive types. A concept with `not empty`, a nested collection or named values is still refused. (#418)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.1)): A composite value that omits an optional member now renders that member as `null` instead of failing to render. (#418)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.116.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.116.1)): Specifications that use composite values now render. A rejection specification whose composite input lists members in a different order from the type's declaration is refused, because the reported first failure would otherwise differ from the model. (#418)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.115.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.115.0)): The legacy renderer refuses validation rules it cannot render instead of writing a `// TODO` comment into the generated validator. (part of #414)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.181.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.181.1)): `TenantId` now formats as its value, so a `generate rule` body that interpolates `context.Tenant` produces the tenant identifier instead of `TenantId { Value = … }` (#798)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.113.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.113.1)): The default sandbox engine now checks `min` and `max` on numbers by value instead of by text length, so `quantity min 1` rejects `0` and `amount max 100` rejects `99999`. Text bounds still measure length. (#425)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.113.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.113.1)): `not empty` on a nested property such as `customer.address.name` no longer rejects every command in the default sandbox engine. (#425)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.113.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.113.1)): The default sandbox engine no longer half-enforces `all >` and `all >=`: they are dropped, like equality and rules with non-literal operands, and rules whose path crosses a collection are skipped instead of refusing every payload. The sandbox documentation now lists which rules the default engine enforces and which it ignores. (#425)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.2)): Parsing a valid model no longer fails with a regular-expression timeout when the host is busy. The compiler's own patterns now run on .NET's linear-time regular expression engine with no wall-clock timeout, so they stay safe against hostile input without depending on how fast the machine is. Parsing the samples is also about twice as fast. (#796)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.2)): Import globs are matched folder by folder without regular expressions, so a glob with many wildcards or `**` segments can no longer exhaust memory or the stack. (#796)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.113.0)): The semantic engine (`cratis run` with the semantic runtime, and the Stage Host) no longer reports Screenplay's informational notes as blocking refusals. Report-only metadata such as descriptions, personas, documentation and deferred screens used to appear among the `Unsupported(Plan) model:` refusals, burying the real errors. Only errors block now. (#430)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.112.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.112.1)): Planning a model whose specification pins a value for a generated concept with an opaque (code) rule no longer throws `InvalidSemanticContract`; the specification is refused with `STAGE-ESM-005`, naming the concept and the rule, like the concept itself. (#429)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.2)): When the commit inside `Chronicle.decide/2` fails, for example because the kernel is unreachable, the unit of work is rolled back. Later appends from the same process go to the event log instead of being buffered into the failed unit of work and lost. (#161)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.2)): `Chronicle.decide/2` and `with_unit_of_work/2` stop the unit of work's process when they return, so a long-lived caller no longer collects one linked process per call. (#161)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.1)): The client's Logger filter now removes the API key, the OAuth bearer token and the gRPC and Mint state that holds request headers from `gen_server`, `proc_lib` and supervisor crash reports, so you no longer need an application-level filter. This covers the Mint connection process, which kept the API key in its HPACK table, and `GRPC.Client.Connection`. See [Crash reports](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/blob/main/Documentation/connections/crash-reports.md). (#79)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.14.1)): Warning and error log text no longer contains the API key, password or tokens the client authenticated with. (#79)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.1)): The executable binder accepts queries with the same name in different slices, such as Arc's `All` and `GetAll` on several read models, instead of refusing them with PLAY0268. A specification's `then query` and `when query` resolve like other references: its own slice first, then the enclosing feature, module and document, or a qualified name; a reference that still matches several queries is reported as ambiguous (PLAY0198) at that reference. (#624)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.180.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.180.1)): A specification that asserts an authorized query declared in a later slice now requires `given caller`, like any other authorized specification (PLAY0389 was skipped for such queries). (#624)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.1)): Generated specification classes and test names now split single-letter words and the end of an acronym. For example, `RegisteringAGreeting` renders as `when_registering_a_greeting` instead of `when_registering_agreeting`, and `HTTPRequest` still renders as `http_request`. Re-rendering renames the affected generated specification classes and files. (#417)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.109.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.109.0)): Stage decides whether generated values are guarded for freshness and shape by whether the model declares a generation strategy, matching the reference evaluator, instead of by ESM version. Models that only use implicit UUID generation are no longer guarded. (#393)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.173.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.173.0)): Generated-concept rule proofs in MCP declaration details use the same UTC assessment year as the compiler, so date patterns get the same proven, refuted or unproven status as the PLAY0688/PLAY0689 diagnostics. (part of #618)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.2)): `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` inside the agent harness images exit cleanly on multi-project solutions: the images disable MSBuild node reuse and the build server, so leftover worker nodes no longer keep build output open and hang an agent or the harness reading it. (#714)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.68.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.68.0)): Events revised on SQL storage now report their revised generation to clients, so a client reading over gRPC can tell the content was revised instead of using the stale stored generation (#4888)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.1)): The agent harness always delivers a result Studio accepts: non-string `gaps` and `selfChecks` items written by the agent are converted to text, nulls are dropped, long text and long lists are capped to Studio's limits, and verbose advisory metadata is trimmed to fit with a marker saying how much was dropped. Usage and correlation values are forwarded byte for byte. (#711)
- **AI** ([Release v7.56.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.56.1)): A result that still cannot meet Studio's contract fails the worker with the clause it breaks, instead of being rejected by Studio without a reason. (#711)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.171.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.171.0)): A specification that appended an event a capture over events reads, and expected `then no events`, passed even though Chronicle would append; the capture now receives the event and its appends are compared. (#483)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.2)): The Screenplay generator no longer emits a model that fails binding when a command's generated value has a generation strategy it cannot recover (`SP0073`) and that value feeds a required event property. Productions that need the value for a required property are withheld (`SP0012`). Mappings to optional properties are omitted with a warning. Dependent responses, conditions, destinations, routes, operations and scenarios are withheld with their existing diagnostics. Everything independent of the value is kept, and the command falls back to its `file` reference only when no production survives. (#3219)
- **cli** ([Release v3.64.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.64.0)): `cratis run` no longer reports a model as ready when Stage refuses to run it. It waits for Stage's status, lists every refusal issue, stops the container and exits 5. A routed model refused by the default engine (`STAGE-ESM-030`) suggests `--engine semantic`. (#368)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.100.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.100.1)): The Screenplay generator no longer drops an xUnit `[Theory]` that builds a local `ReadModelScenario`, `CommandScenario`, `EventScenario` or `ReactorScenario` without a trace: it is now reported with Information `SP0076` like every other omitted scenario theory, so the generated document accounts for every specification it leaves out. (#3295)
- **AI** ([Release v7.53.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.53.1)): The technical-examples guidance on version notes now uses a placeholder version in its example, so the skill itself stays free of concrete release numbers.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.103.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.103.2)): The plan-from-sources reference links to Screenplay's tool page again; the links pointed at a page that does not exist.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.168.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.168.0)): The repair-capabilities schema lists PLAY0688 among the repairable diagnostics.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.103.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.103.1)): A freshly scaffolded application's frontend now type-checks and builds before any slice is rendered. The scaffold's entry point loads the Scene module only when it exists, and until the first render creates it, shows "Nothing to show yet" instead of failing with `Cannot find module '../src/stage'`. Fully rendered applications load their Scene module exactly as before. (#399)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.52.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.52.1)): A read-model field that no event has set yet now reads as the value the class declares (for example `null`), instead of `undefined`, so a projection and a reducer give the same result for the same history. An absent array still reads as an empty array. Requires `@cratis/fundamentals` 7.24.1. (#251)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.24.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.24.1)): `JsonSerializer` no longer overwrites a class's default value with `undefined` when a field is missing from the input; the field keeps what the constructor gave it. Fields present in the input, explicit `null` values and missing collections (which become empty arrays) behave as before. (#1184)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.1)): A `CommandScenario` that seeds events for an event source and pins the same id as a guarded generated value, without `UseDecisionReads()`, now fails fast with `GeneratedIdentifierCollisionRequiresDecisionReads`. Before, the command silently succeeded, because the default mode does not put seeded events in the event log. The fresh-identifier and generated-value testing docs show the working `new CommandScenario<T>().UseDecisionReads()` setup. (#3280)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.1)): Resetting a development kernel, and the reset between integration specs, now also clears the in-memory appended-event queues and live observers. Previously they survived the reset, so a projection registered at runtime in a later spec could receive events from an earlier one, skip its own events and return no read model. A reset that cannot clear them now fails instead of continuing with stale delivery. (#4815)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.63.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.63.0)): The CHR0054 reference and the reactor side-effects documentation no longer say that a synchronous custom side-effect type cannot be used: the runtime accepts it when a registered handler claims the type (#4882)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.4)): The .NET client sends the global event seeds discovered from `ICanSeedEvents` once per event store on each connection, instead of again for every namespace store it opens. Seeds added through `ForNamespace(...)` and seeds queued by hand are still sent every time (part of #4869)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.91.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.91.1)): The Cratis.Arc NuGet packages for 22.91.0 were never published. This release publishes them. In the default scenario mode, `QueryScenario` observes Chronicle read models built with `[ChildrenFrom<T>]`, joins and reducers, and Arc requires Cratis Chronicle 19.59.1 or later.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.3)): On PostgreSQL, an event store and its namespace no longer share one database when their names are longer than PostgreSQL's 63-byte limit. Long database names are now a readable prefix plus a hash and stay distinct, so every namespace migration runs and observers no longer fail with `relation "FailedPartitions" does not exist`. On SQL Server, names longer than 128 characters are created the same way instead of failing. (#4849)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.3)): A migration that reports "already exists" while migrations are still pending now fails with `DatabaseMigrationIncomplete` instead of marking the database as migrated. (#4849)
- **cli** ([Release v3.61.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.61.1)): `cratis screenplay baseline check` and `cratis render`'s release-baseline warnings for PATH input no longer refuse a model root whose `.screenplay` folder has mode `0755`, which is what a git checkout creates. They previously failed with `MetadataPermissions` in fresh clones and CI. Folders that are group- or other-writable are still refused. (#360)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.99.0)): `SemanticModelLoader` and `CratisRendering.PlanFrom` accept a model root's own `.screenplay/identities.json` as `CatalogPath`, the file the Screenplay MCP and `screenplay baseline admit` write, as well as a bare identity catalog. Stage reads it through Screenplay's public `ScreenplayModelRoot` and `PersistedIdentityStateSerializer`. Loading a whole root also computes the release-baseline assessment for the same workspace that is rendered, so `STAGE-BASELINE` warnings work from a real model root. A root that cannot be opened (pending recovery, conflicting identity state, unreadable metadata) reports `STAGE-BASELINE-003` or `STAGE-BASELINE-004` without throwing. (#388)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.99.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.99.0)): In model-root mode, `.play` files under folders Screenplay does not read (`.git`, `.ai-work`, `bin`, `obj`, `node_modules`) are left out of folder selections, as `screenplay baseline check` does. A file named explicitly that the root does not read is refused with `STAGE-PLAN-001` and is never silently dropped. (#388)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.1)): Read-model watchers and observable queries are no longer notified when a reducer fails to fold an event. Previously they received the state from before the failure, and were told the read model was removed when the first event of a batch failed; successful folds, including removals, notify as before. (#4817)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.62.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.62.1)): Events forwarded from another event store's outbox into the inbox now keep their tags, so tag-filtered reactors, reducers and projections observing the inbox receive them. Events forwarded before upgrading stay untagged and are not backfilled. (#4823)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.2)): Generated applications now report healthy at `/healthz` only after Chronicle has registered their read models, projections, reactors and reducers. Before this, a client or test harness that queried as soon as the host turned healthy could get HTTP 500 `ReadModelNotFound`. Stage, its Docker images and the generated scaffold now pin Chronicle 19.59.6, which includes Cratis/Chronicle#4872. (#387)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.98.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.98.2)): The compliance and rendering references no longer claim that `[NotAudited]` protects event payloads. It keeps a command input out of the command causation chain; `[Encrypted]` or `[PII]` protects the value on the event. (part of #175)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.159.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.159.0)): `screenplay baseline check` and other read-only opens of a model root no longer refuse the `.screenplay` directory a git checkout creates (`0755`). Reads now refuse only a directory other users can write; writes and recovery still require owner-only access. (#742)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.7)): Unique constraints on SQL storage are now settled by the database: two concurrent appends claiming the same value no longer both succeed. Per-event-source constraint tables get a unique index on the value on PostgreSQL, SQL Server and SQLite, and a collision is reported as a constraint violation instead of leaving two holders. (#3744)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.7)): On SQL storage, moving a claim to a value another event source already holds is rejected and keeps the original claim, instead of losing it. (part of #3744)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.6)): The ASP.NET Core readiness health check (`chronicle`, tagged `ready`) reports healthy only once the client is connected and artifact registration for the configured event store has succeeded, so a readiness probe no longer routes traffic before reactors, projections and constraints are registered. Until registration completes, or when it fails, the check reports its configured failure status and lists the failed artifacts. Apps that turn off `AutoDiscoverAndRegister` and do not run registration themselves stay healthy once connected. See [health checks](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/clients/dotnet/health-checks.md) (#4872)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.5)): Appending a previous event generation declared with `[EventTypeGenerationFor<T>]` no longer fails the build with CHR0001. Both `Append` and `AppendMany` accept either marker, and the diagnostic message and the [CHR0001 reference](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/blob/main/Documentation/code-analysis/CHR0001.mdx) now name both, with an example of appending a previous generation (#4862)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.2)): The worktree cleanup hook no longer deletes a merged worktree that holds work records: a worktree with a non-empty `.ai-work/` is kept and reported as "has untracked work records (.ai-work/)". Removing it takes an explicit `--include-work-records` run by hand; the automatic hooks never pass it. Locked worktrees (`git worktree lock <path>`) are always kept and reported as locked, and the docs name locking as the way to pin a long-lived worktree. (#670)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.156.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.156.1)): The event model board shows only the appended event in the WHEN row of a state-view or automation specification, and the expected read model in its THEN row, instead of every event the projection consumes. Specification titles are the specification name; read model fixtures the board does not draw stay in the title. (#724)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.4)): Stopping a kernel silo now waits for in-flight projection catch-up and replay work and writes its buffered MongoDB bulk writes within the shutdown budget, instead of leaving writes that can land after the silo reported it had stopped; writes cut off by the deadline are logged with their observer and partitions and recorded as failed partitions where time allows (#4790)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.3)): The in-memory event store enforces unique constraints across event sources: a second event source claiming a value another source holds is rejected as a duplicate and the first claim is kept, a rejected move keeps the old claim, and releasing a claim frees the value (part of #3744)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.2)): Appending a batch with two concurrency scopes for the same event source no longer fails with a generic error: identical scopes are merged and conflicting ones are rejected with a clear append error (#4712)
- **AI** ([Release v7.47.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.47.0)): Corpus verification recognizes nested Screenplay commands such as `screenplay baseline admit` from the pinned contract (part of #655)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.59.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.59.1)): Reindexing unique constraints over an event history that already contains duplicate values completes instead of failing on the first duplicate: the first claimant in event log order keeps the value and each later duplicate claim is logged and skipped (part of #3744)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.93.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.93.0)): A rendered `all` projection no longer gives a property it does not map a default value (such as `0`, `false` or an empty UUID) on read models it creates for unrelated events, where Screenplay leaves the property unset. Rendering now fails with `STAGE-ESM-017` unless every such property is optional, text or a single object. (part of #117)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.2)): The frontend `notEmpty` rule now treats text as empty exactly when it contains only Unicode whitespace, the same set the backend uses, so a value such as U+0085 or U+FEFF is accepted or rejected identically on the client and the server. (#3224)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.2)): An observable query whose arguments fail validation now delivers the validation results to the subscriber as an invalid query result and ends the subscription, over WebSocket and Server-Sent Events, in direct and hub mode, instead of a bare error message or a failed connection. Plain query requests and the React query hooks already surfaced these results. Controller-based observable queries over a direct WebSocket are tracked in https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/issues/3230. (#3225)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.58.0)): Passive reducer reads now apply the reducer's tag, event source and stream filters, so on-demand state matches the state the active reducer builds. (#4785)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.58.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.58.0)): `ReadModelScenario` now applies reducer tag filters and keeps the tags, event source type and stream metadata of seeded events, so filtered reducers produce the same state in specifications as in production. (#4786)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.1)): `CHR0024` no longer reports a variant property that is populated by the variant's `[EntersOn<T>]` event or by a `[GlobalFor<T>]` handler of its group, including the documented shared-handler example (#4831)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.1)): `ReadModelScenario` removes the entity from the other variants when it enters a fluent variant, the same as for model-bound variants and under a running kernel (#4832)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.1)): `ReadModelScenario` now shows the value of a variant's non-entering event that was appended before the entering event once the variant is created, as a running kernel does (#4833)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.1)): The variants and `ReadModelScenario` documentation now describes early joins, shared mappings and testing variants. See [Model-bound variants](https://cratis.io/chronicle/projections/model-bound/variants/) (#4834)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.0)): Captures over public events that do not name a `sequence` no longer fail every batch with an error saying they do not read from their inbox (#4700)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.56.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.56.1)): With `Workbench:BasePath` set, opening Read Models no longer redirects to a URL without the prefix, which broke reloads and shared links behind a path-routed proxy (#4830)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.56.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.56.1)): Navigation everywhere in the Workbench now follows the base path, including the breadcrumb, the sidebar and the redirects to and from the sign-in page.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.87.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.87.1)): A command returning events for several event sources (`EventForEventSourceId` collections, including mixed plain and wrapped events) now appends them as one batch when no command transaction is active, so a refused event no longer leaves the events before it appended while the command reports failure. Per-event routing, subject, occurrence time and tags are kept. When one event source id receives events on several streams and the command declares no concurrency guard, the events still append one at a time, because a batch can carry only one guard per event source id (https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4818). (#3216)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.55.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.55.2)): Projection catch-up and replay on MongoDB no longer lose writes when a kernel silo stops or crashes mid-catch-up: progress is persisted only once every silo has confirmed its buffered writes are stored, and a lost or failed write window makes the job step resume from its last durable position instead of skipping the events (#4814)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.149.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.149.1)): The reference evaluator no longer fails a specification when a child projection event arrives before its parent instance: like Chronicle, it holds the child until the parent exists and then applies it with its original event values, at any nesting depth and across `given`, `when` and command commits; a child whose parent never arrives stays out of the read model, and an ambiguous parent still fails (part of #132)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.149.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.149.1)): Removing a child, deferred or not, applies the ancestors' `every` mappings the way Chronicle does (part of #132)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.1)): `cratis screenplay validate --help` printed the description and examples and then failed with a markup error before listing the options; the full help, including `--scope`, `--check`, `--executable` and `--warnings-as-errors`, is shown again. (part of #339)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.1)): `--warnaserror`, the standalone `screenplay` tool's spelling, is accepted as an alias of `--warnings-as-errors`; before, it was silently ignored and a model with warnings passed. Any other unknown option now fails with exit code 5 instead of being ignored. (part of #339)
- **cli** ([Release v3.57.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.57.1)): An unknown `--check` name fails with exit code 5 and a message listing the valid check names, instead of exit code 255. (part of #339)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.55.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.55.0)): A client call carrying more than 4 MB, such as appending a large event or a client-side reducer whose read model had grown past it, no longer fails with `ResourceExhausted` (#4821)
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.13.1)): Messages from the kernel larger than 4 MB no longer fail with `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`: the client now accepts 100 MB by default, like the other Chronicle clients (Cratis/Chronicle#4821)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.51.1)): Messages from the kernel larger than 4 MB no longer fail with `ResourceExhausted`: the client now allows 100 MB in both directions by default, like the other Chronicle clients (Cratis/Chronicle#4821)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.54.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.54.1)): Events with encrypted (secret) properties of non-string types, such as `int` or `bool` concepts marked for subject, namespace or global encryption, can be appended and read back with their original values and types; previously the append failed (#4813)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.89.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.89.0)): A routed `when append` with a UUID or composite stream identifier now generates the stream-identifier type its specification needs. A slice-scoped render now includes the event-source classes that its filters and appended routes refer to. (#297)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.85.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.85.1)): A command that returns a collection of events for one event source with a compliance subject now appends them as one batch: if any event is refused, none is appended and the command reports the refusal, instead of leaving the earlier events in the event log. The subject, routing, tags and concurrency scope of each event are preserved. (#3187)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.84.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.84.0)): The Screenplay generator states a Chronicle unique constraint with every event it covers: all `On<T>` targets and their properties, and every `RemovedWith<T>` release, are read and stated together. A constraint with any part the generator cannot read exactly (an unread target, an aliased or conditional release, settings Screenplay cannot express) is left out as a whole with `SP0017` instead of being stated with fewer events than Chronicle enforces. Events named only by additional targets or releases are imported like the constraint's first event. (#3188)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.147.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.147.1)): From ESM v15, the reference evaluator no longer rejects a generated command for reusing its identifier just because an earlier fact has no event source; only earlier facts on the same event source count (part of #616)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.83.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.83.0)): On a narrow window the menu toggle changed its label and the navigation never opened, in a live Stage and in a generated application alike. Scene's blueprint stylesheet parked the sidebar off-canvas with a rule the open state could not out-rank; Scene 4.22.1 fixes the rule, and the Stage frontend, the contracts and the pinned frontend package set of generated applications move to it. (part of #237)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.82.0)): The Screenplay generator no longer drops `MenuItem` and `MenuItems` imported on their own from Cratis Components without a diagnostic, and it now names why each unread menu item, route navigation or after-success callback (`CommandDialog onSuccess`) is not stated, keeping navigation after a command succeeds separate from gestures that open its input. (#3049)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.82.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.82.0)): Generated command proxies type a collection response as an array: a command returning `IEnumerable<ProjectSummary>` now exposes `ProjectSummary[]` in `Command<…>` and `useCommand<…>`, matching what the client already deserializes. (#3174)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.53.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.53.3)): A nullable concept over `string` or `bool` on a read model is now marked nullable in the read-model schema, so an unset value on a `[Passive]` read model stays `null` instead of becoming a default such as `false`. Event type schemas are unchanged. (#4081)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.81.0)): Stream-scoped decision reads (`IDecisionReads.Get<T>`, `GetDetached<T>` and `AdmitForStream<T>` with an event source id, stream type and stream id) work inside Arc commands with the same protection, caching and enrollment as key-based decision reads, instead of being refused (#3126)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.81.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.81.0)): The decision-read documentation describes `[Unprotected]` commands consistently: their decision reads are unguarded, not refused (#3208)
- **Scene** ([Release v4.22.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v4.22.1)): The narrow-window (`max-width: 991px`) rule that parks `.layout-sidebar` off-canvas has the same specificity as the `.layout-mobile-active .layout-sidebar` rule that opens it, and comes later, so the open state never won. A `.layout-wrapper.layout-mobile-active .layout-sidebar` rule inside the media query now out-ranks the parked sidebar, so the toggle brings the navigation into the window on every application built on the default blueprint, including a live Stage. (part of #75)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.53.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.53.2)): A child join applied by the in-memory storage, which `Cratis.Chronicle.Testing` and in-memory kernels use, now updates every matching child in place, as the MongoDB and SQL sinks do. Before, it replaced the whole read model with the joined child alone, dropping the root's other properties and sibling children. (#4531)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.53.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.53.2)): Regression specifications confirm that a projection combining `From` with `FromAll` materializes an instance for an event from an unrelated event source the same way in memory and in a running kernel. (#4266)
- **cli** ([Release v3.54.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.54.0)): `cratis render --scope` on a module or feature whose slices were all removed now checks only application-level declarations, so errors in unrelated slices no longer block removing the scope's previously rendered files. Application-level errors still refuse the render and leave the published output untouched. (#334)
- **AI** ([Release v7.38.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.38.3)): The Claude Code tool server no longer breaks a running server on the same port when a server that never started is disposed, and a failed start now retries on a new port instead of failing with a disposed listener; this removes an intermittent failure where a tool call was lost and the turn silently fell back to another pool member (#638)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.53.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.53.1)): A children projection identified by a nested member, such as `.IdentifiedBy(child => child.Identity.Id)`, now updates and removes the matching child instead of adding a new child for every event. This works in a running kernel on MongoDB, SQL and in-memory storage, and in `ReadModelScenario`. (#4779)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.80.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.80.0)): The live Stage no longer silently renders a model whose profile names a custom package as if the package were not there: without the package it says so instead of rendering a placeholder.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.145.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.145.1)): `propose-property` treats `seed` event values as fixtures. Renaming a property rewrites its seed assignments instead of leaving them stale, removing it removes them, and adding a required property writes `fixtureValue` into every seed entry of that event or refuses with `FixtureValueRequired` listing them. `find-references` and `dependencies` with `kind: "Property"` list seed values with role `fixture` (#681)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.145.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.145.1)): The documentation no longer calls `navigate … by <parameter>` or form `columns` legacy. Both are current: `by` passes the target screen's key, and `columns` sets field order and labels independently of `layout`'s geometry (#688)
- **AI** ([Release v7.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.38.0)): Harness setup no longer deletes or overwrites files at adapter paths it did not generate; it reports the conflict and only replaces them with `--force` (#630)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.79.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.79.1)): Keyed lookup and filtered live queries whose argument concept has validation rules are rendered again instead of being refused with `STAGE-ESM-010`. The argument is accepted as its underlying primitive and converted inside the query, so a key that breaks the concept's rules finds no instance, matching the Screenplay reference, rather than failing validation. Composite or collection arguments that contain a validated concept are still refused, with a message that names the concept. (#350)
- **AI** ([Release v7.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.37.0)): The Chronicle Kotlin client skill no longer says transactional appends return `Unit`; inside a unit of work they return a placeholder `AppendResult`, and the real results are on the unit of work after commit
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.2)): A projection future is no longer lost when a catch-up or replay write fails. While a projection catches up or replays, a write that resolves a future is now made immediately with a known outcome instead of being buffered, and the future is removed only after that write succeeded. Failed writes are reported as failed partitions. (#4753)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.2)): Failures of buffered MongoDB projection writes during catch-up and replay are reported exactly once, and writes queued behind a failed batch are still written. (#4753)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.2)): A projection future stays registered if storing its resolution fails, instead of disappearing from memory while it is still stored. (#4753)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.1)): Projections that map nested members now apply the naming policy to every segment of the path. Before, only the first segment was camel-cased, so `.Set(m => m.Topic).To(e => e.Brief.Topic)` read `brief.Topic` from an event stored as `brief.topic` and left the property empty. This affected both a running kernel and `ReadModelScenario`. Nested paths in keys, parent keys, joins, children, counters, `FromAll`/`FromEvery` and the model-bound attributes are fixed the same way. The changed projection definition is rebuilt automatically under the default `Automatic` definition evolution; with `PartialOnly` or `Manual`, Chronicle raises a replay recommendation so you can rebuild it (#4493)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.1)): Model-bound attributes accept dotted member paths such as `[SetFrom<Briefed>($"{nameof(Briefed.Brief)}.{nameof(Brief.Topic)}")]` and validate every segment against the type, instead of rejecting them before the projection is built (part of #4493)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.51.1)): An event type migration's `DefaultValue` or `MapValues` for a `ConceptAs<T>` property now writes the plain underlying value, for example `"global"`, instead of `{"Value": "global"}`. Migrated events then deserialize into the concept as expected (#3941)
- **AI** ([Release v7.36.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.36.1)): The Stage rendering, render and gap-fill, UI composition, read-surface and authoring-workflow guidance no longer tell agents that a guarded Close action and a double-click interaction are refused by design. Stage has run both since 4.65; on public CLI 3.53.1 with `cratis/stage:4.73.1` the live browser run offers Close only while the item is open, posts `CloseWorkItem`, hides Close afterwards, and navigates or notifies on a double click depending on the row (Cratis/Screenplay#671). `STAGE-SCENE-ACTION-001` and `STAGE-SCENE-INTERACTION-001` stay documented as the refusal for a guard Stage cannot evaluate. A verification spec now fails if the guidance says otherwise. (part of #536)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.77.0)): The Screenplay generator no longer states a reactor that returns several events as `produces` when Chronicle could refuse the batch differently from Screenplay: Chronicle appends a returned batch as one unit, while a Screenplay reaction appends each fact separately. When a later event can be rejected by a constraint (a unique property, a class-level `[Unique]`, or an `IConstraint` target), an earlier event closes the stream, or a constraint cannot be read, the reactor keeps its `file` reference and `SP0070` explains why. (part of #3093)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.77.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.77.0)): The Screenplay generator reads screen source with a scanner that understands strings, template literals, regular expressions and JSX text and attributes, so `//` inside a value such as `title="https://example.com"` is no longer cut off as a comment and an `import` inside a template literal is no longer read as an import. A screen that renders other components or HTML beside the Cratis Components it reads now reports each part it does not read with `SP0028`, and a screen whose source cannot be scanned reports that once instead of stating a partial structure. (#3095)
- **AI** ([Release v7.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.35.0)): Arc identity guidance: forwarded identity headers are trusted only with `TrustForwardedIdentityHeaders=true`, discovery endpoints need authentication outside Development, and the `.cratis-iden

## Removed

- **Arc** ([Release v22.126.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.126.1)): The MongoDB integration no longer configures a Polly retry strategy that could never fire, because the collection interceptors already handle every error themselves. Retry, cancellation and error behavior are unchanged. The interception layer itself stays until the next major release. (part of #2858)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.88.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.88.2)): `Cratis.Chronicle.Testing` no longer ships the unused internal `InProcessChronicleConnection` and `InProcessServices` types; scenarios keep running on `ChronicleConnectionForTesting` and `TestingServices`, so nothing changes for consumers. (#4929)
- **cli** ([Release v3.73.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.73.0)): The one-time automatic restart from `eventmodel` to `semantic` in `cratis run`, and the `engineFallback` member of its JSON output. A refusal now stops the container and exits with `5` on the engine that was selected. (part of #324)
- **AI** ([Release v7.51.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.51.1)): Version tables, product-source pages and provenance history from these skills. (part of #662)
- **AI** ([Release v7.48.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.48.3)): The toolchain skill's `versions.md`, `doc-contradictions.md` and `provenance.md` references. The documentation contradictions that still hold are tracked as issues on Cratis/Screenplay. (part of #662)

## Security

- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.216.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.216.0)): A destination-intent repair is refused, before the proposal is kept and again before files are installed, if any attachment it relies on overlaps a source, identity, journal, staging or backup path, including through physical aliases or uncertain links. A proposal is also refused at apply if its source, catalog, files on disk or attachments changed after review. (#397)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.121.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.121.0)): A non-public static helper on a read model can no longer be reached over HTTP as a query endpoint with caller-chosen arguments. (#3368)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.119.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.119.0)): Query health no longer exposes per-connection user IDs and client addresses to anonymous callers outside Development. (#2873)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.116.0)): A host `FallbackPolicy`, or Arc Core's requirement that callers authenticate when authentication handlers are registered, can no longer be bypassed by reaching an unannotated query through the observable query hub. (#2952)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.116.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.116.0)): An open hub connection whose session a guard revokes can no longer keep reading non-observable query results. (#2955)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.108.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.108.0)): A command declared with read protection `On` blocks on `Error` and `Unknown` validation results from validators, `Provide()` and `Handle()`, even when the caller sends a more permissive `X-Allowed-Severity`; callers can still accept warnings and information. An explicit `[BlockOnValidationSeverity]` still takes precedence, and `Off` and `Measure` are unchanged: under those modes, declare `[BlockOnValidationSeverity(ValidationResultSeverity.Error)]` on commands whose validators must not be skipped (#3332)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.17.0)): `Cratis.Orleans.Storage.Sql` references `System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs` 10.0.13, replacing the 9.0.11 that Microsoft.Data.SqlClient brought in, which is affected by GHSA-846f-f764-76mx and GHSA-2fmv-rv2c-59x7.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.2)): The message of `ConflictingEnrollmentScope` no longer contains the event source identifier or any scope values. It only names the scope dimensions that differ, so a generated or sensitive identifier is not written to logs or responses that show the message. Read the values from the typed properties when you need them. (#4909)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.65.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.65.1)): The SQL storage provider now references System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs 10.0.13 directly. Microsoft.Data.SqlClient 7.1.1, the latest release, otherwise brings in version 9.0.18, which is affected by GHSA-2fmv-rv2c-59x7 and GHSA-846f-f764-76mx. (#4912)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.95.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.95.2)): .NET 10 packages are updated from 10.0.12 to 10.0.13, and `System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs` and `System.Security.Cryptography.Xml` are pinned at 10.0.13. This fixes advisories GHSA-2fmv-rv2c-59x7, GHSA-846f-f764-76mx, GHSA-c399-9853-6jmj, GHSA-7hqp-7c5m-4vq6 and GHSA-r955-8q5j-3h83. `Microsoft.OpenApi` moves to 2.12.2, which `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` 10.0.13 requires. (#3282)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.64.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.64.2)): Move the .NET packages from 10.0.12 to 10.0.13. This clears the advisories against System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs and System.Security.Cryptography.Xml 10.0.12, which arrive through Microsoft.AspNetCore.DataProtection: GHSA-2fmv-rv2c-59x7, GHSA-846f-f764-76mx, GHSA-7hqp-7c5m-4vq6, GHSA-c399-9853-6jmj and GHSA-r955-8q5j-3h83. (#4897)
- **cli** ([Release v3.59.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.59.1)): The Chronicle workbench's terminal UI library, SharpConsoleUI, moves to 2.7.0, which no longer depends on SixLabors.ImageSharp. The five ImageSharp advisories (GHSA-gwg2-r3hj-4w44, GHSA-j3p4-wp97-rph4, GHSA-j9gm-c75j-xc9q, GHSA-jjfr-hcj7-qf5w, GHSA-wmxv-xphr-5c9g) no longer apply, and their NuGet audit suppressions are removed, so a future advisory in the dependency graph fails the build again. (#277)

## Deprecated

- **Arc** ([Release v22.129.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.129.0)): `GeneratedIdentifierEventsCannotBeSplit` is no longer thrown, because events for a generated source now append in one batch with the rest of the command's events. It is marked obsolete. (#3423)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.148.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.148.0)): The `eventmodel` Host engine is deprecated and will be removed in Stage v6. Selecting it logs a warning once at startup. (part of #23)
- **AI** ([Release v7.74.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.74.0)): `RecordDecisionUsage`, `DecisionUsageRecorded`, `DecisionUsageByDay`, `RecordAIProviderUsageSnapshot` and `AIProviderUsageSnapshotRecorded` are obsolete. They remain so existing event stores still replay, and are removed in the next major release (part of #767).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.113.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.113.0)): `ReadRegionsCannotBeSuspended` is obsolete; Arc no longer throws it (#3242)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.57.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.57.0)): The `sequence` line in a capture's `source events` block. Stored declarations that still contain it keep working and log a deprecation warning; remove the line and rely on the `from` events' origin (#4700)
